## XXXV Return to the Summer Palace / 第三十五章 重返颐和园

===Original===
Sending off the picture to St. Louis did not sever my connection with the Palace, for I had still other work to finish! At the end of April, a month later than usual, the Court moved out to the Summer Palace for the rest of the year. The country was beautiful, the trees were almost in full leaf, and lilacs, blue and white, bloomed everywhere. My garden in the Park of the Palace of the Emperor’s Father was full of them, and over my entrance gate clambered a beautiful yellow rosebush laden with masses of blooms. Wild flowers were springing up at every turn, and my dog Me-lah in his wild races through the park, when we were out for our walks, would often start up coveys of birds; or rabbits would scurry away at his approach. I went back to my favorite haunts in the park, to the summerhouse, where upon the threshold, cut in stone, lay the plaint of the Seventh Prince! It was a delightful change to be in this beautiful spot after the four months in Peking, and to see Nature everywhere budding into perfection. The grounds of the Summer Palace were one maze of delight. The peonies in all their royal splendor, the fragrant lilac, the stately magnolia, and the budding elms, each added their charm to this beautiful spot, where everything was lovely. I could not wonder at the Empress Dowager’s desire to come back again to all this beauty.

A charming studio was fitted up for me at the Summer Palace on our return. Her Majesty saw how much more satisfactory it was for me to have a proper place to work in, where I would be undisturbed, and even had she not seen the utility of a studio, I think she would have granted my request for one, for she was always kind and considerate. Upper windows of plate-glass were put into the north side of one of His Majesty’s Throne-rooms, behind the Imperial loge. It looked over a charming terrace of the garden. The days were long, and it was a delight to live and breathe, and the quiet of the studio, where I could work at leisure, made me resume my work with renewed vigor.

I began at once to finish up the small sketch of the St. Louis portrait, which Her Majesty wished to keep, and then to put the final touches on the two portraits begun at the Summer Palace. The Throne-room that was now my studio had only one disadvantage. It was so near the Theater that on theater days I could hear the music and the voices of the actors. And on those days, the court outside my windows was filled all day with eunuchs and Their Majesties’ attendants, moving to and fro. I decided if it was necessary for me to go into Peking at any time, to take a “Theater day” to do so.

===Chinese===
画像送往圣路易斯之后，我并未就此与宫廷断了往来，因为我还有别的活计要完工！四月底，朝廷比往年迟了一个月，移驻颐和园，预备在此消度这一年余下的光阴。乡间正是美景宜人，树木几乎都已披满新绿，蓝的、白的丁香处处绽放。我在皇帝生父府邸花园里的那片园子，便满是丁香，门楼口还有一丛娇美的黄玫瑰攀援而上，花朵累累。野花处处从地里钻出来，我那条名叫「美拉」（Me-lah）的小狗，每当我们一同出门散步时，总要在园里撒欢地跑，时常惊起一群群的鸟；不然便是野兔见它来了，仓皇地窜开。我重又去了园中那些我最爱流连的所在，去了那座凉亭——亭子的门槛是石头的，上面刻着七王爷的一首题诗！在紫禁城住了四个月之后，来到这样一处美丽的地方，看万物处处抽芽、各臻其美，真是一桩叫人欣然的变化。颐和园的园囿，简直是一座令人目不暇接的乐园。牡丹以其王者般的富丽，丁香以其幽幽的清香，玉兰以其亭亭的仪态，还有那正在抽芽的榆树，各各为这处美丽的地方添了几分风韵，这里无处不惹人怜爱。皇太后一心想回到这一片美景中来，我又何怪之有。

我们回到颐和园之后，园里为我收拾出一间宜人的画室。皇太后看得出，让我有一处不受打扰的、像样的地方作画，要方便得多；便使她不曾看出画室的好处，我想她也会答应我这一桩请求，因为她待人向来是又体贴、又周到的。在皇上的一间宝座殿的北面、御楼的后头，安上了几扇平板玻璃的高窗。窗外正对着花园里一处宜人的露台。日子渐渐长了，活着、呼吸着都是一桩乐事，而画室的清静，又使我可以从容作画，于是我又抖擞精神，重新开工。

我立即着手完成圣路易斯那幅画像的小样——这是皇太后要留下的——随后再为已在颐和园动笔的那两幅画像作最后的收拾。如今充作我画室的这间宝座殿，只有一样不好：离戏台太近，逢着演戏的日子，我听得见里头的丝竹与伶人的唱念。那样的日子，我窗外那片庭院里整日里都是来来往往的太监和两位陛下的随侍。我便拿定主意，日后若需进北京城，便拣一个「戏日」动身。

===Original===
One Theater day I did go into Peking, and on my return to the Summer Palace the next day I found that His Majesty the Emperor had taken advantage of my absence to occupy his Throne-room the day before, for I found his Theater program, distinguishable by being written on Imperial yellow paper, and he had also left a few papers scattered around with characters and phrases written with the “Vermilion Pencil,” which may only be used by His Majesty. On one paper he had evidently been trying to draw a plan of the part of Manchuria where the war operations were then being carried on. He had also drawn a part of the Great Wall of China, and the dividing line between China and Manchuria.

So the Emperor, notwithstanding his stoical smile, his apparent unconcern, was not indifferent to affairs in Manchuria. He was watching the course of events there, and he probably worried and grieved as much as even the Empress Dowager, about what might be the result for China. He had probably schooled himself to appear indifferent. The ceremonies and festivals at the Palace had been going on as usual, but the two central figures of all these functions had their own secret anxieties and cares. The Emperor was following the campaign in Manchuria, and the Empress Dowager was probably planning and thinking of the best course for China to follow.

===Chinese===
有一个戏日，我当真进了北京城。次日回到颐和园，便发觉皇上趁我不在时，昨日已用过他这间宝座殿——因为我发现了他的戏单，那戏单一眼可辨，是写在御用的黄纸上的；他还留下了几张散乱的纸，上头用「朱笔」写着一些字句和词语——朱笔唯有皇上才可使用。其中一张纸上，他显然是在试着画出当时满洲战事所在的的那一处地图，还画了一段中国长城，以及中国与满洲之间的分界线。

可见皇上虽然面带那淡然的微笑、虽是一副不闻不问的神情，对满洲之事却并非漠不关心。他一直在关注那里的局势，对于此事对中国可能酿成的后果，他心中的忧惧与伤痛，恐怕并不在皇太后之下。他大约是刻意练就了这副无动于衷的样子。宫中的典礼和节庆照旧举行，然而这些场合中的两位中心人物，各有各的隐忧和心事。皇上在追踪满洲的战事，皇太后则大约正在筹算着中国该走的上策。

===Original===
In May, the Empress Dowager had another Garden Party for the ladies of the Legation, at which she, as usual, asked me to assist. When I went into the Audience Hall for this reception, a few moments before the ladies were to arrive, Her Majesty, after greeting me and scanning my toilet, which was all in gray without any color, took a pink peony from a vase at hand, and pinned it on my dress, saying I needed a little color. I had just finished the largest of the other three portraits I had painted at the Summer Palace, and Her Majesty told me she liked it so much that she had decided to show it to the foreign ladies at this Garden Party. As I had heard nothing of this plan before leaving my studio that morning, I had made no preparations for it. The picture was on my easel, unframed, and I told her I would prefer it to be placed in its frame, before it was shown. This frame, designed also by the Empress Dowager, and made by the Palace workmen, was a magnificent piece of work, elaborately carved and beautiful in form. It was in the natural color of teakwood, and this quiet tone admirably set off the vivid color of the gown and accessories, and was a great improvement to the picture. When she heard what were my wishes on the subject, Her Majesty said she would see that the picture was placed in the frame, and it was arranged that as soon as I had finished my luncheon, I would return to the studio and overlook things myself, and arrange the portrait as I wished.

The Audience passed off as usual. Immediately after luncheon the ladies were invited to go to the studio to see the portrait. The Empress Dowager had evidently forgotten about my wish to go there first, and as she herself, contrary to all precedent, led the way, followed by the ladies, I could not, of course, precede her. I had not thought that she would make such an innovation as to, herself, accompany the ladies to the studio. I felt greatly honored, but I feared the eunuchs had not arranged things as they should be, and knew I could do nothing with Her Majesty present, and what was my chagrin on reaching the hall in the wake of the Empress Dowager and the ladies, to find that the portrait, though placed in the frame as I had desired, was in the center of the narrow hall, and every window on both sides had been opened to its widest extent, and the light came in from all sides! I had shut off all the lights of this hall, except the double windows to the north, where I had the upper glasses put in, and this is where the picture should have been placed, but as Her Majesty’s Throne always occupies the center of the Throne-rooms, the eunuchs evidently thought that was the proper place for her portrait when on exhibition. As the halls are narrow in proportion to their length, no one could get further off than four feet from this life-size portrait. This, added to the cross-lights, was heartrending. I was in despair. Her Majesty’s presence prevented my ordering the eunuchs to change the position of the portrait, and, besides, everyone had already seen it! The ladies, who could not do otherwise than express their admiration in the presence of both the August Subject and the artist, duly praised the portrait. Her Majesty, who knew how it looked in its proper light, and who only glanced at it here, did not realize at what a disadvantage it appeared, and was perfectly satisfied with the effect.

===Chinese===
五月里，皇太后又为各国公馆的夫人们办了一次游园会，照旧叫我过去相帮。夫人们将至之前片刻，我走进觐见的大殿，皇太后与我见礼之后，把我那一身装束打量了一番——我那日通身灰色，不见一点颜色——便顺手从近处一只瓶中取了一朵粉红的牡丹，替我别在衣上，说我总该添一点颜色才好。我在颐和园所绘的另外三幅画像中，最大的那一幅刚刚完工，皇太后告诉我，她爱极了这幅画像，已决意要在这次游园会上让外国夫人们看一看。我那天早晨离开画室之前，并不曾听说这一打算，是以毫无准备。那幅画还在画架上，未曾装框，我便对她说，最好还是先装进画框再行展示。这画框也是皇太后亲自画的样式，由宫中的工匠所制，乃是一件华美的杰作，雕工繁复，造型端丽。框子是柚木的本色，这一沉静的色调恰好衬出袍褂和配饰那鲜明的颜色，使画像增色不少。太后听了我的意思，便说她自会吩咐人将画装进框里，并约定：我一用完午餐，便先回画室亲自照看，按我的心意把画像安放好。

觐见一如往常。午餐一毕，夫人们便被请去画室看画像。皇太后显然把我先去那儿的心意忘了，而她竟亲自领路——这是破了所有的先例——身后跟着一群夫人，我当然不能抢在她前头。我原想不到她竟会有这样的创举，亲自陪夫人们去画室。我自觉荣幸之至，可又怕太监们未曾把物件安排妥当，明知太后在场我亦无可奈何；待我随着皇太后和夫人们走到那间大殿时，叫我何等懊恼——画像虽如我所愿装进了框里，却被安放在这狭长大殿的正中，两边的窗子每一扇都开到了最宽，光线从四面八方涌进来！我原本把这大殿所有的光都遮断了，只留着北面那几扇双层窗——也就是我新装上高窗的地方——画像本该安放在此；然而因为皇太后的宝座一向都设在宝座殿的正中，太监们显然以为，她的画像在展示时，自然也该放在那个位置。这些大殿狭而长，任谁也退不过离这幅与真人等大的画像四英尺之外。这一点，再加上那交叉错乱的光线，真叫人痛心。我一时绝望极了。太后在场，我无法吩咐太监去挪动画像的位置；况且，人人都已经看过了！夫人们在至尊的描画对象与画师跟前，除了一力称赏之外也别无他法，便都依礼将那画像夸赞了一番。皇太后知道这幅画在它应有的光线下是何模样，而她在此处不过是瞥了一眼，并未察觉它此刻显得何等吃亏，竟对这效果十分满意。

===Original===
The Portrait of the Empress Dowager in Its Frame

This Frame is Made of Camphor-wood Carved in the Palace after the Empress’s own Designs and under Her Direction

An amusing little incident took place while the ladies were looking at it. The Empress Dowager, in her cursory examination in this light, noticed a part of the trimming of the gown where the design was not well worked out. She came up to me, as I stood in a group of ladies, and pointed out the defect. She took my hand in hers, and said in an almost pleading way, “There is a bit of trimming that is not well finished. You will arrange it for me, will you not, Ker-Gunia?” She did not believe in leaving anything to the imagination, and wished every detail fully worked out!

This portrait was very successfully photographed, and Her Majesty concluded she liked it much better than the one which had been sent to St. Louis. She said it would make me “famous.” But when I thought of how I might have painted this wonderfully interesting woman in the unique setting in which she was placed, I realized that “it might have been” are really the “saddest words of tongue or pen.”

===Chinese===
镶框中的皇太后画像

此框以樟木雕就，在宫中按皇太后亲拟的图样、在其亲自督导下制成

夫人们正在看画的时候，发生了一桩有趣的小小插曲。皇太后在那样的光线下草草一顾，便注意到袍褂上有一处花边，纹样做得不够圆满。她走到我身边——其时我正站在一群夫人中间——将那一处瑕疵指给我看。她握住我的手，几乎是带着央求的语气说道：「有一处花边还没收尾呢。你会替我把它弄好的，是不是，柯姑娘（Ker-Gunia）？」她最不肯把任何东西留给想象，每一处细节都务必要做到圆满才罢！

这幅画像拍照十分成功，皇太后断定她比送去圣路易斯的那一幅还要喜欢得多。她说这幅画会令我「名扬天下」。然而一想到我本可以怎样去描画这位极其有趣的女子、怎样在她那绝无仅有的环境之中去描绘她，我便深深感到：「本可以」三字，实是「口中笔下最伤人的字眼」。

===Original===
The precedent having been established, the idea of a representation of the Sacred Person of a Chinese Majesty being seen by the world having been accepted, the painting of Her Majesty’s first portrait not having been followed by the dire results that the Chinese had prophesied, the traditional prejudice was overcome, and when she saw how quickly the photograph was made of the portrait, and how satisfactory it was, she decided she would have the photographer try one of herself, and she was not one to stop at a single trial. After waiting sixty-eight years to see a counterfeit presentment of herself, I know she will now indulge this new fantasy of hers to its fullest extent, and perhaps some other artist may at some time paint her according to western ideas, and represent her attractive personality in its best setting. But there must always be a pioneer, and he it is who suffers the hardships and makes the way clear for others, which must be my solace and consolation for not being able to paint her as I should have liked. The Empress Dowager “consented” to have a portrait of herself painted. Before I finished the first one she told me she wanted “many,” and suggested my passing the rest of my life out in Peking. I painted four. Who will do the others?

I felt I could not go on forever painting portraits, according to Chinese traditions, of the Empress Dowager. I could not spend my life in this dalliance with Oriental splendor. The world beyond the Palace gates called me. I hurried to finish my task. The last portrait was nearing completion. My sojourn at the Palace was drawing to a close. Though I longed to be where I might paint in a freer way, I looked forward with real regret to leaving the Palace, and especially to leaving the Empress Dowager and the young Empress, for I had come to really love them. I found Her Majesty by far the most fascinating personality it had ever been my good fortune to study at such close range. The young Empress was a sweet, kind nature, full of dignity and pathos, for whom I prayed there might be greater happiness in store than had yet fallen to her lot. My sojourn at the Palaces of Her Imperial Majesty the Empress Dowager of China, my association with herself and the Ladies of her Court, I shall always remember as one of the most charming experiences of my life.

===Chinese===
先例既开，中国一位陛下的「圣容」可为世人得见一事既已为人所接受，皇太后的第一幅画像绘成之后，中国人所预言的种种凶险后果又并未应验，那由来已久的成见便被打破了；而当皇太后看到画像的照片是何等迅速地拍成、效果又是何等叫人满意时，她便决意让摄影师为她本人也试一试——而她原不是浅尝辄止的人。等了六十八年才头一回见到自己的画像，我深知她此后必将这一桩新的雅兴起劲地发挥到极致；或许日后的某一天，另有一位画师能依西法为她造像，把她那动人的风采，置于最相宜的境地之中去描摹。然而凡事总要有一个开路的人，正是这人承受了艰辛，为后人辟出一条坦途——这便只能是我未能如己所愿地描绘她时，所能得到的安慰与宽解了。皇太后当初不过是「应允」让人为自己画一幅像。我尚未画完第一幅时，她便告诉我说，她要「许多幅」，并提议我就在北京度过余生。我画了四幅。余下的，将由谁人来画呢？

我觉得，我不能一辈子照着中国的规矩，为皇太后画下去。我不能把此生都耗费在与这东方的锦绣繁华的这段旖旎之中。宫门之外的那个世界，正在召唤我。我加紧完工手头的活计。最后一幅画像已近完工。我在宫中的日子，也将走到尽头。我虽渴望去到一个能以更自由的手笔作画的地方，可一旦想到要离开宫廷，尤其是要离开皇太后和皇后，我心中仍生出真切的怅惘——因为我已经真心地喜爱上她们了。我觉得，皇太后是我有幸能在如此近处细细考察过的、最最迷人的一个人物。皇后性情温婉仁厚，端庄之中带着几分哀愁，我常常为她祈愿，盼她日后能得着比从前所遭际的更大的福分。在中国皇太后陛下各处宫禁中的这一段羁留，与她本人及宫中诸位夫人的这一段交往，我必将永志不忘，视作我平生最迷人的一段经历之一。

## XXI A European Circus at the Palace / 二十一 宫中一场西洋马戏

===Original===
Chinese ceremonies and celebrations were not all I was destined to enjoy while at the Summer Palace. There began to be talk of some "foreign entertainment" soon to be given, and when I found this foreign entertainment was to be a circus, a real European circus, I was delighted. I had been out in China two years, and had not had much European entertainment during that time, and—shall I confess it?—I dearly loved a circus if the horses and animals were fine. This circus was then in Tientsin, and someone had suggested to Their Majesties it would be an interesting thing to see. A young Manchu was sent to Tientsin to investigate. When he returned, flaming posters were submitted to Their Majesties by the Chief Eunuchs. When the Empress Dowager saw the vulgarly colored picture of a summarily clad young woman of the show, I was watching her face and I saw a look of contemptuous scorn pass over it. She brightened up, however, when she saw the pictures of the animals at their tricks, and the men on horses, and it was decided that the circus should be brought up from Tientsin! The animals and performers were to be domiciled in one of the parks near by, but the tent was to be stretched within the enclosure of the Palace.

Sites for the ring were discussed, and it was finally decided to have the tents pitched at the extreme western end of the lake. There was a large open field here, planted in turnips! As the turnips were ready to be gathered, it was decided that the crop should be pulled up and this place prepared for the tents.

===Chinese===
在颐和园（Summer Palace）小住期间，我命中注定要领略的，远不止中国的种种典礼与庆贺。不久便有了风声，说是要办一场什么「西洋玩意儿」；待我得知这西洋玩意儿竟是一场马戏——一场地地道道的西洋马戏——我真是喜不自胜。我来中国已满两年，这段时日里西洋的消遣见得不多，而——容我招认么？——只要马匹与兽类出色，我是顶爱看马戏的。这马戏班其时正在天津（Tientsin），有人便向两位陛下进言，说看一看倒也有趣。宫里便派了一位年轻的满族（Manchu）官员前去天津察看。他回来后，几位大太监便将那些花花绿绿的海报呈给两位陛下御览。皇太后瞧见海报上那个衣着简陋、颜色俗艳的女演员像，我正留神端详她的面容，只见一丝鄙夷不屑的神色从她脸上掠过。然而待她看见兽类做把戏、汉子骑马的图画，神色便为之一展，于是便定下：将这马戏班从天津接进京来！兽类与演戏的人就近安顿在附近一处园子里，而帐篷则要搭在宫禁之内。

场地的位置议来议去，末了定下：将帐篷搭在湖最西头的那一端。那里有一大片开阔地，地里正种着萝卜！萝卜既已长成、到了收成的时候，便议定将这一茬萝卜尽数拔起，腾出地方来搭帐篷。

===Original===
One day we went out into the turnip field, and the Empress Dowager herself pulled the first turnip; then the Empress and all the Princesses pulled some, and when they found a curiously shaped one, it was given to Her Majesty. It was a strange sight to see the Great Empress Dowager, sitting there at the side of the field, on her yellow campstool, smiling and interested, with the turnips piled around her, and the gaily dressed Empress and Princesses in their silken gowns flitting in and out of the field, apparently enjoying, to its utmost, the simple task of pulling these prosaic vegetables. The eunuchs and attendants stood in crowds around to take the turnips when pulled. They were not allowed, however, to pull any themselves. When a small square was denuded, Her Majesty and the Ladies returned to the Palace, and an army of workmen came and pulled up the whole field and began to prepare the ground for the circus tents.

As the performance of the circus was to be on the first day of the month, the Imperial players were at the Theater. When the morning Audience was finished, Their Majesties and the Empress and Ladies went to the Theater and listened to two or three plays. After luncheon, taken in the Imperial loge, Their Majesties started for the landing-place, followed by the young Empress and Ladies. The lake was gay with beautiful barges, great houseboats, and numbers of flat boats for the eunuchs. The barges and houseboats were picturesquely decorated with flying banners, pennants, and tasseled wands. Two steam-launches, puffing away, gave an air of modernity to this most Oriental fleet. One of the steam-launches was splendidly decorated with yellow banners, with gorgeous yellow silk scarfs festooned around the cabin and the Imperial flag flying above it. The Empress Dowager and the Emperor descended the marble steps to this gaily decked launch, and started off alone for the other end of the lake, the Imperial banners and colors flying.

===Chinese===
一日我们到那萝卜地里去，皇太后亲手拔下头一棵萝卜；随后皇后与众位福晋格格也都动手去拔，谁若拔得一棵形状稀奇的，便呈给太后。这光景着实奇特：堂堂大清皇太后，坐在地头那张黄色折凳上，面带笑意、兴致勃勃，身边堆满萝卜；衣饰鲜丽的皇后与众位福晋格格身着绸缎袍褂，在田间穿来穿去——拔这种平淡无奇的蔬菜这样一桩简简单单的活计，她们似乎都乐在其中，享到极致。太监与侍从们则围成一群一群地站着，萝卜一拔出来，便上前接过去。只是不许他们自己动手拔。待一小方地的萝卜都拔尽了，太后与诸位宫眷便回宫去了，一队工匠便来将整片地都拔尽，开始平整土地，预备搭马戏班的帐篷。

马戏演期定在初一，宫里的伶人其时正在戏台上。早间的觐见散后，两位陛下携皇后与众位宫眷到戏台去，听了两三出戏。在御用看阁里用了午膳之后，两位陛下便动身往湖边的船坞去，皇后与众位宫眷随行。湖上十分热闹：华美的画舫、宽敞的游船，还有许多供太监们乘坐的平底船。画舫与游船上装饰得如画一般，旌旗飞扬，幡带飘舞，还有那缀着流苏的长竿。两艘小火轮，噗噗地冒着烟，给这支东方气息十足的船队添了几分时新气象。其中一艘小轮船装饰得金碧辉煌，挂满黄色幡旗，船舱四周悬着华美的黄绸帷，桅顶上飘着龙旗。皇太后与皇上沿着石级下到这艘装饰一新的小轮船上，独自先行往湖的那一端驶去，船上御用旗帜飞扬。

===Original===
The Empress and Princesses went in the Empress's State boat; the visiting ladies followed in another of these ponderous but picturesque affairs. In size they are as large as an ordinary Chinese pavilion. The Empress's cabin was carpeted and splendidly upholstered in cloth of gold, with the usual tea-tables and lounges. It had one of those gallery-like prows with silken awnings, where the Princesses stood. The young Empress sat within, on one of the gold-covered couches. As she had been brought up with several of the Princesses as playmates, the young Empress generally waived ceremony with them; but she knew how, when necessary, to maintain a sweet dignity that was charming and perfectly in accord with her exalted position. Today was a State occasion. She sat alone, and the Ladies remained outside on the prow. She asked me to come in and showed me the interior and some of the curiously inlaid tables. She knew I was interested in all these things. She made me sit at her side, and when I demurred she said she knew it was not the foreign custom to sit on cushions on the floor, as was the habit of the Ladies when in her presence, and that I must sit beside her. This was the consideration they always showed me at the Palace, which I fully realized was not due to any special liking for me, but simply to their exquisite breeding—their desire to make me feel comfortable and at home.

When we arrived at the other side of the lake, the Empress and Ladies stood while Their Majesties landed. They were welcomed by a great burst of music from the bands. A number of Princes and Officials stood waiting to receive them and conduct them to the handsome loges that had been prepared for them.

===Chinese===
皇后与众位福晋格格上了皇后的仪船；来做客的女眷们则登上另一艘这样笨重却别致的大船。这种船的体量，足有普通中国亭子那般大。皇后的船舱里铺着地毯，椅榻都用金线锦缎装点得金碧辉煌，照例设有茶几与卧榻。船头有一处廊台一般的所在，张着绸篷，福晋格格们便站在那里。皇后却坐在舱里一张蒙着金线锦缎的榻上。因皇后自幼与好几位福晋格格一同长大，是儿时的玩伴，是以她寻常并不与她们拘礼；然而必要之时，她也懂得如何端出一份和悦而端庄的气度来——这气度颇为动人，又与她崇高的位分十分相称。这一日的场面，乃是仪礼之典。她独自坐着，宫眷们都留在舱外的船头。她唤我进去，给我看舱中的陈设，还有几张镶嵌得十分精巧的桌子。她知我对这些物事都留心。她要我坐在她身旁，我逊谢之时，她便说，她知道按洋人规矩，并不像宫眷们到了她跟前那般，习惯坐在地下的坐垫上，是以我定要坐在她身边才是。这便是我在宫中向来所蒙的体恤；我心里十分明白，这并非由于对我有什么特别的厚爱，只不过是出于他们那份深厚的教养——出于他们要我感到舒适自在的那份心意。

我们一行到了湖的对岸，皇后与众位宫眷都站起身来，等候两位陛下登岸。两班乐队的乐声响成一片，迎候着他们。一班王公大臣立在那里候着，迎上来将两位陛下引到为他们预备的华美看阁里。

===Original===
It was a picturesque procession that started from the landing-place—the Empress Dowager and the Emperor, under the big, embroidered, yellow silk, State umbrellas, preceded and surrounded by gorgeously attired attendants and splendidly gowned officials, the young Empress and Ladies, in gala attire, following after, with their eunuchs and attendants. The day was perfect, and glorious sunshine added to the brilliant effect. The side of the tent toward the Imperial loges was open. There was a railed platform before the pavilions that had been erected as loges. These pavilions were luxuriously fitted up: Their Majesties' loges were hung with the Imperial yellow. A yellow satin chair (with a smaller one at its left) was placed in the center of the raised platform, under the silken awning, and Their Majesties could sit here or within as they chose. The Empress and Ladies stood in groups on either side of this platform.

About two hundred officials had been invited to see the circus, and, contrary to the usual custom, there was no screen between them and the Imperial party. On the right were two bands of foreign music, or rather of Chinese musicians who played foreign music on European instruments. These were the bands of Yuan-Shih-Kai, Viceroy of Tientsin, and of Sir Robert Hart, the Inspector-General of Imperial Customs. Sir Robert's band was formed about eighteen years since, when, as music is his hobby, he decided to try to have some Chinese taught European music on European instruments. He has now a well-equipped band of twenty trained Chinese musicians under a competent European conductor. They play on both brass and stringed instruments. His efforts have been so successful that his example has lately been followed by several high Chinese Officials, first among whom was Yuan-Shih-Kai. The latter's band is military, with fifty musicians, who play only on brass instruments. The two bands played alternately during the intervals of the performance.

===Chinese===
从船坞起步的仪仗，实在是一幅如画的景象：皇太后与皇上撑着那绣花大黄绸御用伞盖，前后左右簇拥着服饰华美的侍从与袍褂鲜明的官员；皇后与众位宫眷盛装相随，身后跟着她们的太监与随从。这一日天色绝好，灿烂的日光更添了几分辉煌气象。帐篷朝向御用看阁的那一面敞开着。充作看阁的几座亭子前，搭着一处带栏杆的平台。这几座亭阁里陈设奢靡：两位陛下的看阁，悬的是御用明黄帷幔。平台正中、绸篷之下，设着一张黄缎宝座（左侧另有一张小些的），两位陛下或坐此处，或入阁内，悉听尊便。皇后与众位宫眷三五成群，立于平台两侧。

受邀来观马戏的官员约二百人；与寻常惯例不同，此次他们与御前之间并不设帷幔。右首是两支西乐乐队，更确切地说，是两班中国乐工，操着西洋乐器奏西洋乐曲。一支是直隶总督袁世凯（Yuan-Shih-Kai）的乐队，一支是大清海关税务司罗伯特·赫德爵士（Sir Robert Hart）的乐队。赫德爵士这一班乐队，约莫十八年前便已组建——音乐乃是他素所爱好，那时他便决意试试看，教些中国人学西洋乐器、奏西洋乐曲。如今他已有了一支编制齐全的乐队，二十名受过训练的中国乐工，由一位称职的西洋指挥统率。铜管、弦乐，一应俱能。他这一番用心成效斐然，近来几位中国大员也起而效法，头一个便是袁世凯。袁世凯那支乐队是军乐队，五十名乐工，单奏铜管乐。演出的间隙，两班乐工便轮流奏乐。

===Original===
For the first time during my residence at the Palace, I now had an opportunity of seeing the Imperial Princes and many of the great nobles and officials. Though they were often asked to the Theater at the Palace, the screen between them and the Imperial loge was never removed, except at the end of the performance, when they bowed their thanks and when the Ladies retired to their own loge. The gentlemen, however, could be well seen at the circus; and though the Chinese Ladies did not glance in their direction, I took advantage of being a foreigner, and when I was behind the others, and could do so without being seen, I closely scanned their faces and attire. Several of the Princes of the Imperial Family came up to the platform where Their Majesties sat and made their bows to them, afterward slightly saluting their relations among the Ladies and Princesses.

Among these young Princes at the circus was a son of Prince Kung and an adopted son of the Imperial Princess. This young man not only had a remarkably fine figure, tall and slender, with broad shoulders, but his face was very handsome. His bow, on coming up to pay his respects to Their Majesties, was as graceful as that of a young chevalier. His regard was so ingenuous, his expression so clever and withal so modest, his whole demeanor so gracious, I was much struck with him. His dress was elegant, and his jewels chosen with discretion. There was none of that overloading of belt ornamentation that the young dandies of the Imperial set were then affecting. His father was one of the great Princes of China, and if this young man develops and carries out the promise of his youth (he was then only seventeen), I fancy he will be heard of also. Like most of the young Manchu Princes, he held a position in the Imperial household, such as Master of the Horse or Captain of the Archers.

===Chinese===
自从我入宫居住以来，这是头一遭有机会得见宗室王公，以及许多显贵大臣。王公们虽也常被请入宫中听戏，然而他们与御用看阁之间所张的帷幔，除却散戏之时——其时他们要行礼谢恩、女眷们也要退回自家的看阁——是从不撤去的。然而看马戏之时，这些男宾却可以看得清清楚楚；中国的夫人们虽不朝他们那边瞥一眼，我却借了自己是外国人的便宜，待到落在人后、不至于被人瞧见之时，便细细打量他们的容貌衣着。宗室里有几位王公走到两位陛下所坐的平台前行礼，礼毕又微微颔首，向人丛里与自己有亲的宫眷和福晋格格致意。

这一日到场的小王公当中，有恭亲王（Prince Kung）的一位公子，还有一位大公主（the Imperial Princess）的养子。这位青年不但身段极好——修长匀称，肩背宽阔——容貌也甚是俊美。他走上前去向两位陛下行礼，那姿态之潇洒，犹如一位年轻的骑士。他目光率真，神情聪慧而又谦逊有加，举手投足温文可亲，我对他颇为留意，留下深刻印象。他的服饰雅致，所佩珠宝也挑选得极有分寸。那时宫中一干时髦少年所好似的、腰带上琳琅满目堆叠的饰物，他身上全无此弊。他的父亲乃是中国数一数二的大王公，这位青年若能如其少年时这份气象那般发展、有所作为（其时他不过十七岁），我想日后人们也定会听到他的名字。与大多数年轻的满族王公一样，他在内廷当差，担任的职位或是御马总管，或是弓箭侍卫统领之类。

===Original===
It was not much of a circus, but none of the Imperial party had ever seen one before, and the setting was so gorgeous, it was unique as a circus performance even to me. The Empress Dowager and the Emperor had splendidly jeweled opera glasses, which a eunuch held ready for their use. The Emperor, disliking to be looked at, held his own glasses before his face most of the time. It seemed to me he used them principally for the purpose of screening himself. The animals pleased both Their Majesties; but aside from the dwarfs, of which there were two, the rest of the performers seemed to have but a mediocre interest for them. Her Majesty was particularly interested in the dogs and trained animals, and His Majesty in the horses and fancy riding. I was standing near him, and he looked keenly at me several times to see how the performance struck me; and one of his head eunuchs asked me in English—the Emperor would not try it—whether I thought it was "good or bad." Their Majesties sat through the performance, the Empress Dowager only retiring to her loge once during the time, which was while one of the summarily clad young ladies was gyrating on a trapeze. There was a magnificent tigress which the circus master had trained, and which was his pièce de résistance. The Empress Dowager would not allow this to be taken out of its cage, and though it was brought out in front of the Imperial platform, it was too catlike to interest her. She has a great antipathy to anything feline. When the performance was finished, the Imperial party left in the same state in which it had arrived, Their Majesties accompanied to the launch by the Princes and high Officials, the music of the two bands playing simultaneously. The Ladies of the Palace and Their Majesties, themselves, have so little novelty in their lives, I think, on the whole, the innovation of the circus was generally appreciated.

===Chinese===
这马戏班子算不得怎样出色，只是御前上下从前从未见过马戏，而此间的排场又如此华美，便是我，也觉得这场马戏别开生面、独一无二。皇太后与皇上都有镶珠嵌宝的观剧镜，由一名太监捧着，随时听候取用。皇上不愿被人注视，大半时间都将观剧镜举在面前。我瞧他那模样，戴这观剧镜，多半是用来遮掩面目的。兽类倒博得两位陛下同声叫好；只是除了那两名侏儒而外，其余的演员似乎都引不起他们多大兴致。皇太后于狗与受过训练的兽类兴致最浓，皇上则爱看马匹与花样骑术。我正站在皇上近旁，他几次三番凝神望着我，要看这演出在我眼中如何；他身边一位首领太监便用英语问我——皇上自己却不肯开口——我以为这演出「好还是不好」。两位陛下从开场坐到散场，整场之中，皇太后仅退入看阁一次——那正是一位衣着简陋的女演员在空中秋千上盘旋之时。还有一头雄壮的母虎，是马戏班主亲手驯出来的，乃是他压轴的看家本领。皇太后不许将它放出笼来，那虎虽被抬到御前平台外面，到底太像猫了，引不起太后的兴致。太后于一切猫科之物，都抱有一种极深的反感。演出既毕，御前一行人便依着来时那般仪仗离去，王公大臣们一直送到小轮船边，两班乐工同时奏起乐来。宫眷们与两位陛下，平生所历新奇之事如此之少，是以总而言之，这场马戏之举，倒也算是皆大欢喜的一桩新鲜事。

## XIX A Garden Party at the Summer Palace / 第十九章 颐和园的游园会

===Original===
Not long after this, Her Majesty gave a garden party for the ladies and gentlemen of the Legations. These garden parties occupy two days, for ladies and gentlemen are not received at the same time by Their Majesties of China. The Corps Diplomatique and attachés were entertained the first day, and the ladies of the Legations the following day. The entertainment was the same for each. The gentlemen were formally received in the Great Audience Hall by Their Majesties, after which a repast was served them in a pavilion near. When this was finished, they were taken for a tour of the gardens and lakes, and they left the Palace about two o’clock. None of the Ladies of the Court, except, of course, the Empress Dowager, were present at the receptions of the gentlemen of the Corps Diplomatique. The ladies of the Legations were received the following day.

===Chinese===
此后不久，皇太后为各国公使馆的夫人们和先生们举办了一场游园会。这种游园会要占去两天时间，因为中国的皇上与皇太后从不同时接见男女宾客。第一天招待的是外交使节团及其随员，第二天则招待各公使馆的夫人。两日的款待内容相同。先生们先在正大光明殿由皇上与皇太后正式接见，随后在附近的一座亭阁内赐宴。宴毕，由人引着游览园中的亭台湖山，约莫午后两点便离宫。在招待外交使节团先生们的场合上，宫中的命妇们一概不出席，自然，皇太后是例外。各公使馆的夫人们则在次日被接见。

===Original===
I was rather embarrassed as to what I should do, at this first formal reception, for the ladies of the Legations, since my arrival in the Palace. Being a foreigner, I thought it looked incongruous for me to receive with the Chinese Ladies. My uneasiness seemed to be divined by Her Majesty (she was always wonderful for her tact); she said, as I had been presented first in private Audience, it would be well for me to be presented also in public Audience. She suggested that I should go to the Foreign Office, meet Mrs. Conger on her arrival, and come into the Throne-room with her. When the eunuchs announced that the ladies had arrived at the Foreign Office, Her Majesty ordered my red Palace chair to take me there.

===Chinese===
自从入宫以来，在这次为公使馆夫人举办的首次正式觐见上，我颇为尴尬，不知自己应当如何行事。身为外国人，我觉着自己与中方命妇一同接宾，未免有些不相宜。皇太后似乎看出了我的局促（她向来以分寸得宜著称），说道：既然我此前已先行私下入觐，那么此番也当于大庭广众之下被正式引见。她提议我到外务部去，待康格夫人抵达时迎候她，与她一同入宝座殿。待太监们通报夫人一行已抵外务部，皇太后便吩咐抬我那顶红色的宫轿，送我前去。

===Original===
The Foreign Office is only a few hundred yards to the left of the Imperial entrance to the Palace. Mrs. Conger was one of the first ladies to arrive. When the other ladies came, all walked over to the gate of the Palace, and, after entering, went to a pavilion at the right of the Audience Hall, where they arranged themselves in the order in which they were to be presented.

===Chinese===
外务部距宫中御道入口的左侧不过几百码之遥。康格夫人是最早抵达的夫人之一。待其他夫人陆续到齐，众人便步行至宫门，入宫之后，先到觐见殿右侧的一座亭阁中，按引见的次序排定位置。

===Original===
The verandah and large marble platform of approach to the Audience Hall were shaded with tent-like silken awnings and covered, for the day, with red carpets, the latter a concession to foreign taste; for Her Majesty, though having many beautiful carpets stored up, has none in use, and only in winter and for certain functions are the courts carpeted. She never uses them in the interior.

===Chinese===
通往觐见殿的游廊和那方宽阔的大理石月台，都用帐幔般的丝绸天篷遮起，又在当日铺上了红地毯——此举乃是为了迁就外宾的口味；因皇太后虽然库中收着许多精美的地毯，平日却一概不用，唯在冬日或某些特定典礼时，庭院中方才铺毯。殿内则从不铺设。

===Original===
A double line of Princesses, led by the Princess Imperial, descended the steps of the Audience Hall and met the ladies on the marble platform. The Princesses then turned and preceded them into the Audience Hall. Here they separated and stood in a picturesque group on either side of the Throne dais.

===Chinese===
两列王妃格格，由大公主领头，自觐见殿的台阶上拾级而下，到那大理石月台上迎接夫人们。王妃格格们随即转身，在前引导众人入殿。进殿之后，她们分立两旁，在宝座阶陛的两侧各成一组，如画般侍立。

===Original===
Here, in the dim obscurity, sat the Empress Dowager on the Dynastic Throne, with the Emperor seated at her left. In front of Her Majesty stood the official table, with its cover of Imperial yellow reaching to the floor. To the ladies standing below the dais only the heads and shoulders of the Empress Dowager were visible above the table, with its pyramids of fruits and flowers.

===Chinese===
就在这幽暗的光影之中，皇太后端坐于御座之上，皇上则坐于其左侧。皇太后面前摆着御案，那明黄色的案帷直垂至地。立于阶下的夫人们望去，御案上堆叠如塔的果品花卉之上，只能看见皇太后的头部与双肩。

===Original===
The ladies made three reverences on entering, and each advanced and went up on the dais at her presentation. Her Majesty’s interpreter, the elder Miss Yu, stood at her right, a little behind, and repeated the Chinese name and title of each lady presented. Her Majesty, who has a royal memory for faces, recognized each lady who had been presented before, but treated all with equal cordiality. This cordiality was sometimes construed by the ladies, on their first presentation, as a special mark of interest in themselves; but it was the Empress Dowager’s invariable position toward all the foreigners at these diplomatic receptions. Like all well-bred hostesses, she was most particular to show no difference even to those ladies she liked best.

===Chinese===
夫人们入内时三行礼，引见时则依次上前，登临阶陛。皇太后的通译——年长的裕小姐——立于其右侧稍后处，将每一位被引见的夫人的中文姓名及称谓逐一陈奏。皇太后记人的本事绝佳，凡曾引见过的夫人无不认得，然对待众人同样殷勤。这种殷勤有时会被初次引见的夫人误作是对自己格外垂青的表示；但此乃皇太后在外交宴会上待一切外国人的不变之态。正如一切教养良好的女主人，她最是讲究一视同仁，即便是她最钟意的夫人，也绝不稍有差等。

===Original===
When all had been presented, the eunuchs removed the official table behind which the Empress Dowager received the formal presentations, and she descended from the dais. One of her yellow satin chairs was brought and she sat down at the right side of the Audience Hall. The ladies were then, collectively, presented by Her Majesty to the young Empress and the Princess Imperial, and tea was ordered. While the ladies were drinking tea, standing around the Empress Dowager’s chair, she said a few words to each, informally.

===Chinese===
待全部引见完毕，太监们便撤去皇太后接受正式引见时所凭的那张御案，皇太后遂从阶陛上下来。一顶明黄缎的轿椅抬了过来，她在觐见殿的右侧落座。随后，皇太后将夫人们一并引见给皇后与大公主，并吩咐赐茶。夫人们围着皇太后的轿椅立着饮茶之际，皇太后不拘礼数地与每人闲谈了几句。

===Original===
When the tea was finished, the ladies, conducted by the eunuchs and accompanied by the Princesses, went through the court of the Theater, past the Palace of the young Empress, through Her Majesty’s court to her Throne-room, where luncheon was served. This was in alternate courses of foreign and Chinese food. There were foreign wines and table waters, as well as Chinese, and quantities of sweet champagne, without which, the Chinese imagine, no foreigner can eat.

===Chinese===
茶毕，夫人们在太监引导及王妃格格们陪同下，穿过戏楼庭院，经过皇后的寝宫，再过皇太后的庭院，来到她的宝座殿中，于此赐宴。席间中西菜肴交替上桌，洋酒、矿泉水与中土饮品一并俱备，更有大量的甜香槟——中国人以为，无此物则洋人便不能下咽。

===Original===
After luncheon, at which the Imperial Princess and Princesses acted as hostesses, the visiting ladies went to the marble terrace overlooking the lake. Here they were met by the young Empress and the secondary wife of the Emperor, for they were never present at the table when the foreign ladies were entertained, any more than Her Majesty herself.

===Chinese===
宴毕——席间由大公主与诸王妃格格充作主人——来宾夫人们便移步至俯瞰昆明湖的大理石台榭。在此迎候她们的是皇后与皇上的侧室，因为款待外国夫人时，她们与皇太后本人一样，是从不入席的。

===Original===
The Empress Dowager’s barge did not lead the Palace fleet that day. There were three big houseboats, each of which ponderous affairs had a large cabin with a yellow-covered seat for Her Majesty, which, though she never used, was never occupied by anyone else. Anything covered with yellow is sacred to Their Majesties, and is never used except by them.

===Chinese===
那一日，皇太后的御舟并未领航宫中的船队。共有三艘大型楼船，每一艘这等笨重的舟船上都设有一座宽敞的舱房，其中备有一张铺着明黄帷垫的座位，是专为皇太后而设的——她虽从不使用，却也无人敢擅坐。凡覆以明黄之物，皆为皇上与皇太后所独尊，除他们之外，任何人不得使用。

===Original===
The Secondary Wife of the Emperor

In Summer Coiffure

===Chinese===
皇上的侧室

夏日发髻

===Original===
We were rowed across the lake, first to the island, where the Palace and small temple adjacent were visited, after which the ladies took the boats again and continued the tour of the lake to the Marble Boat. This Marble Boat was built over the lake as a summerhouse for one of the Emperors, and is on the plan of the Palace houseboat, but with an upper, as well as lower deck. It is one of the things in the Summer Palace most talked about by foreigners, and it is a curiosity, though not a thing of beauty. It was never made the objective point of any of Her Majesty’s promenades, nor visited, except when foreigners were invited to the Palace. On the lower deck of the Marble Boat, where was the best view of the lake, light refreshments, sweets, and fruits were served. When the tour of the lake was finished, the ladies made their adieus to Their Majesties and the young Empress and Princesses, and left the Palace grounds for the Foreign Office, where they took their own chairs and carriages for Peking.

===Chinese===
众人荡舟渡湖，先至湖中岛，游历了岛上的宫殿与邻近的小庙；随后夫人们复又登舟，续游湖景，直至石舫。这座石舫建于湖上，原是某位皇上消夏的别业，其式样仿照宫中的楼船，却有上下两层甲板。这是外国人在颐和园中谈论最多的景物之一，也算得上一桩奇观，却并不算美。皇太后平时散步，从不以此为目的地，也从不游览此处，唯有邀请外国人入宫时方才一至。在石舫下层甲板——此处观湖景最佳——供奉了些许茶点、糖果与时鲜果品。湖上游历既毕，夫人们便向皇上、皇太后、皇后及诸王妃格格辞行，离宫返回外务部，在那里各乘自家的轿子和马车回北京。

===Original===
In spite of Her Majesty’s cordiality and the efforts of the Princesses and Ladies, these garden parties were not always as pleasant as they might be. There seemed an absolute lack of harmony among the ladies of the Legation. Each seemed to watch the other with a jealous eye, in constant fear that someone might overstep her place. Some did not hesitate, even, to show their private animosities on the steps of the Throne, or before their hostesses at the table. They seemed to act on the principle that the Chinese, not understanding the language, would not understand anything else. It was unfortunate that this most punctilious of people, the Chinese, should have had this apparent lack of friendliness to judge the European ladies by. They received all with equal favor and perfect etiquette, and it was a pity that the lack of harmony among the foreign ladies should have led them to commit what seemed to be breaches of etiquette, which the Chinese could not have failed to observe. I was astonished to see how observant the latter were and how accurately they gauged our standing.

===Chinese===
尽管皇太后待人以诚，王妃格格与命妇们也费心周旋，这些游园会却未必都尽如人意。公使馆的夫人之间，似有一种绝对的不睦。人人皆以妒忌的目光打量旁人，生怕有人僭越了自己的位次。有的甚至毫不顾忌，竟在宝座阶陛之前，或在席间女主人面前，公然流露私怨。她们似乎抱着这样的心思：中国人既听不懂她们的话，自然也看不出别的来。可叹这最讲礼仪的中国人，竟要凭此等显然不睦之举，来评判欧洲的夫人。中方以同等礼遇和完美的礼节接待众人，而外国夫人之间这份失睦，竟使她们做出若干看似失礼的举动，中国人又怎能看不出来呢。后者洞察之细、品评我们身分之准，着实令我惊讶。

===Original===
Their comments on our costumes were also very interesting. Her Majesty seemed to like foreign dress, especially when in pretty colors, for she reveled in color. She said the foreign costume was very becoming to well-made and well-proportioned people; but she thought, while it showed off to advantage a good figure, it was unfortunate for anyone who was not so blessed. She thought the Chinese costume, falling in straight lines from the shoulder, was more becoming to stout people, for it hid many defects. One universal comment, among these Chinese ladies, on us, was that we look old for our years. The well-bred Chinese repress, from early childhood, all outward evidences of emotion. They lead such simple, wholesome lives—“Early to bed and early to rise”—that there are rarely any lines visible in their faces until they reach an advanced age, when they seem to go suddenly from ripe womanhood into extreme old age.

===Chinese===
她们对我们服饰的评点，也颇有意味。皇太后似乎喜欢洋装，尤喜色彩鲜丽者，因她本就嗜好色彩。她说洋装穿在体态匀称、风姿绰约之人身上十分相宜；但她也以为，洋装虽能衬出好身段，于那些天资未逮者却不免苛刻。她认为中式的衣裳自双肩笔直垂落，于丰腴之人反更相宜，因它能遮掩诸多瑕疵。这些中国夫人对我们有一句普遍的评语——便是我们看上去比实际年龄显老。教养良好的中国人，自幼便压抑一切情绪的外露。他们过着那样简朴而健康的生活——「早睡早起」——以致脸上极少见皱纹，直到年事已高，才仿佛由丰韵的中年遽然步入极老之态。

===Original===
They have a particular aversion to blond hair. They did not tell me so, as I have blond hair; but on the stage all the demons are represented with blond hair, and the more blond it is, the more wicked the demon. One day, one of the Ladies suggested to me that there were some very fine vegetable hair dyes for turning the hair black without injuring it; in fact, the growth was increased thereby. She said if I used this, my hair “might in time become black; at least, it would grow much darker.”

===Chinese===
她们对金发有种特别的厌恶。这一点她们并未明说与我，因我自己便是金发；但在戏台上，一切妖魔鬼怪都作金发扮相，金发越浅，则其妖越甚。一日，有位命妇向我建言，说有种极好的植物染发剂，可使头发变黑而无伤发质，反能使头发更易生长。她说我若用了此物，头发「假以时日或可变黑；至少也会深得多」。

## VI Continuation of the Portrait—Her Majesty's Dogs / 第六章 续绘画像——皇太后的狗

===Original===
I had daily morning sittings from Her Majesty for the portrait, but always surrounded by the whole Court, with eunuchs coming and going. The sittings were long enough, for I had an hour in the morning and a half-hour in the afternoon with Her Majesty, but she did not expect me to work except when she posed, and this was not enough to make any headway on the picture, as there was a great deal I might have done at other times. Though there was so much going and coming in the Throne-room, it was a great advantage working in Her Majesty's own milieu, surrounded by her favorite furniture, flowers, and fruits. This was some compensation; but I saw, if Her Majesty insisted upon my resting when she did—if I were allowed to work only in the Throne-room and only when she posed—the work could not go on as it should. Sitting for her portrait seemed to be looked on somewhat in the light of an amusement by the Empress Dowager, as a time for conversation and relaxation. She put me many questions while she sat, and I felt she was studying me as closely as I was studying her during that time.

My interest in the personality of this wonderful woman increased each day. I loved to watch the extreme mobility of her countenance when she was at ease and was not invested in her official expression, nor her Buddha-like pose. Her voice was most musical, with no indication of age in it. Her enunciation was clear, and I loved to hear her talk. Though understanding but little of what she said, the music of her voice, the grace of her gesticulations, and the charm of her smile made her conversation most delightful to watch and listen to.

===Chinese===
我每日上午都为画像向皇太后坐班，但身侧总是被全宫的人簇拥着，太监们来来去去，络绎不绝。坐班的时间原不算短，上午在太后跟前一个钟头，下午半个钟头，可她并不许我在她不摆姿势的时候动笔，而这远远不够把画像推进下去，因为有许多活计我原可趁别的时候做。宝座殿里人来人往虽多，能在皇太后自己的天地里作画，被她钟爱的家具、花卉、果品环绕着，却也是一桩极大的便宜。这总算是一种补偿；然而我也看得明白，倘若皇太后执意要我也随她一同歇息——倘若只许我在宝座殿里作画、只许在她摆姿势的时候动笔——这活计便断不能如它应有的那样推进下去。在皇太后看来，坐着让人画像，似乎多少被当作一种消遣，是闲谈与松散的辰光。她坐着的时候向我问了许多话，我觉着在那段时间里，她把我打量得与我打量她一般仔细。

我对这位奇女子性情的兴趣，与日俱增。她一旦松弛下来，不再端着那副临朝的神情、也不再摆出那佛陀似的姿态，面上那种极灵活的变幻，我最爱看。她的声音极是动听，里头一点也听不出年纪。她吐字清晰，我爱听她说话。虽说她的话我懂的甚少，可那声音的乐感、那手势的优雅、那笑容里的风韵，使她的言谈无论看起来还是听起来，都极是动人。

===Original===
I was delighted that Her Majesty seemed to like me, and I appreciated her consideration in not wishing me to tire myself out with my work, and her kind hospitality which desired to make me acquainted with the charms of the Summer Palace and which allowed me to participate in her promenades and the simple amusements of her Ladies; but I felt it was important to advance the work on the portrait as quickly as possible. I knew that the "favor of kings" is uncertain, and I feared Her Majesty might soon tire of this new departure, of having her portrait painted! I feared the openly expressed opposition of the Chinese to a foreign lady being made a member of the Court circle, their superstition regarding the painting of a portrait of one of Their Majesties, which was against all Chinese tradition, might any day put a stop to the work; but, notwithstanding my fears and my desire to work, the days passed with little painting, and this was the only flaw in my perfect enjoyment of the fairy-like days and the unique experiences through which I was passing.

===Chinese===
皇太后似乎颇喜欢我，我甚是欣喜；她体恤我不愿我因作画累坏了身子，又一片好意地款待我，要领我识得颐和园的诸般妙处，许我随她一同出游、与她的命妇们一同做些简单的消遣——这些我都领情；可我心里明白，尽快把画像推进下去，才是要紧的事。我深知「天威难测」，只怕皇太后不久便会对这桩新鲜事——让人给自己画像——生了厌！我又怕中国人公开表示的反对，不愿一位外国女子被纳入宫廷的圈子；怕他们那种为皇上皇太后画像的忌讳——此事有悖于一切中国的旧例——说不定哪一日便会使这活计戛然而止；然而，尽管我心怀忧惧、又急于动笔，日子还是一天天过去，画却画得甚少。这是我在那一段如仙境般的日子里、在那一段绝无仅有的经历中，唯一美中不足之处。

===Original===
The walks with Her Majesty had all the pomp and ceremony of the boat-rides—Her Majesty's and the Empress's yellow satin sedan chairs, with their six bearers, leading off, followed by the red chairs of the Princesses and Ladies-in-waiting, according to their rank, with a rigorous adherence to precedence, and attended by an army of eunuchs and chair-bearers, etc. No one ever knew what our destination was to be when we started out on these walks, Her Majesty directing her chair-bearers as she was carried along, and the others following this lead; but we were always taken to some interesting spot, where there was something quite worth seeing. When Her Majesty's chair stopped, all the others were immediately put down by the bearers, and the Ladies got out and went up to where the Empress Dowager's yellow campstool was placed. She had excellent taste in the choice of stopping-places, and the views were always picturesque. She seemed to take great pleasure in showing off the charming points of view, as well as the flowers, grounds, and buildings.

===Chinese===
随皇太后步行出游，也和乘舟游湖一样地仪仗煊赫、礼数周全——皇太后和皇后的明黄缎轿各由六名轿夫抬着，走在最前头，随后是王妃与命妇们的红轿，按品级鱼贯而行，等级森严、次序不乱，前后还有一大群太监、轿夫等伺候。每回启程出门，谁也不知我们要去往何处，皇太后坐在轿里，一边走一边吩咐轿夫，其余的人只管跟着她的引领；然而我们总被抬到一处有趣的所在，那里总有颇值得一看的东西。皇太后的轿一停下，其余的轿立刻也被轿夫放下，命妇们下轿，走到皇太后那张明黄的折叠御座安放之处。她择取停驻之处，眼光极好，那处的景致总是如画一般。她似乎极乐于向人炫示那些悦目的观景之处，以及那些花卉、园囿、亭台楼阁。

===Original===
On one of our walks, her dogs were brought out by their attendant eunuchs. Dogs are great favorites with all the Chinese, and especially with the Empress Dowager. She has some magnificent specimens of Pekingese pugs and of a sort of Skye terrier. The pugs are bred with great care and have reached a high state of perfection, their spots being perfectly symmetrical and their hair beautifully long and silky, and they are of wonderful intelligence. The King Charles spaniels are said to have been bred out of the first of these dogs ever carried to Europe. The Empress Dowager has dozens of these pets, but she has favorites among them, and two are privileged characters. One of these is of the Skye variety, and is most intelligent and clever at tricks. Among other tricks, he will lie as dead at Her Majesty's command, and never move until she tells him to, no matter how many others may speak to him. Her other favorite she loves for his beauty. He is a splendid, fawn-colored Pekingese pug, with large, pale-brown, liquid eyes. He is devoted to her, and she is very fond of him, but as he was not easily taught, even as a puppy, she called him "Shadza" (fool). Her dogs all have most appropriate names, given by herself. They know Her Majesty's voice and will obey her slightest word.

===Chinese===
有一回出游，她的狗由各自看管的太监牵了出来。狗是中国人——尤其是皇太后——极钟爱的。她养着几只极出色的京巴哈巴狗，还有几只斯凯㹴一类的狗。这些哈巴狗是用心繁育出来的，已到了极高的境地，身上的花斑对称得一丝不差，毛长得如丝一般柔美，又极其聪慧。据说英国的查理王猎犬，便是从当初最早带到欧洲的这类狗繁育而来的。皇太后这样的宠物有几十只，可其中也有她最偏爱的，有两只更是有特权的角色。一只是斯凯种，极其聪明，又擅作种种把戏。诸般把戏之外，皇太后一声令下，它便会直挺挺地躺着装死，无论旁人再有多少人去唤它，它一动也不动，直到她发话才起来。另一只爱犬，她爱的是它的美貌。它是一只神气的、淡黄褐色的京巴哈巴狗，一双淡褐色的大眼睛水汪汪的。它对她忠心耿耿，她也极疼它，只是这狗自小便不大容易教导，于是她唤它「傻子」（Shadza）。她的狗每一只都有一个最贴切的名字，都是她亲自起的。它们都认得皇太后的嗓音，她只消轻吐一字，它们便无不听从。

===Original===
The Empress Dowager does not care for the small sleeve-dog; she hates the thought of their being stunted by being fed only on sweets and wines. She says she cannot understand animals being deformed, at man's pleasure. The day we first met the dogs in the garden was the first time I had seen them. They rushed up to Her Majesty, not paying the slightest attention to anyone else. She patted their heads and caressed and spoke to her favorites. After a while they seemed to notice that a stranger was present, and they bounded over toward me. Some of them growled and showed other evidences of displeasure, some seemed surprised almost to fear; but as the instinct of a dog never deceives him as to who is his friend, this was all soon changed to friendly greetings. I bent down to caress them, and forgot my surroundings, in my pleasure at seeing and fondling these beautiful creatures. I glanced up, presently, never dreaming Her Majesty had been paying any attention to me, as I was standing at a little distance behind her, and I saw on her face the first sign of displeasure I had noticed there. It seems her dogs never noticed anyone but herself, and she appeared not to like her pets being so friendly with a stranger at first sight. Noticing this, I immediately ceased fondling them, and they were presently sent away. It was but a momentary shadow that passed over her face, and I quite understood the feeling. One does not like to see one's pets too friendly with strangers, and I had been tactless in trying to make friends with them at once.

===Chinese===
皇太后不喜欢那种小巧的袖犬；她一想到它们只喂糖果和酒、以致长不大，便心生厌恶。她说，动物竟为着人的趣味而弄成畸形，她实在不能理解。我们在园中初见那些狗的那一日，是我头一回见到它们。它们一齐扑向皇太后，对旁人正眼也不看。她拍拍它们的头，抚摩着她最疼的那几只，又与它们说话。过了一会儿，它们似乎察觉有生人在场，便蹦跳着朝我奔过来。有几只发出低吼，露出了几分不悦的模样，有几只则像受了惊、几乎怕将起来；然而狗的直觉从不曾教它认错谁是朋友，这一阵很快便化作了亲热的招呼。我弯下身去抚弄它们，一时忘了身在何处，只顾着看见、抚摸这些美丽的生灵而满心欢喜。过了一会儿我抬起头——我原站在太后身后稍远处，做梦也不曾想她竟留意着我——便瞧见她脸上露出了我到此以后所见的第一丝不悦之色。原来她的狗从不理睬她以外的人，而她似乎不喜欢自己的爱畜一上来便与一个陌生人这般亲热。我察觉之后，立刻不再抚弄它们，不多时它们便被带下去了。那一丝不悦不过是她脸上转瞬即逝的一片阴云，其情我也甚能体谅。谁都不愿自己的爱畜与生人过分亲热，我贸然就要和它们交朋友，未免太不知轻重了。

===Original===
A few days later, on another of our walks, some young puppies were brought to be shown the Empress Dowager. She caressed the mother and examined critically the points of the puppies. Then she called me up to show them to me, asking me which I liked best. I tried not to evince too much interest in them this time, but she called my attention to their fine points and insisted upon my taking each of them up. She seemed to be ashamed of her slight displeasure of the day before, and to wish to compensate for it.

===Chinese===
几日之后，又一次出游，有人抱了几只小狗崽来给皇太后看。她抚弄着母狗，又将那几只小狗崽的品相细细相看过。随后她把我叫到跟前，把它们指给我看，问我最喜欢哪一只。这一回我竭力不让自己对它们显出过多的兴致，可她却一一将那些好处指给我瞧，非要我每只都抱上一抱不可。她似乎为自己前日那一点不悦而抱愧，想要以此作个补偿。

===Original===
The dogs at the Palace are kept in a beautiful pavilion with marble floors. They have silken cushions to sleep on, and special eunuchs to attend them. They are taken for daily outdoor exercise and given their baths with regularity. There are hundreds of dogs in the Palace, the young Empress, the Princesses and Ladies, and even the eunuchs, having their own. Some of the eunuchs are great fanciers and breeders of them. One of them still breeds the sleeve-dog. Her Majesty's known dislike to these latter is probably the cause of fewer being bred in the Palace now than formerly; and the race is slowly dying out. All the other dogs in the Palace, except Her Majesty's, are kept in the apartments and courts of their owners, and are not seen by her.

She dislikes cats very much, but some of the eunuchs have very fine specimens of the felines. They keep them, however, sub rosa and within rigid bounds, on no condition allowing them to come within Her Majesty's ken.

===Chinese===
宫中的狗养在一处华美的亭阁里，地下铺着大理石。它们睡的是绸缎的垫子，各有专职的太监伺候。每日由人带出户外活动，沐浴也有定时。宫里的狗数以百计，皇后、王妃、命妇，乃至太监们，都各有各的。太监里有几个是养狗、繁育狗的大行家。其中有一人至今还在繁育袖犬。皇太后对袖犬的反感众所周知，这大约便是如今宫中所繁的袖犬比从前少的原因；这一品种正慢慢地绝迹。除了皇太后的狗以外，宫里其余的狗都养在各自主子的屋子里和院落中，她是从不见的。

她极厌恶猫，可太监中有几个养着极好的猫。只是他们都偷偷地养，严严地管束着，无论如何也不许它们落入皇太后的眼帘。

===Original===
The pavilion at the Summer Palace where the Empress Dowager's dogs were kept was near her Throne-room, and also near the pavilion she had set aside for me. When the Court was taking its siesta, I used to go out where the dogs were basking in the sun in their court and look at and play with these interesting little animals. I was free to do as I pleased, and no one but the dogs' guardian eunuchs saw me there.

Among the younger set, of these pampered pets, was one that caught my fancy—one of those which had been brought for Her Majesty to look at in the garden. He was a beautiful white-and-amber-colored Pekingese pug. He soon learned to know me and would come running to me when I crossed the threshold of the court. Not long after I had discovered where the dogs were kept and had been paying them my daily visits, one night, when we had finished dinner at Her Majesty's table, one of her eunuchs brought in this very little dog and put it in my arms, saying Her Majesty had presented it to me from her own kennel! She had evidently learned of my visits to the dogs, though none of the eunuchs around her person had seen me go there, at least so I thought! I was delighted to own this beautiful animal, and when the Empress Dowager came into the Throne-room from her own apartments, I went up to her and kissed her hand and thanked her for it. She seemed much pleased that I liked it, and remarked that she had heard it was my favorite of her dogs, that I was to call him "Me-lah" (Golden Amber), from the color of his spots. Her Majesty and the Princesses were all much amused at the way he followed me around, not leaving my side for an instant, nor paying any attention to their frequent efforts to attract his attention. From that day, he became my constant companion and faithful friend.

===Chinese===
颐和园里养皇太后那些狗的亭阁，就在她的宝座殿附近，也离她拨给我住的那间亭阁不远。宫中人午歇之时，我常走到狗们晒太阳的院子里，去看这些有趣的小生灵，与它们玩耍一番。我尽可随意来去，除了看狗的太监，也无人瞧见我。

在这些娇养的爱畜里，年岁小的一辈中，有一只甚合我的心意——便是那一日抱到园里给皇太后看的那几只里的一只。它是一只美丽的、白底带琥珀色斑的京巴哈巴狗。它不多时便认得了我，我一跨进那院子的门槛，它便奔过来迎我。自从我发觉狗养在何处、每日前去探望之后不久，有一晚，我们在太后桌上用罢晚膳，她的一名太监便将这小小一只狗抱了进来，放到我怀里，说这是皇太后从她自己的狗窝里赏给我的！她显然已知晓我去看狗的事——虽说她身边那些太监并未曾见我到那里去过，至少我原是这样以为！得了这只美丽的生灵，我欢喜得很，待皇太后从自己的寝宫来到宝座殿，我便走到她跟前，吻了她的手，向她道谢。见我喜欢，她似乎十分欣慰，又说她听说这是她那些狗里我最偏爱的一只，叫我唤它「美拉」（Me-lah，意即金琥珀），是依它身上斑点的颜色而起的。皇太后和几位王妃见它一路跟着我寸步不离，任凭她们频频逗引也不理睬，都觉着十分有趣。自那日起，它便成了我形影不离的伴侣、忠心耿耿的朋友。

## XXXIII Continuation of the St. Louis Portrait—Spring Days at the Sea Palace / 第三十三章 圣路易斯画像续篇——西苑春日

===Original===
There began now to be some discussion as to what would be the most propitious date for finishing the portrait. I had thought I might finish when I could, but this was not to be the case. The almanacs were consulted, and it was decided that the nineteenth day of April would be an auspicious time to finish and before four o’clock! The Empress Dowager informed me of the “happy augury” of this date and asked me if I thought it possible to finish then. Not only had the date for beginning the portrait been carefully chosen, but there was much deliberation as to the proper time for finishing! Her Majesty seemed very anxious until she received my reply as to whether it would be possible to finish at this happy date, for I could not say at first, as I had never thought of finishing at any particular moment! When I finally told her I could finish it before four o’clock, April 19th, she was delighted. She said “How good” and asked me to please “not disappoint her.” As the portrait neared completion she came very often to the studio and watched over the painting-in of all the accessories, which she seemed to consider quite as important as the likeness itself. As she was tired after the Audiences, she gave me two or three sittings at this time before she went to the Audience Hall, and I painted from half-past six to eight a.m. for two or three days. The jewels in the headdress, all official, were the subject of much deliberation. After a jewel was painted in, she would decide she didn’t like it and that something else would be better. She seemed to think it was as easy to take it from the picture as to remove it from her person. All these requests for changes were so graciously made, I never complained. She would sometimes say, “I am giving you a great deal of trouble, and you are very kind.” I didn’t mind the trouble, only these changes took away the freshness of the painting and did not add to the artistic effect of the picture.

Her Majesty ordered a magnificent frame for the portrait. She, herself, made the design. The Double Dragon at the top struggled for the “flaming pearl” with the character “*Sho*” on it. The sides were elaborately carved in designs representing the symbol of “ten thousand” years with the characters for longevity. The frame was to be set in a superbly carved stand, as the Chinese do their mirrors. The whole, of rare camphor-wood, was made by Her Majesty’s own artisans at the Palace﻿—the most expert workmen in China.

===Chinese===
此时开始有人议论，究竟哪一日才是画像完工最为吉利的日子。我原以为画完便是，谁知不然。皇太后命人查阅历书，议定四月十九日乃完工的吉时，且须在午后四时之前！皇太后将这日子的“吉兆”告知于我，问我届时能否完工。原来画像开笔的日子是精心择定的，如今连完工的时辰也颇费了一番斟酌！皇太后殷切盼我的答复——能否恰在这吉日完工——神情颇为焦虑。我起初难以应承，因我从未想过要在某一特定时刻收笔！待我终于告诉她，四月十九日午后四时之前可以完工，她满心欢喜，说“好极了”，并请我务必“不要让她失望”。画像将近完工时，她常到画室里来，亲自看着我把种种饰物一一描上，似乎视这些配件与肖像本身同等紧要。因觐见之后每每疲惫不堪，此时她在前往觐见大殿之前，给了我两三次坐班的机会，我接连两三日从清晨六点半画到八点。凤冠上的珠宝，皆按朝廷规制，成了反复斟酌的题目。往往一颗珠宝画上之后，她又觉不甚合意，以为换一样更佳。她似乎以为，将珠宝从画上去掉，便如同从她身上取下一件饰物那般容易。但凡这些更改之请，她说来都如此和颜悦色，我从未有过半句怨言。她有时会说：“我给你添了许多麻烦，你真是太好了。”我倒不嫌麻烦，只是这些改动损耗了画面的鲜润，于整幅画的艺术效果并无增益。

皇太后又为这幅画像定制了一架华美的画框，图样是她亲手所绘。框顶双龙戏珠，珠上镌一“寿”字。两侧精雕细琢，刻着“万”字不断头的纹样与“寿”字图案。画框将嵌于一架雕工绝妙的座子之中，恰如中国人镶嵌铜镜的法子。整座画框以上等樟木制成，出自皇太后宫中的匠人之手——乃是全中国最精湛的良工。

===Original===
The days were lengthening now, the trees beginning to bud and the flowers in the courts to bloom. The icy fetters that had locked the lake were broken; the boats again glided over its bosom. In the mornings we no longer had to take the winter “chairs” and be carried the long distance from the gates to the Throne-room. The comfortable boats once more lay moored at the foot of the landing-steps, just within the gates, and we enjoyed again those ideal trips across the lake.

The Empress Dowager began to take long promenades now and was much out-of-doors. Sometimes in the mornings, on our arrival, she would already be in the gardens. One day we met her on the banks of the lake and made our morning salutations there. Another day, she and the Emperor were inspecting the new buildings which were being erected to replace those burned during the occupation of Peking by the Allies, when Count von Waldersee had his headquarters in the Sea Palace. Splendid buildings were being erected on the site of those burned. The Emperor and Empress Dowager, each with his own suite, carefully visited every part of these new constructions and seemed much interested in their progress. Of course, the workmen were banished during the visit of Their Majesties. One of these new halls was to be used for the entertainment of foreigners, when they are invited to the Palace, and many concessions had been made to foreign ideas in its construction. Let us hope it may not lose its Chinese character! I am sure the foreigners will regret this innovation and would prefer the typical Chinese interior, even though it be less suited to the exigencies of a modern reception.

Sometimes we would see the Empress Dowager in her Japanese jinricksha. This was a beautiful, gold-lacquered affair in dragon form, the two dragons’ heads in front. It had splendid gold-lacquered shafts and wheels﻿—the latter with rubber tires. It was pulled by one eunuch and pushed by another, and Her Majesty seemed greatly to enjoy this novelty for a while, but she said she preferred to walk or to be carried in her open chair, as a usual thing.

===Chinese===
此时日头渐长，树木抽芽，庭中百花竞相开放。锁住湖面的冰枷已然断裂，船只又在其波光之上轻轻滑行。清晨我们不再须乘坐冬日的轿椅，从宫门一路抬到正殿那么远的一段路。那几只舒适的游船又重新泊在登岸的石阶脚下——就在宫门内侧——我们得以再度享受那几趟横渡湖面的理想之旅。

皇太后此时常出来作长距离的散步，多半时日都在户外。有时清晨我们赶到时，她已在花园之中了。一日我们在湖畔遇见她，便在彼处行了晨间请安之礼。又一日，她与皇上正在视察新建的殿宇——这些殿宇是为了替代联军占据北京时被焚毁的那些建筑，当年瓦德西伯爵（Count von Waldersee）曾将统帅部设于西苑之内。在被焚殿宇的旧址上，一座座壮丽的新厦正拔地而起。皇上与皇太后各带各的随从，仔细察看这些新建筑的每一处，似乎对其进展颇为关切。两宫驾临之时，工匠们自然要暂且回避。这些新殿之中，有一座将专门用来款待应邀入宫的外国人，其建造已在许多地方迁就了外国的式样。但愿它不至于失去中国的风神！我深信外国人见了这种革新必会引以为憾，他们宁愿要那地道的中国式陈设，哪怕它不太合乎现代招待会的需要。

有时我们会看见皇太后坐在她那辆日本人力车上。这是一件金漆髹就的华美之物，整车作龙形，车前是两颗龙头。车辕与车轮皆金漆彩绘——后者且蒙着橡皮轮胎。一名太监在前拉，一名太监在后推。皇太后一时似乎颇喜这新鲜玩意儿，但她说寻常还是宁可步行，或是乘坐她那顶敞开的轿椅。

===Original===
Two other modern and novel methods of locomotion had been installed in the grounds of the Sea Palace. There was a small railway, which ran from the outer gates to the dwelling Palaces, which had its engine and complete running outfit. This had been constructed by some progressive Mandarins, who wished to get the Empress Dowager’s support for some railway scheme, but though she often spoke of how much she had enjoyed her one trip on a real railway, her spirit was too utilitarian to care for toy pleasures. She couldn’t stand the puffing of the engine, the tiny cars, and all this trouble for so short and useless a jaunt.

There was also in the Sea Palace, as well as at the Summer Palace, a number of automobiles, which had been presented to Their Majesties by Chinese nobles and officials who had been abroad, as examples of the curiosities of European civilization. One of these was gorgeously fitted up in the Imperial yellow and gold lacquer, with the Double Dragon. The body was enclosed in glass and there was a throne-like seat within for the Empress Dowager. The question of how the chauffeur should run the machine standing, as he would be obliged to do if Her Majesty were inside, had not then been solved. She was, however, willing to throw tradition to the winds in this instance, and was most anxious to try one of these motorcars. Her entourage was, however, bitterly opposed to it, even for a short distance in the grounds. They were afraid of an accident. She never tried one while I was there, but I am confident that her venturesome spirit will not rest content until she has had a ride in one of these modern carriages.

In April, kite-flying time begins in China. High Officials and dignified literati indulge in the pastime as well as children and young people. The popular pastimes of the people, as well as their serious occupations, being always honored in the Palace, kites were, of course, sent off by the Empress Dowager and the Ladies. The first day the kites were to be flown Her Majesty sent for me to come into the garden, where the kite-flying was to take place. The kites were of paper, wonderfully fashioned, representing birds, fish, bats, and even personages. The strings were wound on curiously shaped reels and the cleverness with which Her Majesty let out the string and manipulated the kites was wonderful. After she had let one go, she graciously handed me her own reel and told me she would teach me to fly a kite. I was hard at work at my painting when I was called out into the garden and I wished to return to it as soon as possible; and as I knew I would not be very clever at kite-flying, I begged her to allow me to watch her instead. The young Empress and Princesses were also very proficient in flying them, and Her Majesty flew hers as she did everything else, with unusual grace.

===Chinese===
西苑的宫苑之内，还安装了另外两种新式而新奇的代步之物。其一是条小铁路，从外门直通至寝宫，配有机车及一应行车设备。这是几位趋新的大臣所造，原想借此博得皇太后对某项铁路计划的支持。然而皇太后虽常说她在真正的铁路上曾有过一次旅行，如何津津有味，她的性情却太讲实用，对这种“玩具”式的乐趣不感兴趣。机车扑扑的喷气声，那些小车厢，还有为这么短一段毫无用处的路程大费周章——她到底受不住。

西苑与颐和园之中，还各有汽车数辆，乃是中国出洋回来的王公大臣进呈两宫的，作为欧洲文明中一项新奇之物。其中一辆装点得金碧辉煌——通体御用明黄，髹以金漆，饰以双龙。车身四周镶着玻璃，车内设有形如宝座的座位，专为皇太后而设。至于司机若皇太后在车内，便须立着开车——这一难题彼时还未解决。然而这一回皇太后倒愿意将祖宗规矩抛诸脑后，一心想试乘一回这些汽车。只是她身边侍从极力反对，哪怕只在苑内走一小段也不肯。他们唯恐出事。我在宫中之时，她始终未曾试过一回，但我深信，以她那敢作敢为的性情，一日不曾坐过这种新式车马游历一番，便一日不得安宁。

四月间，中国开始放风筝。朝廷大员与端庄的文士也和孩童少年一般，都以此道为戏。民间流行的种种消遣与正经营生一样，在宫中向来也受尊崇，于是放风筝之事，自然也由皇太后与宫中女眷们亲自施行。放风筝的头一日，皇太后派人唤我入园——风筝便要在园中放飞。那些风筝乃纸扎而成，造型奇巧，有作飞鸟的，有作游鱼的，有作蝙蝠的，甚至有作人物的。线缠在形状古怪的线轴之上，皇太后放线、操纵风筝的手法之巧，令人叹为观止。她放起一只之后，便和颜悦色地将自己的线轴递与我，说要教我放风筝。我原在画前埋头用功，被唤入园中，巴不得即刻回去；况且我自知放风筝不甚灵巧，便央她让我在一旁观看为是。皇后与众位公主放风筝也都十分娴熟，皇太后放起风筝来，正如她做其他事一样，举手投足间自有一种异乎寻常的优雅。

===Original===
One of these beautiful spring mornings as we were softly gliding across the lake, propelled by the graceful Palace boatmen, I lay back on my cushions reveling in the scene of quiet loveliness before me and drinking in the ineffable perfume of the spring, when my glance, roaming lazily around in perfect content, caught sight of a group of gentlemen on the bank of the lake beyond. The rays of the morning sun, glinting upon the gold of their embroidered costumes and touching, with iridescent rays, the peacock’s feathers upon their hats, revealed their rank and official standing.

As it was a most unusual thing to see gentlemen in the Palace Enclosure, I was at once all attention, knowing there must be some important event on hand, especially as, on looking closer, I saw one small figure in their midst more plainly dressed than the others, whom I at once recognized as His Majesty the Emperor. As we slowly approached I saw the Emperor go over to a plow to which was hitched an ox, and which stood at a little distance off in the field. Fortune favored me! I was to see the Emperor plow the first furrow of the year! For it was only on the morrow that the official public ceremony was to take place at the Temple of Agriculture, near the great triple altar of Heaven. I was to see the private plowing, done in the Palace grounds and viewed only by the Princes of the Imperial Family and the highest Manchu nobles.

When all was ready the Emperor took the handles of the plow and guided it down a furrow marked off the ground, and when the furrow was upturned, the seed was dropped in. The ox for this ceremony, which I had heard was white, was (at the Palace function) of a soft doe color. He seemed to have been trained for the purpose and performed his part with a dignity in harmony with the attitude of all the assistants and in keeping with the solemnity of the occasion.

===Chinese===
一个晴美的春日清晨，宫中船夫身手矫捷，轻撑着我们悄然滑过湖面。我倚在软垫之上，沉醉于眼前静谧可爱的景致，饱吸着春日里那不可名状的芳馨——正当此时，我那慵懒四顾、满怀惬意的目光，忽然瞥见远处湖岸上有一群男子。晨光洒在他们绣金朝服的金线之上，又以五彩的光芒点染他们帽顶的花翎，其所佩戴的品级与官阶便一览无遗。

在宫禁之内见到男子乃是极不寻常之事，我顿时全神贯注，心知必有重大典礼正在进行。尤其待我定睛细看，只见那群人中间有一位身量不高、装束较他人朴素的人物——我当即认出，正是皇上。船渐行渐近，我看见皇上走向一架套着牛的犁，那犁就立在田中不远之处。真是天赐良机！我竟得以亲眼目睹皇上耕下这一年的第一犁！因为正式的典礼要到次日才在先农坛举行——就在那座宏大的三重天坛附近。我所见的乃是宫苑之内的“亲耕”之礼，只有皇室亲王与满洲最高贵族方得在旁观礼。

一切就绪之后，皇上扶住犁柄，引犁沿着地上划好的垄沟前行，待土翻起，便将种子撒入其中。此礼所用之牛，我曾听说是白色的，然在宫中这场典礼之上，却是一种柔和的浅黄褐色。这牛似乎久经训练，举止间自有一番端严，恰与在场诸人的神情相称，也合于这场合的肃穆庄重。

===Original===
I was rejoiced to have an opportunity of seeing this interesting ceremony and to learn that even this great rite, which I had thought, like the sacrifice to the Invisible Deity on the triple altar, was only performed in the grounds of the Temple to Heaven; and to learn that every custom dear to the people, or incorporated in the National life, is observed in the Palace by the Emperor and Empress﻿—that His Majesty really plants the first furrow of the year and gathers the first sheaves of ripened wheat, and that the Ladies of the Palace really spin the first silk and pull the first fruits.

The slow movement of the Palace boats was never so appreciated by me as on this morning, for I was thus enabled to see well this curious National ceremony, which I would never have seen but for the accident of the hour of my crossing the lake and the time it took to do so; for, as at all ceremonies where men are present, there were, of course, no members of Her Majesty’s entourage, and none of the Ladies or Princesses had ever seen this ceremony!

===Chinese===
我能有机会亲见这一有趣的典礼，心中十分欢喜；由此又得知：这场我曾以为如同三重天坛上祭祀皇天上帝的献祭一般、只在天坛之内举行的盛大典礼，其实并非如此——更得知凡为百姓所珍视、或已融入国民生活的种种习俗，皇上与皇太后在宫中也无不一一遵行——皇上当真亲手耕下每年的第一犁、收割第一束成熟的麦穗，宫中女眷们当真亲手缫第一缕丝、摘第一枚果实。

宫船行得迟缓，从未有如这一晨般令我欣喜，正因如此，我方能从容细看这场新奇的国家典礼。若不是恰逢其时渡湖，又恰巧花了那么多时间，我断无缘得见——因为凡有男子在场的典礼，皇太后身边自无女眷随行，宫中的夫人和公主们竟也从未见过这典礼呢！

## Dedication / 题献

===Original===
**To Sir Robert Hart**

To whose helpful encouragement I owe so much, I affectionately dedicate this account of my experiences at the Court of the country he has so long and faithfully served.

Katherine A. Carl.
New York, May, 1905.

===Chinese===
**献给罗伯特·赫德爵士（Sir Robert Hart）**

他出于好意的鼓励使我受惠良多，我满怀感激地将这份宫廷经历记述奉献给他——那个国家，他已长久而忠实地效力多年。

凯瑟琳·A·卡尔
纽约，一九〇五年五月

## VII Festivities at Court / 七 宫中庆典

===Original===
Preparations were now beginning at the Palace for the celebration of His Majesty the Emperor’s Birthday. This is not celebrated on the anniversary of the day he was born, but two days earlier. His Majesty must make the Autumnal Sacrifices to his Ancestors three days after the real date of his Birthday, and he must prepare himself for these sacrifices by a rigorous fast of three days. As it would be impossible to accomplish the ceremonial prescribed for the Imperial Birthday while fasting, the celebration of the Birthday was advanced, a special edict having been issued by the two Empresses, when Co-Regents for the young Emperor, ordering the Birthday celebrations to be advanced by two days, for the date of the sacrifices could not be changed﻿—the sacrifice to one’s Ancestors being the most sacred of obligations to the Chinese, and most rigidly and religiously observed. Even the Chinese Emperor’s Birthday is not celebrated for two years after the death of his predecessor, so rigorous are the rules of respect to the dead and the rites accorded to one’s Ancestors in China.

===Chinese===
宫中如今开始筹备皇上（the Emperor）万寿节（Emperor's Birthday）的庆典。这万寿节并不在皇上降生的正日那天庆贺，而是提前两天。皇上须在真正万寿正日之后的第三日，向列祖列宗行秋祭之礼，为此他必须斋戒三日，以示诚敬。斋戒期间既不能举行万寿节既定的种种典礼，故而将万寿庆典提前。当年两位皇太后曾共同为年幼的皇上摄政，其时特颁一道懿旨，命将万寿庆典提前两日——盖因祭祖之期不可更动：祭祖对中国人而言乃是最为神圣的义务，奉行最严，敬守最虔。就连中国皇上宾天之后，新君的万寿节也要停办两年，由此可见中国对亡者的礼数与对祖先的祭仪，规矩是何等森严。

===Original===
I knew no painting could be done during these festivities, and I expected to go back to the United States Legation. I never dreamed I should be invited to participate in this celebration, hitherto unseen by any foreigner. A week before the Birthday itself, when out for one of our walks with Her Majesty, she called me up to her side and said the Emperor’s Birthday was to be celebrated the next week, and invited me to remain in the Palace for these festivities. I was, of course, overjoyed at this gracious mark of her favor, and delighted to be able to see the Oriental pomp and pageantry that accompanied these ceremonial celebrations in China.

There were to be magnificent theatrical performances, splendid fireworks and decorations, and all sorts of pageants. The Imperial company of actors had already begun rehearsing special poems and plays, written to celebrate the occasion. Eunuchs were constantly bringing Her Majesty specimens of the work of the decorators and painters who were carrying out her designs as to special scenes and tableaux, or coming to ask for further instructions. The literati, who were preparing the original poems, sent in their manuscripts, that she might judge of their merits and make suggestions. She herself overlooked every detail, and seemed most interested and anxious to have everything successful.

===Chinese===
我原知此等节庆期间断不能作画，便打算回到美国公使馆（United States Legation）去。做梦也不曾想到，自己竟蒙恩获邀参与这场庆典——此等恩遇，向来为外国人所不得见。万寿正日之前一周，有一日随皇太后（Her Majesty）出外散步，太后将我唤至身边，说下周便是皇上的万寿节，遂留我在宫中一同参与这些庆典。蒙此恩眷，我自然喜出望外，能借此一睹中国此等典礼上那东方气派的盛况与排场，心中不胜雀跃。

届时将有盛大的戏剧演出、绚烂的烟火与陈设，以及种种仪仗。宫中的御用戏班已开始排演专为这场庆典所撰的诗赋与戏文。太监们川流不息地将布置陈设的工匠画师所做出来的样子呈给皇太后御览——这些人正依太后的心意，办理各种特定的景致与陈设；也有的来请进一步的懿旨。那些作新词的词臣，也将文稿呈进来，由太后品评其优劣高下，加以指授。太后事事亲验，样样过目，兴致极浓，唯愿一切尽善尽美。

===Original===
The festivities began four days before the Birthday with gala performances at the Theater. Each day the decorations of the buildings, the courts, and gardens increased in beauty. In the principal courts, magnificent bronzes, all sorts of antique instruments of music, used only on these great occasions, were brought out as decorations; for music forms part of every ceremonial, official or religious, in China. Among the curious instruments were splendid bronze frames, with several superposed octaves of triangular musical-stones suspended therefrom; elaborately carved supports for different-toned bells; huge “triangles”; immense bronze tam-tams, curiously and beautifully wrought; big drums on splendid bronze stands; wonderfully chased bells; and many other quaint instruments, used only for official and state processions in honor of Their Celestial Majesties.

The slanting and projecting, upturned roofs of the different buildings forming the Palaces were decorated with scarfs of varicolored silk, knotted into a curious sort of fringe of rosettes, about two feet long; yellow, the Imperial color, and red, the festive color, predominating, but other colors were introduced into the color-scheme to accentuate these.

The large Square in front of the Imperial gateway, outside the Precincts, was filled with huge, tent-like, yellow satin umbrellas, with deep curtains around the edge. These umbrellas are used for all great festivities in China, and are generally of red. Those for the Emperor’s Birthday were, of course, of the Imperial yellow, and were richly embroidered with emblematic designs. Presents for the Emperor were arriving daily from all parts of the Great Empire, and though everything was directed by splendid system the commotion was nevertheless great.

===Chinese===
庆典自万寿正日的前四天便已开始，当日戏台上先有一场盛大的演出。此后每过一日，殿宇、庭院、园苑中的陈设便愈添几分华美。主要庭院里，那些宏大的铜器、种种唯有此等大典方才动用的古乐器，都搬出来充作陈设——盖因在中国，无论官家还是庙堂的典礼，都离不开音乐。那些奇特的乐器之中，有华美的铜架，架上层层悬挂着数个八度的三角编磬（musical-stones）；有精雕细刻的钟虡，承着音调各异的编钟；有巨大的「三角」；有硕大的铜锣，铸工奇巧而华美；有立于华美铜架上的大鼓；有雕镂精绝的编钟；还有许多别致的古乐器——皆是唯有为天家两位陛下（Their Celestial Majesties）所行的大典仪仗中方才一用的。

宫中各处殿宇那倾斜上挑、飞檐翘角的屋顶，都用彩绸结成的长带装饰起来，绸带绾作一种奇特的玫瑰花结般的穗子，约有两尺来长；作为御色的黄、作为喜色的红，两色为多，其余诸色则点缀其间，以衬托这两种主色。

宫门外那片开阔的广场上，满满地张着一顶顶帐篷似的大黄缎伞盖，伞沿四周垂着深深的帷幔。此等伞盖，中国凡有大庆典时皆要张设，寻常多用红色。皇上万寿节所用的，自然是御用的明黄色，上面满绣着种种图样，华美非凡。各省进贡给皇上的礼物，每日自大帝国的四面八方络绎运到；虽有完善的章程调度一切，然而人马杂沓，场面仍极喧嚣。

===Original===
Finally, there was the first gala performance at the Theater. Her Majesty occupied her loge nearly all day, overlooking every detail, sending now and then to the stage one of her eunuchs to transmit her Imperial commands as to the speaking of certain lines or the using of certain postures. On the day of this gala performance she invited all the Ladies of the Palace to lunch, for the first time since I had been there, in the court of the Theater. Her Majesty lunched in the Imperial loge, and then ordered our repast to be served in the court, where tables were laid and served with all the pomp and ceremony that characterized the meals at the Palace. Even this alfresco entertainment was ceremonious.

===Chinese===
终于，戏台上举行了头一场盛大演出。那一日，皇太后几乎整日都坐在她的看阁里，事事过目，时时留心；不时差一名太监到台上去，传她关于某一句念白、某一种身段的懿旨。也就在这头一场盛典之日，太后把宫中一应女眷都请到戏台的庭院里来用午餐——这是我入宫以来破天荒头一遭。皇太后自己在御用看阁里用膳，吩咐将我们的膳食摆在庭院之中：桌上铺陈齐整，照着宫中用膳时那般仪注排场伺候上来。即便是这等露天的款待，礼数也照样一丝不苟。

===Original===
Most of the large courts of the Summer Palace have roofs of matting erected over them, to keep out the sun. These mat-roofs make, of the flower-filled courts, delightfully cool, outdoor parlors. The mat-sheds at the Palace are almost works of art. Tall poles, reaching from twenty to thirty feet above the roofs surrounding the courts to be protected from the sun, are painted in festive designs, and they support transversal beams, also gaily painted. Over these roof-beams are stretched strips of the beautiful matting which the Chinese excel in making. Matting-curtains drop from the roof of the sheds to a level with the Palace roofs. These side-curtains, as well as huge sections of the matting-roof, are movable, and may be opened and raised by means of cords and pulleys attached to the supporting pillars. The whole structure, supporting pillars and transversal beams, is tied together with ropes the same color of the beams, and not a nail is used. The mat-sheds are put up in June and taken down in September.

===Chinese===
颐和园（Summer Palace）里多数大型庭院，都搭着席棚遮阳。这些席顶之下，繁花满庭的院落便化作清凉宜人的露天客堂。宫中的席棚，搭得几乎可称得上是一件艺术品。为要遮阳，那院落四周殿宇的屋脊之上，竖起一根根高大的竿子，高出屋脊二三十英尺，竿上绘着喜庆的花样；竿上架起横梁，梁上也绘得花花绿绿。梁上铺着一张一张美丽的席子——这席子是中国人最擅长的手艺。席棚四围自棚顶垂下席帘，直垂到与宫中殿宇的屋檐相平。这些侧帘以及大片大片的席顶，都可以活动，借着连在立柱上的绳索与滑轮，可卷可掀。整座棚子，立柱与横梁一概以与梁同色的绳索绑扎而成，不用一颗钉子。这种席棚，六月里搭起，九月里拆去。

===Original===
New ladies were arriving at the Palace every day for a week before the Birthday﻿—members of the Imperial Family from a distance, and the wives and daughters of Manchu nobles who were of sufficient rank to present their congratulations in person. The young Empress never failed to introduce me to these ladies. A foreigner in the Chinese Court is a much more extraordinary circumstance than a Chinese at a European Court would be, and this was, in most instances, the first meeting of these Princesses with any foreigner; but they were uniformly courteous and even cordial, never evincing the slightest curiosity as to my dress or my habits. I doubted whether a Chinese at a European Court, or at our White House, would have been treated with the same consideration by all, even to the servants. The children, of whom there were several at Court at this time, were as well-bred as their elders in their treatment of the “foreign lady.”

===Chinese===
万寿正日之前的一周里，宫中每日都有新到的女眷——远道而来的宗室福晋格格，以及品级够得上亲自入宫祝寿的满族（Manchu）王公贵胄的妻女。皇后（the Young Empress）从不曾疏漏，每每将我引见于这些夫人。一个外国人置身中国宫廷，远比一个中国人现身欧洲宫廷更为罕见；而对这里大多数福晋格格而言，这也是她们头一遭见到外国人。然而她们待我，无不彬彬有礼，甚至殷勤亲切，对我的衣着与习惯，从未流露过一丝一毫的好奇。我倒有些疑心：若是一个中国人置身欧洲宫廷，或到了我们的白宫（White House），未必能这般处处受到上上下下、连仆从都无不周到的体恤。其时宫中还有几位小辈孩子，他们对待这位「洋夫人」，也与其长辈一般彬彬有礼。

===Original===
After our first lunch in the court of the Theater, when the theatrical performance of the day was finished and the actors had left, I approached the stage of the Theater and began examining, with interest, its construction and appointments. The Palace Theater is raised about twelve feet from the ground, and its main floor is on a level with the Imperial loge. The building consists of three stories and a cellar. The latter is used for the few pieces of scenery of the scenic plays, and is where the simple devices used for moving it are manipulated. Like the Greek theater, the stage is open on three sides; and the actors come out and speak their parts, their entrance being to the left and the exit to the right of the stage.

Her Majesty was within her loge while I was examining the construction of the Theater; but she evidently noticed my movements, for the eunuchs soon threw open the great plate doors and she descended the steps of the Imperial loge and came across the court to where I was standing. She asked me if I would not like to go on the stage and look over the building and examine things thoroughly. She added, “You probably may never have such a chance to see a good Chinese theater again.” She, herself, went up the steps leading from the court to the stage, and told me to follow her.

===Chinese===
那日在戏台庭院里的头一遭午餐既毕，当日演戏也告竣、伶人皆散，我便走到戏台跟前，兴致勃勃地端详起它的构造与陈设来。宫中的戏台高出地面约十二英尺，台面与御用看阁相平。整座建筑上下三层，外加一处地窨子。地窨子是用来安放少数几出布景戏的景片的，搬移景片的简单机关也在此处操动。戏台与希腊剧场相类，三面凌空；伶人们从台左上场，到台前念唱做打，再从台右下场。

我端详戏台之时，皇太后正在她的看阁里；她显然留意到了我的举动，因为不多时，太监们便将那一扇扇厚玻璃门推开，太后从御用看阁的台阶上下来，穿过庭院，走到我站立之处。她问我可愿上台去看一看、将这座戏楼细细端详一番？又道：「这般好机缘，能见识一座像样的中国戏台，你日后恐怕再难遇到了。」她亲自从庭院登上通往戏台的台阶，叫我随她上去。

===Original===
The stage is about twenty-five feet square, is roofed over, and projects into the court, its three sides being open. The fourth side has doors and curtains for the entrance and exit of the actors. There are no actresses in China. The men perform the parts of women, and represent them with such success that I was much surprised when I learned there were no actresses. At the back of the stage sit the musicians, who accompany all the theatrical performances in China.

===Chinese===
戏台约有二十五英尺见方，上有顶盖，向庭院中凸出，三面凌空。第四面则设门设帘，为伶人上场下场之处。中国本无女伶。男子扮演女子，描摹之妙，竟使我初闻台上无女伶时大为惊异。台后坐着乐工，中国戏台上的演奏，皆由此辈承应。

===Original===
Her Majesty, herself, led the way across the stage and we went behind the scenes. Here, I examined closely a number of “Floats” that were to be used, in the procession in honor of the Emperor, on the day of the Birthday. These floats had all been designed by the Empress Dowager. After we had looked at these, she suggested that I had better see the upper floors. These latter are not in general use in Chinese theaters. The theaters, even at the other Palaces, have but one stage. The steps which lead to the second stage, and thence to the third stage, are behind the scenes. The two upper stages are used for spectacular plays and tableaux, when certain of the players group themselves in pyramidal form on these superposed stages and speak their lines therefrom. The upper stages have also trapdoors and pulleys for use in the spectacular plays. Her Majesty went up, herself, to show me these stages.
