When famine devastates the land, when drought or any other National Calamity is visited upon the Empire, the Emperor prays in these Temples for its cessation, for he is not only the High Priest, but as “Son of Heaven” is the expiator for the afflictions visited upon his people by Heaven, and he publicly holds himself responsible for the misfortunes of the Empire. According to the Book of Rites, he says in time of trouble, “I will purify myself by sacrifice that these calamities may be lifted from the Empire and the people. I alone am responsible.”

===Chinese===
皇上不仅是那唯一配在大坛之上向无形之上帝献祭的大祭司，整个自然崇拜的神职体系，也都寄于他圣躬之一身。唯独他，于《礼记》（Book of Rites）所定的时日、以及特殊的场合，在天坛及其他诸大坛庙中献祭。当饥荒遍野、当旱魃或任何其他国之大灾降于帝国之上，皇上便于此诸坛庙之中祈祷灾祸止息；盖因他不仅为大祭司，更以「天子」之身，为上天降于其子民的灾厄承当赎罪之责，并公开自承对帝国种种不幸负责。据《礼记》所载，逢国有灾厄之时，他如此说道：「我当以献祭洁净己身，使此等灾厄从帝国与百姓身上除去。其责惟我一人承担。」

===Original===
The great semiannual celebration to the Invisible Deity is not only the most solemn of the religious rites the Emperor performs; it is at the same time the most formal of his official acts as ruler of the Great Empire. He prepares himself, by fasting and subduing the body, for the religious rite; for the official ceremony as Emperor of China, he is accompanied by all his ministers and the highest nobles of the land and surrounded by splendid pomp and Imperial pageantry.

===Chinese===
每年两次对无形之皇天上帝所行的大祭，不仅是皇上所行诸般宗教祭典中最为庄严肃穆者，同时也是他作为大帝国之君所行的最为隆重的官方典礼。为这宗教祭典，他以斋戒和克己修身来自我预备；至于作为中国皇上的官方典礼，则由全体大臣与国中最高贵的王公贵族扈从，前后左右尽是堂皇的盛况与皇家的仪仗。

===Original===
Though in his triple quality of Emperor, High Priest, and Expiator, he personally sacrifices only in the great Temples to Nature, the Emperor has all the religions of his Empire under his protection and is their nominal Head. He assists indiscriminately at Buddhist or Taoist ceremonies, and encourages with impartiality both cults. But these religions have priestly hierarchies, and complicated Rituals, and the Emperor is only the Ex-officio Head and High Priest. All the festivals and fasts of both are celebrated in the Palace.

===Chinese===
皇上虽以皇帝、大祭司、承罪者这三重身份，仅在祭自然诸大坛庙中亲自献祭，但帝国之内的所有宗教，都置于他的庇护之下，而他为名义上的首领。他无分彼此地出席佛教或道教的仪式，对两教一视同仁地予以扶持。但这两种宗教自有其神职体系与繁复的仪轨，皇上不过是因职而兼任的首领与大祭司罢了。两教一切节庆与斋期，皆在宫中举行。

===Original===
The Chinese are not a religious people, though so moral a race. They are rather followers of a philosophy than members of religious bodies. The two most popular religions of China, Buddhism and Taoism, have become more or less outward forms. They are empty shells which may once have contained the Spirit, but have now become mere conventional representations of ancient rites. The Chinese people are really Confucians, and Confucianism is a system of ethics, a philosophy rather than a religion. Whether they be Buddhists or Taoists, they are all followers of Confucius, and live by the rules the Great Sage has laid down for them. The doctrines of Buddha and Laotze have become so encrusted with error in China as to afford no moral or ideal help to their followers. The Chinese participate indiscriminately in either of these religious rites, many of which have become mere outward spectacular ceremonies, where there is a great deal of display and much form, but very little real worship. They get all their moral support from the writings of Confucius and all their ideals from communion with Nature. They are really philosophers and worshipers of Nature, and the Emperor’s semiannual sacrifice on the altar of Heaven and those at the Temples of Nature typify the real worship of the people.

===Chinese===
中国人虽是如此重道德的民族，却并非一个宗教性的民族。与其说他们是宗教团体的成员，不如说是一种哲学的信徒。中国最盛行的两大宗教——佛教与道教——多已沦为外在的形式。它们是空壳，或许曾盛装过灵性，如今却不过成了古老祭典的惯常摆设。中国人实在是儒者，而儒学是一套伦理体系，与其说是宗教，不如说是哲学。无论信佛信道，他们都是孔子的门徒，都遵这位至圣先师为他们所立下的规矩而行。佛祖与老子的学说，在中国已为谬误所重重包裹，再不能给予其信众任何道德上或理想上的助益。中国人不分彼此地参与这些宗教祭典中的任何一种，其中许多早已沦为徒有其表的盛大仪仗——场面极大，礼数极繁，唯独少有真正的虔敬。他们的道德支柱，皆得自孔子的著述；他们的理想，皆得自与自然的契合。他们实在是哲学家，是自然的崇拜者；而皇上岁中两次在天坛之上、以及在祭自然诸坛上所行的献祭，正象征着百姓真正的崇拜。

===Original===
All the religious rites in China have their origin in, or are in celebration of, some natural phenomenon or some periodical event in Nature. They celebrate the summer and winter solstices, the equinoxes, the New Year, the awakening of spring, when the sap (life-giving element) begins to mount. The Harvest Moon is the time of the going to rest of this life-giving element. Their complicated ceremonial is but the crystallization of some simple observance of Nature’s fundamental laws. This ceremonial has been kept alive all these centuries, because of the vivifying spark of Nature which enkindled them. These rites are now observed without a thought of their origin, but Nature still remains their creative force. In spite of their conventions, the Chinese have kept very near to Nature, and I believe this is the secret of their wonderful vitality. They have been overrun and conquered by many different races, and their assimilation of these conquerors is one of the most astonishing things in the ethnic study of this wonderful race.

===Chinese===
中国所有的宗教祭祀，无不源于、或为纪念某种自然现象、某种自然界中周期性的事件。他们庆贺夏至与冬至、春分与秋分、新年、春之苏醒——是时，生气（即赋予生命之物）开始上升。丰收月，则是这赋予生机之物去安息的时节。他们繁复的仪注，不过是对自然根本法则某种简单奉行方式的结晶。这套仪注之所以历数百年而不衰，全赖最初点燃它们的、那一缕自然的生机之火。如今人们奉行这些祭典时，已不再思及其由来，然而自然仍是其创造的源泉。中国人虽为种种成规所囿，却始终与自然相去不远；我相信，这正是他们那种惊人活力之奥秘所在。他们曾为许多不同的民族所侵入、所征服，而他们将这些征服者一一同化，在研究这个奇特民族的人种学上，乃是最令人惊异的现象之一。

===Original===
No conquering race has ever changed the Chinese. Tartars, Mongols, Manchus have all passed and become amalgamated with them. Their conquerors have adopted the Chinese philosophy and religion, their customs and habits, and even their system of government. And they have never been able to impose any really new system of government upon the Chinese.

===Chinese===
从无任何一个征服民族曾改变过中国人。鞑靼、蒙古、满族（Manchus），都一一经过，并与他们融为一体。那些征服者反倒采纳了中国的哲学与宗教、中国的风俗与习惯，甚至中国的政体。他们却从未能将任何真正全新的政体强加于中国人之上。

===Original===
These founders of Dynasties in China have all been “warriors bold” and reckless marauders with little philosophy and no literature to speak of. When the Manchus, the last of these conquerors, founders of the present Dynasty, established themselves in Peking, in 1646, they were a wild and warlike race. They, like all the other conquerors of China, conciliated their vanquished foes by all sorts of concessions, and they now rule by Chinese laws, and today are hardly distinguishable from the Chinese. They have never made, any more than the other conquerors, the slightest impress upon this calm and passive race; and they have become Chinese. “Today the Emperor Kwang-Hsu is recognized as one of the best Chinamen in China.” The Manchu men today wear Chinese dress. The Emperor, himself, shaves his head and wears the queue, the one visible sign of degradation that is said to have been imposed upon the Chinese at the time of the conquest. Oh! Irony of fate!

===Chinese===
这些在中国的开朝之主，无一不是「勇猛的武夫」，是肆无忌惮的劫掠者，谈不上有什么哲学，更无文学可言。当满族——这些征服者中的最后一族，亦即当今这一朝的开基之主——于 1646 年在北京立足之时，他们还是一个野蛮好战的民族。他们一如所有其他征服中国之民族，以种种让步来安抚手下败将；如今他们按中国的法律来治理，到了今日，几乎已与中国人无从分辨。他们与别的征服者一样，从未在这个沉静而柔顺的民族身上留下丝毫印记；他们已变成了中国人。「如今皇上（光绪帝）已被公认为中国最优秀的中国人之一。」如今满族男人都穿中国衣裳。皇上自己也剃发留辫——那是在当初征服之时，据说强加于中国人的唯一可见的屈辱标志。噢！命运何其嘲弄！

===Original===
The Manchus are now as quiet and peaceful a race as the Chinese themselves. They dread war. They live by the laws of Confucius. Though not a race of thinkers or philosophers, they have come to have the same ideals as the Chinese, and this, without the natural amalgamation brought about by intermarriage, for it is only within the last few years that the Throne has issued an edict, allowing intermarriage between the Chinese and Manchu, and even, with this edict, up to this time there has been very little mixture, by marriage, of the races. The Chinese seem easily led and conquered, but their national vitality is very vigorous, and has kept them pure in racial characteristics after their thousands of years of national existence.

===Chinese===
如今满族人和中国人一样，是个沉静而爱好和平的民族。他们畏惧战争，按孔子的准则度日。虽然他们并非一个思想家或哲学家的民族，却渐渐有了与中国人相同的理想；而且这种趋同并非出于通婚所带来的自然融合——因为朝廷允许满汉通婚的诏书，不过是近几年内才颁发的；即便有了这道诏书，时至今日，两族借婚姻而造成的混血仍极少。中国人看似易于引导、易于征服，但其民族元气极为旺盛，在数千年的国族存在之后，仍使其种族特征保持纯粹。

===Original===
Altar to the Invisible Deity
In the Park of the Temple of Heaven

===Chinese===
皇天上帝之坛
天坛园苑之内

===Original===
The Festival of the Harvest Moon, which typifies the season when the life-giving element in Nature goes to rest for the Winter, I have already described. It is intermixed with legends and practices that destroy its original meaning; but the ceremony to the awakening of Spring has not departed from its original intention, and is simpler and nearer Nature. The awakening of Spring, the day when the sap is supposed to stir from its long sleep and to feel the first throes of renewed life, is commemorated in a pretty, homely ceremony at the Palace. The radish and young shoots of lettuce, the first vegetables to receive the benefit of the rising sap, are presented on a silver salver to Her Majesty by a kneeling eunuch. She partakes of them, and then gives them to the young Empress and Ladies to taste of. When Her Majesty raises the first radish to her lips, the young Empress, Princesses, and Ladies assembled in her Throne-room, repeat the wish for Imperial happiness, synonymous with “National prosperity.” This wish is echoed by the high attendants in the antechamber, and reechoed by the eunuchs kneeling in the courts without, and still echoed and reechoed by every inmate of the Palace, until the waves of sound reach to the outer walls. Then Her Majesty makes a wish that the sap may rise in such abundance as to produce a fruitful season, that all the people of the Great Empire may enjoy peace and plenty.

===Chinese===
丰收月之节（Festival of the Harvest Moon），象征着自然界中那赋予生机之物入冬安息的时令，我已于前文记述过了。它与种种传说和习俗相掺杂，已使其本意丧失；但迎春的祭典却未曾背离其初意，更为简朴，也更贴近自然。立春之日，视为生气自长眠中苏醒、感受到新生之初阵悸动的时辰，宫中以一种别致而素朴的小仪来纪念。萝卜与生菜的嫩苗，乃是最先受到上升生气之惠的菜蔬，由一名跪着的太监（eunuch）盛在银盘之上，呈给皇太后（Her Majesty）。太后尝过，再赐与皇后（the Young Empress）与诸位女官尝食。当皇太后将头一片萝卜送到唇边时，聚集在宝座殿中的皇后、福晋格格与女官们，便齐声祝颂皇家安康——这与「国泰民安」本是同义。这祝颂之声在前厅的高级侍从间回响，又在院中跪着的太监们口中再起回响，如此一应一答，由宫中每一个人层层传响，直到声浪传至外墙之下。然后皇太后许下一愿：愿此一年生气勃发，以致五谷丰登，愿大帝国的全体子民，皆得享太平与丰足。

===Original===
Thus are these first fruits of the awakening Spring partaken of with a simple ceremony of praise and thanksgiving. Thus are these homely plants consecrated with wishes made for the good of the country and the happiness of its rulers. It was to me a beautiful ceremony, so simple that it brought these people with all their conventions and all their forms very near the heart itself of Nature.

===Chinese===
春醒时的这初熟之物，便是这样以一场简朴的赞美与感恩之仪来品尝的。这些朴素的菜蔬，便是这样在为国之福祉、为君上之安乐的祝愿之中得以祝圣。在我看来，这是一场极美的典礼，简朴得竟使这些人——纵然为种种成规与礼数所裹挟——直直地贴近了自然本身的心脏。

===Original===
The annual plowing of a furrow and sowing of the first seeds of the year by the Emperor, the planting of a mulberry tree (to nourish the silkworms) by the Empress, are other touches of Nature which show how near the Chinese are to the heart of things. One of the honorary offices which is considered a great mark of Imperial favor, and which the highest ladies of the land receive with reverent gratitude, is to be appointed “Guardian of the Cocoons”; for the silk industry is one of the great sources of National prosperity in China. These ladies of high degree, guardians of the cocoons, go in annual pilgrimage to the mulberry groves, where the cocoons flourish, to make sacrifices and prayers for the health and growth of the cocoon. Being so near to Nature, the Chinese are naturally a pastoral people, a race of agriculturists; and agriculture, being thus honored by the Sacred Persons of Their Majesties, becomes a lofty ideal. Labor, which the Emperor publicly performs, loses all taint and grows into an Inspiration.

===Chinese===
皇上每年亲犁一垄、亲播一年之第一种，皇后亲植一株桑树（以饲养蚕儿），亦是另一些自然的笔触，可见中国人何其贴近万物之本。其中有一项被视为皇恩隆重之标志的荣誉差事，国中最尊贵的夫人们无不怀着感戴之心来承领——便是被指为「蚕茧之守护」（Guardian of the Cocoons）；盖因蚕桑之业乃中国国富民强的一大源泉。这些贵妇——蚕茧的守护者们——每年都要往桑林茂盛、蚕茧丰美之处作一次朝香，在那里为蚕茧的康健与生长献祭祈祷。中国人既如此贴近自然，自然便是一个田园之族，一个农耕之民；而农事既蒙二位陛下（Their Majesties）圣躬如此尊崇，便成了一种崇高的理想。劳作，因有皇上亲自行之于众，便洗尽了一切低贱之嫌，升华为一种启迪。

## XII Return to the Summer Palace / 第十二章 重返颐和园

===Original===
The next day the Court returned to the Summer Palace. The festivities and sacrifices in connection with the Emperor's Birthday being now over and the Court settled down to its usual routine, I hoped I might be allowed to go regularly to work on the portrait, and that Her Majesty would allow me to paint when she was not posing. There was much I could do between times, and she could pose but for a short time each day. Up to that time, Her Majesty had treated me as a guest at Court, whose amusement was the most important thing to be looked after. She seemed much interested in the work, but my painting was an incident and even the "Sacred Picture" a secondary consideration. All these walks, these delightful excursions, were perfectly charming, and, had I gone to the Palace to enjoy myself, or to study Her Majesty and Chinese manners and customs, I would have been perfectly satisfied. I had, in the Empress Dowager, a psychological study full of ever varying and constant interest. I was living through a unique experience, seeing what I could never hope to see again, but I was not allowed to paint on the portrait as much as I should have liked. Could I but have had permission to work more, I should have been very happy. Had I been able to speak Chinese well enough, I felt I would obtain what I desired; she had shown herself so uniformly kind. She probably felt I was enjoying myself more in this way than working at my painting.

===Chinese===
第二天，朝廷回到了颐和园。皇上寿辰相关的一系列庆典和祭祀既已结束，宫中也恢复了日常的起居程式，我便希望能获准按时继续画像工作，也希望皇太后允许我在她不坐班时继续作画。两班之间的空隙里我能做许多事，而她每日能坐班的时间却很短。在此之前，皇太后一直把我当作宫中贵客款待，让我开心解闷是头等要事。她似乎对这幅画像很感兴趣，但我的作画不过宫中的一桩插曲，就连「圣像」本身也只是次要之事。所有这些散步、这些令人愉悦的出游，都雅致迷人至极——倘若我入宫是为寻欢取乐，或为研究皇太后与中国的风俗人情，我本应心满意足。在皇太后身上，我得到了一个永远变化、永远新鲜、永远有趣的心理学研究题目。我正经历着一段独一无二的时光，所见之景此生恐难再睹，可惜我不能如愿尽情地为画像挥毫。倘若能获准多加工作，我便十分欢喜了。我心想，若是中文能说得再流利些，定能求得我所愿；她待我一向如此体贴周到。她也许觉得，这样游乐消遣比埋头作画更能让我快活吧。

===Original===
While I thoroughly enjoyed the promenades with Her Majesty, I loved the daily sittings. Every portrait painter knows the sort of intimacy that establishes itself between him and his sitter, however unsympathetic the latter may be at first sight, which was certainly not the case in this instance. The effort of the painter to get under the exterior and discover the real person of his sitter; the desire to see the best side and make the most of it, meets generally with a sympathetic response. If the rapport is properly established, they get to know each other better by the time the portrait is finished than they could otherwise have done, perhaps in years. Though I saw Her Majesty so intimately at other times, I felt I was not seeing her "face to face" (figuratively speaking), except at the sittings.

===Chinese===
与皇太后一同散步固然令我十分愉快，但我最爱的还是每日的坐班。每个肖像画家都明白，画家与坐班者之间会自然生出一种亲密之感——纵然初见时彼此并无好感（这种情形在此自然不适用）。画家竭力穿透外表、探寻坐班者真实的一面；他渴望发现对方最美的一面并尽量加以呈现——这般种种用心，通常都能换来对方善意的回应。若这种默契能够恰当地建立起来，待画像告成之时，他们彼此相知之深，恐怕胜过寻常交往数载。虽然在其他场合我也能如此亲近地见到皇太后，但我总觉得，除了坐班之时，我并未真正（喻义而言）与她「面对面」。

## On the Road from Peking to the Summer Palace / 自北京至颐和园之路

===Original===
The morning after our return to the Summer Palace, my easel was again placed in the Throne-room. The portrait was taken down from its resting-place and work resumed. Her Majesty gave me a long sitting, and the portrait made a step ahead. If I had only had a place to work alone, where I might study the picture, when she was not posing, I could have made so many improvements! But I was obliged to possess my soul in patience, and work along for the short space of an hour or so a day and stop the moment Her Majesty felt fatigued, when my brushes and palette were whisked away, as if by magic. There was no chance to study the portrait or to do anything, except when the Empress Dowager and the crowd of attendants were present.

===Chinese===
回到颐和园的第二天早晨，我的画架又被安放在了宝座间。画像从存放之处取下，工作重新开始。皇太后给我坐了颇长一段时间的班，画像又向前推进了一步。若能有一处我可以独自工作的地方，让我在她不坐班时仔细揣摩这幅画像，我本可以做多少改进啊！可我不得不按捺住性子，每日仅在那短短一小时左右的时间里作画，皇太后一觉疲倦便立刻停下，我的画笔和调色板也随即如变戏法一般被收走。除非皇太后和那一群侍从在场，我根本没有机会独自研究画像或做任何事情。

===Original===
I had taken to the Palace only a small folding easel, which was not at all suitable for regular work on so large a portrait, but it was impossible to get a better one in Peking. Her Majesty, who observed everything, noticed that it was not convenient, and suggested that I draw a design for a large easel and give it to the Palace carpenters to copy. She thought they would be able to make me one. I did so, and they made me a very satisfactory working easel. When the eunuchs found that this Palace easel suited me, five others of different sizes were made. I asked for what reason, and was told that everything for Her Majesty was made in sixes. It would have been establishing a precedent, making an innovation, to have fewer than six easels for her portrait.

===Chinese===
我带到宫中的只是一架小型折叠画架，根本不适合用于这么大一幅画像的日常绘制，但在北京又寻不到更好的。皇太后什么都看在眼里，察觉到这架画架用着不便，便建议我画一张大画架的图样，交给宫中的木匠照样打造。她觉得他们定能为我做出一架来。我便照办，他们果然做出了一架十分合用的工作画架。太监们见这架宫制画架合我用，便又另造了五架大小各异的。我问这是何故，答称：凡是皇太后御用之物，皆须成六件。为皇太后的圣像备办画架若少于六件，那便是破了规矩、改了祖制了。

===Original===
Her Majesty also ordered some large flat boxes, with lock and key, to be made for my materials. These boxes were covered in yellow, for they were to be used for the Sacred Picture, and must be in the Imperial color. I forgot to say the six easels had all been stained a bright yellow! A table, surmounted by one of these yellow boxes, occupied a prominent place in the Throne-room during the whole time this portrait was being painted. When I finished painting each day, the Chief Eunuch, himself, removed the picture from the easel, and a number of others came and took my brushes and palette, put away the easel and closed the yellow box and locked it. Our head eunuch carried the key to the box.

===Chinese===
皇太后还命人做了几只带锁的大扁箱子，专门用来存放我的画具。这些箱子外都包着黄绫，因为它们是用来盛放圣像的，必须用皇家御用之色。我倒忘了说，那六架画架也都染成了明黄色！画像绘制的整个期间，宝座间里总摆着一张桌子，桌上一只这样的黄箱格外显眼。每日作画完毕，总管太监亲自将画像从画架上取下，又有几名太监上前收去我的画笔和调色板，把画架收好，再合上黄箱落锁。这箱子的钥匙则由我们这位首领太监掌管。

===Original===
When the afternoon sitting was finished, we went out for another of those delightful promenades around the grounds. The days were now growing visibly shorter, and the evenings were beginning to be cool. As we went through the gardens, Her Majesty stopped at all her favorite points and looked for a few moments at the view, as if to greet it again, after her absence. She loved the Summer Palace and it always seemed a pleasure to her to return to it. We had tea in one of the teahouses where there were tables and seats. She ordered the eunuchs to make a sort of blancmange of lotus-root flour, which was delicious, and, as she said, most wholesome. When the Empress Dowager goes for a walk, portable stoves and all the paraphernalia necessary for cooking a light repast are taken along. It seemed wonderful to me to see the way the Chinese could cook, with apparently so few conveniences. After this we had tea. The finest tea in China is sent to the Palace. The first leaves of the plantations all over the Great Empire are reserved for Their Majesties. Her Majesty, who is a great epicure, has her choice of these chosen leaves. She adds to the delicacy of its already fine flavor by putting into her teacup the blooms of dried honeysuckle, the flowers of jasmine, or other fragrant blooms. The honey from these flowers slightly sweetens the tea, besides giving it a delicate, subtle flavor, quite unique. These dried blooms are brought in a jade bowl, with two long cherry sticks, with which Her Majesty takes the flowers and places them in her cup, stirring them into the tea with these graceful wands. The Chinese never use a teaspoon. Her Majesty drinks her tea from a jade cup, which is placed in a curiously fashioned, cunningly wrought, openwork, silver saucer. The Chinese take their tea boiling hot, and the jade does not get so hot as a porcelain cup.

===Chinese===
下午的坐班结束后，我们便又到园子里作那种令人愉快的散步。此时白昼已明显变短，傍晚开始有了凉意。穿过花园时，皇太后每到她素来钟爱的几处景点，都要驻足片刻，眺望一番景色，仿佛是离别之后重新向它们致意。她深爱颐和园，每次归来都显得喜形于色。我们在一处备有桌椅的茶亭里用茶。她吩咐太监们用藕粉做了一种类似牛奶冻的点心，十分可口，照她说还极养人。皇太后每次出门散步，随行的都有便携的炉子以及烹制一顿简便茶点所需的全套家什。中国人能在那样看似简陋的条件下做出种种食物，实在令我叹服。此后我们便饮茶。全中国最上等的茶叶都送入宫中，大清帝国各处茶园的头茬嫩叶，都专门留供皇上和皇太后。皇太后深谙饮食之道，从这些精选茶叶中再挑出合她意的来。她又在茶杯里放入干金银花、茉莉花或其他芬芳花朵，为茶汤本已清雅的滋味更添一层精致。花中所含的蜜意使茶味微微发甜，又赋予茶汤一种清幽细腻、难以言传的风味。这些干花盛在一只玉碗里，旁置两根长长的樱桃木小棍，皇太后便用这两根优雅的小棍拈起花朵放入杯中，将花与茶一同搅动。中国人从不使用茶匙。皇太后用一只玉杯饮茶，玉杯搁在一只雕工奇巧、镂空錾花的银碟之中。中国人饮茶都喜滚烫，而玉杯不似瓷杯那般烫手。

===Original===
We continued our walk through the gardens after leaving the teahouse, and when we were passing a bed of flowers Her Majesty spied some curious grass, which she ordered the eunuchs to gather. When it was brought to her she deftly wove several blades of it into a perfectly recognizable representation of a rabbit. She did it so quickly I did not realize she was trying to make anything until she tossed the finished result over to me and asked me what I thought it was. It was unmistakable.

===Chinese===
离开茶亭后，我们继续在园中漫步。正走过一片花畦时，皇太后瞥见了几茎奇特的草，便命太监们采来。草取到她手中，只见她手指灵巧地把几片草叶编成了一只一看便知的兔子。她做得极快，我起初竟未察觉她在做什么，直到她把编好的成品抛给我，问我看出是什么。那兔子的模样一丝不差。

===Original===
When we reached our objective point, one of the highest eminences in the grounds, with the whole panorama of the Western Hills spread out beneath us, and the setting sun glowing over all in brilliant splendor, it was a glorious scene. She called me up to her side and made a graceful, sweeping gesture of the hand that said, "This is all mine, but you may share it with me." She had that sense of possession of nature's beauties which all artistic souls feel, for their appreciation makes what they view their own. She felt it was hers, because she loved it so, and she knew I would appreciate it, which few of her entourage did, as none of them were such passionate lovers of nature as the Empress Dowager, and custom had dulled their perception of the beauty of the scene. The exquisite pleasure the contemplation of this glorious view gave me, made me tremble with delight. As the day was fading and as I was thinly clad, Her Majesty thought I was cold, and, seeing I had no wrap, she called to the Chief Eunuch to bring me one of hers. He selected one from the number that were always brought along for these promenades, and gave it to Her Majesty, who threw it over my shoulders. She asked me to keep it and to try to remember to take better care of myself in the future.

===Chinese===
待我们终于走到目的地——园中最高的一处山岗，脚下西山全貌尽收眼底，夕阳的光辉将万物染成一片辉煌灿烂，景象壮丽非凡。她把我唤到身旁，优雅地挥了挥手，那姿态分明在说：「这一切都是我的，但你不妨与我共享。」凡艺术心灵都能体会到的那种对自然之美的占有之感，她也有——因为真心赏识，所观之物便仿佛归己所有。她觉得这景色是属于她的，因为她深爱着它；她也知道我会赏识此景——可她身边的随从能赏识的却寥寥无几，因为无人像皇太后这般酷爱自然，长久的习见也已使他们对此处美景的感受变得迟钝。凝望这般壮丽景色所生的精致愉悦，使我快乐得微微发抖。天色渐冥，我又穿得单薄，皇太后以为我冷了，见我未带披风，便叫总管太监取一件她自己的来给我。他从这一类散步照例随带的一堆衣物中挑了一件，呈给皇太后，皇太后便亲手将它披在我肩上。她让我把这件披风留下，又叮嘱我今后务必多加珍重自己。

## XI Some Characteristics of Her Majesty—Second Visit to the Sea Palace / 第十一章 皇太后的若干性情——二度游西苑

===Original===
I spent the next day at the Legation, and thoroughly enjoyed it, but I was glad to think that I was to spend the following day at the Palace again. The study of Her Majesty had now become to me like a thrilling novel. I could not bear to lay it down; and when I was forced to do so, I was longing to be able to resume it. She was such a delightful surprise to me. I had heard and read so much of her, before I went to the Palace, and nothing that I had heard or read had at all prepared me for the reality, so charming, so unusual was her personality. Not charming and interesting by fits and starts, but always so! She was so considerate and tactful, and seemed so really kind in her relations with those who surrounded her. I had been now nearly a month in daily contact with her. I saw her, not only when she sat for the portrait; I was with her the greater part of the day, and I began to let myself go in my admiration of her. The days seemed flat and stale when I could not see her—so full of interest and charm I found her. She was a woman of such infinite variety! There was always something new and fresh to study in her. She was the very embodiment of the Eternal Feminine. She was at once a child and a woman with strong, virile qualities. She would go into the Audience Hall, transact weighty affairs of State for three hours, and then go for her walks or excursions, and take a childish interest in the simplest pleasures. She would be seated in one of her Throne-rooms in trivial conversation with her Ladies, when an Official Despatch, in its yellow silk case, would be brought in, and presented by the eunuch on bended knees. Her face would immediately become full of serious interest; she would bend her brows and become the statesman; a few moments later, when she had duly considered, and given orders relative to the despatch, she became again the woman, full of interest in her flowers, dresses, and jewels.

===Chinese===
次日我在公使馆度过，过得十分惬意，可一想到次日又要回到宫里，我心里便觉欢喜。如今，对皇太后的端详，于我已如读一部引人入胜的小说，叫我不忍释卷；被迫搁下之时，便一心盼着能早些再续。她于我而言，是一个令人欣喜的意外。入宫之前，我听过、读过许多关于她的事，然而无论所闻所读，都未能让我对眼前这真人有半分准备——她的气度竟是如此迷人，如此不同寻常。她并非一时一处才显得动人有趣，而是时时如此！她那样体贴入微，进退有度，与左右之人的相处之中，显得那样真情实意地慈和。我此时与她朝夕相接已近一月。我不仅在画像坐班之时见她；一日里大半的时辰都与她同在，我渐渐放纵自己对她的钦慕。凡不见她的日子，便觉平淡寡味——她竟是这样一个有趣而富于魅力的人。她是一个变化无穷的女子！她身上总有某种新鲜可学之处。她是「永恒的女性」最完美的化身。她同时既是孩童，又是一个具备强悍刚健之资的女人。她会步入觐见殿，处理三小时繁重的国事，然后出宫散步或游赏，以孩童般的心性去喜爱最简单的玩乐。她会坐在某一处宝座殿里，与女官们闲话家常，这时一份装在黄绸匣中的御批奏折被送了进来，由太监跪着呈上。她面上立时现出郑重的关切；她蹙起双眉，化身为政治家；片刻之后，等她斟酌妥当、就奏折发下谕旨，她又变回那个女人，专注于她的鲜花、衣裳与珠宝。

===Original===
A distinguished Frenchman once said of Her Majesty the Empress Dowager, “*C’est le seul homme de la Chine*,” and she deserves the appellation of “man,” if it goes to mean superior intelligence and executive ability; but it was not the “statesman” that I had the best opportunity of studying. It was the woman in her private life; and I had unusual advantages for this study, and the more I saw of her, the more remarkable I found her! Her favors to the Ladies of the Court were very impartially distributed. She had her favorites, but she did not allow them to gain any supremacy over her, nor to warp her judgment. Although her entourage never expressed an opinion contrary to hers, in her presence, and though she always accepted their *expressed* views in the most courteous manner, one could see she was not imposed upon, and that she knew, perfectly well, their real opinions, so great was her natural penetration.

===Chinese===
一位显赫的法国人曾这样评价皇太后：「她是全中国唯一的男人。」倘「男人」一词意指超群的智识与果决的执行之才，那么她当之无愧；只是我最有机会端详的，并非那位「政治家」。我所研读的，是她在私生活中的女人一面；此番研读我又得了不同寻常的便利，而越是看得多，越觉她了不起！她对宫中女官的恩赏，分派得极为公允。她自有其宠眷之人，却不容她们借宠擅权，也不容她们左右自己的决断。虽然她的近侍在她跟前从不敢道出与她相左之见，又虽然她对他们所陈之见总以极其谦和之礼相接，然而一眼便看得出，她并不为人所蒙蔽——她对他们真正的想法了然于心，她的天性的洞察力，便是这般敏锐。

===Original===
I was astonished to find in what veneration the Empress Dowager was really held by the Ladies of the Court and her entourage. Her favorite title, and that by which she has been longest known to the courtiers, is, “Lao-Fo-yeh,” the “Old Buddha,” which shows that they invest her with sacred qualities. After her return from Hsi-An Fu, where the Court went when the allied troops occupied Peking, and where the sacred Persons of Her Majesty and the Emperor suffered so many hardships and endured them so bravely, the courtiers gave her another, a closer and more affectionate appellation, “Lao-Tzu-Tzung” (The Old Ancestress). This was the title by which she was called in the Palace, by the Emperor, Empress, and Princesses, and by which she allowed me to address her.

===Chinese===
我惊异地发觉，宫中女官和她的近侍对皇太后实是心怀何等深切的敬仰。她最钟爱的称号，也是朝臣们叫她最久的一个，便是「老佛爷」，足见他们已将神圣之质赋予了她。自西安府回銮之后——当日联军进占北京，宫廷西迁，皇太后与皇上的圣驾在那一路上吃了许多苦，又那样坚忍地承受下来——朝臣们又给了她另一个更亲近、更饱含深情的称呼：「老祖宗」。这是皇上、皇后、公主们在宫中对她的称呼，也是她准我用以称呼她的称呼。

===Original===
On our arrival at the Sea Palace, the day of my second visit there, after making our bows to Her Majesty, we started, in our chairs, to the Hall of the Mongolian Princes. This is a magnificent hall in the northeastern part of the park, some distance away from Her Majesty’s and the Emperor’s Palaces. It is of one story, as usual, but this nearly forty feet high. The interior is spacious, with only a few dragon tables and chairs and no ornaments or other furniture. There is a raised dais at the back, with several steps leading up to it. Upon the dais stood a splendid Throne of archaic design, and over the Throne there are two huge tablets of black marble, with inscriptions in Chinese and Manchu characters. This great hall is used only for receiving the Mongolian Princes on their annual visit to Peking, when they come in state, with hundreds of followers and retainers, to pay homage and tribute to the Emperor of China. The rear of the hall opens on a court surrounded by smaller buildings, which are used as waiting-rooms for the retainers and followers of the Princes.

===Chinese===
我第二次到西苑那一日，我们到达之后，先向皇太后行礼，随后便上了轿，前往蒙古王公觐见殿。这是苑东北部一座宏伟的大殿，距皇太后和皇上的宫寝颇有几里路。殿如宫中常制，只有一层，然此一层便高近四十英尺。殿内极为轩敞，仅设几张雕龙桌椅，再无别的陈设或家具。殿后有一座高台，须登数级台阶而上。台上设一张古制华美的宝座，宝座上方悬着两块硕大的黑色大理石匾，匾上以汉字与满洲字镌着铭文。这座大殿专为接见蒙古王公而设——王公们每年进京朝觐，率数百随从仆从，排开仪仗，前来朝拜中国皇上、贡献方物，便在此殿。殿后开向一座庭院，四围环以若干较小的房屋，乃是供王公们的随从、仆役候旨之所。

===Original===
From this hall we were carried in our chairs along the banks of the lake, beyond the Marble Bridge to a distant part of the grounds, where stands the famous Dragon Wall. Most of the Chinese houses have a sort of stone screen opposite the principal gate of entrance. This screen, called “A Wall of Respect,” often has some sort of painted or carved representation of a dragon, which is supposed to chase away evil spirits. This superstition does not seem to obtain as regards the residences of the Son of Heaven, for I never saw a dragon wall built in front of any of the entrances to the buildings in the Palace enclosures. Perhaps the Son of Heaven is immune from the visit of demons, or is it that the rampant Double Dragon on everything Imperial serves as sufficient protection to the Palace? The Dragon Wall, in the Sea Palace, must have formed a part of some of the outside palaces or temples which were brought into the sacred enclosure when the Emperor Hsien-Feng decided to make it a place of residence and enlarge its domain. Many foreigners in Peking can remember when the beautiful Marble Bridge, of such noble proportions, of such exquisite design, now within the Precincts, was used by the public. However it got there, the Dragon Wall is at present within the Palace enclosure, though in an unused part of the grounds—not in front of any residence, and hence not filling its mission as a “Wall of Respect,” to keep the wicked spirits from crossing the threshold. This Dragon Wall of beautiful white marble is of great beauty, exquisitely carved in its minutest details, and fine in general conception and line.

===Chinese===
出了此殿，我们坐着轿子沿湖岸前行，过了汉白玉石桥，来到苑中一处较远的地方，那座有名的九龙壁便立于此。中国人家的大门正对面，大多立着一面石照壁。这照壁称作「照墙」，上面常绘或雕有龙形，据说可以驱邪避祟。然而这种迷信，在天子的居所似乎并不通行——我在宫禁之内各处殿宇的门前，从未见过建有龙墙的。也许是天子本就不为妖魔所侵，又或者，凡属御用之物上那无处不在的「二龙戏珠」纹样，便足以护佑宫禁？西苑里这堵龙墙，想必本是宫苑之外某些宫殿或庙宇的一部分，待咸丰帝决意以此处为驻跸之所、扩充苑囿之时，便被一并圈入了这片禁苑。北京有不少外侨还记得，那座比例雄阔、形制精绝的汉白玉石桥——如今已圈在禁苑之内——从前原是任凭百姓通行的。无论这堵墙当初是如何到了此处，如今它既已入了宫禁，却设在苑里一处不用的角落——并不在任何殿宇之前，因而也无以尽其「照墙」的本分，挡住邪祟进门。这堵汉白玉龙墙甚是华美，雕工精微入妙，整体构思与线条也都极见精彩。

## Chinese Architecture / 中国建筑

===Original===
Her Majesty had returned from the Audience when we got back to the Palace from our morning promenade. She was now attending to household affairs. The eunuchs were bringing up, for her inspection, the baskets of splendid fruits, which are daily sent into the Palace. Among others, there was a basket of magnificent grapes. She was delighted with their beauty, and held up one splendid bunch against the light, before she tasted them, remarking that “the beautiful color lent an added zest to the delicious fruit.” Her Majesty then lunched, while we joined the Empress and Princesses on the verandah, after which we lunched again in this beautiful Throne-room. The meals taken with the young Empress and Ladies of the Court had now come to be gay reunions. Her Majesty would ask us every day to lunch or dine at her table, and I rarely took a meal in my own quarters. I had discarded the knife and fork and was learning to use the chopsticks. I thought them such graceful implements when wielded by the beautiful hands of the Chinese Ladies, that I determined to learn their use. Though I never became an adept with them, I found these dainty implements perfectly adapted for eating the Chinese food. They are used both in the same hand like twin fairy wands, and seemed to me much more delicate and graceful than a knife and fork. My efforts at using them, and my desire to try all the new dishes, amused and pleased the Ladies. Each would give me special tidbits from her favorite dishes; they tried to teach me the Chinese names of the viands. My efforts at pronouncing these names, or my giving them to the wrong dishes, sometimes raised peals of laughter from the whole table. Her Majesty often heard the merriment, and would ask us, when we went into her private apartments after the meal, what had been the cause; and sometimes she would say, “What has ‘Ker-Gunia’4 said?”

===Chinese===
我们早晨游罢回到宫里之时，皇太后已从觐见中回来了。她这时正在料理家务。太监们正把一篮一篮华美的果品——此乃每日送进宫来的——逐一呈上来给她过目。其中有一篮极壮美的葡萄。她见了那艳丽的颜色十分欢喜，尝之前先将一串硕大的葡萄迎着光举起，说道：「这般美丽的颜色，给美味的果子又添了一重滋味。」随后皇太后用午膳，我们则到廊上去陪皇后与众位公主；之后我们在这间华美的宝座殿里又用了一顿午膳。与皇后和宫中命妇们一同用膳，如今已成了一种欢快的聚会。皇太后每日都要请我们到她席上午膳或晚膳，我难得在自己的住处独自用饭。我已弃了刀叉，开始学着用筷子。我见中国女眷们那美丽的手使起筷子来，姿态那般优雅，便决心也要学一学。虽说始终未成高手，我倒觉得这精巧的器具用来吃中国菜实在相宜。两根筷子同在一手之中，犹如一对仙人的小杖，于我看来，比刀叉更见精巧雅致。我学使筷子的笨拙模样，以及我样样新菜都要尝一尝的兴头，颇令命妇们觉着有趣、又觉着欢喜。她们每人都从自家爱吃的菜里挑些精美的给我；又试着教我那些菜肴的中国名字。我发音时的种种难处，又或把它们安错了菜，有时竟引得全席哄堂大笑。皇太后常听见这般欢笑声，待我们饭后到她私室里去时，便要问我们笑些什么；有时她便会问：「『克姑娘』又说了什么？」

===Original===
We had scarcely finished luncheon, on this my second day at the Sea Palace, before the chairs were ordered for a promenade. It had begun to rain, and the air was chilly; but Her Majesty had made up her mind to have a walk at that hour, and nothing ever interfered with her plans, in so far as she was able to carry them out. No weather, however disagreeable or severe, ever kept her from an outdoor promenade that she had planned. The open chairs were brought, as if the day were fine. Her Majesty and the Empress took their seats in their yellow chairs. Their attendant eunuchs unfurled the huge yellow umbrellas, used only for Their Imperial Majesties and the young Empress; the second Empress took her orange-colored chair; the Princesses and the rest of us seated ourselves in our red chairs, and our eunuchs raised the red umbrellas over us. Her Majesty the Empress and Princesses, clothed in the brilliant colors daily worn, the eunuchs still wearing their richly embroidered gala costumes, the chair-bearers still clad in the festive red, the yellow and red chairs with the big yellow, orange, and red umbrellas made a quaint procession, bright with color, that started off through the courts into the gardens.

===Chinese===
我在西苑的这第二日，午膳方毕，便传下轿来，又要出去游赏。天已下起雨来，空气里带着寒意；然皇太后既已定在这个时辰出门散步，便无人能扰她的计划——只要她力所能及，便无更改。无论天气多令人不快、多么严酷，都挡不住她那已经筹划好的户外游赏。敞轿被抬了上来，仿佛是个好天。皇太后与皇后坐进她们的明黄轿。随侍的太监撑起硕大的黄罗伞——此伞唯二位陛下与皇后可用；皇后（此处原文为 second Empress，亦指隆裕）坐进她的橘黄色轿；公主们与我等其余人坐进我们的红轿，我们的太监在我们头上撑起红伞。皇太后、皇后与众位公主身着日常所穿那一身鲜丽颜色，太监们仍穿着那绣工繁富的吉服，轿夫们仍穿着那喜庆的红色衣裳，黄的、红的轿子配着那大黄、大橘、大红的大伞，列成一支古意盎然的行列，色彩斑斓，穿过一道道庭院，迤逦走入苑中。

===Original===
Her Majesty loves every phase of nature and every kind of weather; but it seemed to me as if she particularly loved rain. She once said it lent such a poetic charm to the landscape, bathing it in a soft mystery and washing away all defects. Peking is a dry place, and rain is a rarity, which probably accounts for this predilection. Her Majesty was in great good humor, but her partiality for rain was not shared by the other Ladies of the Palace, and these rainy promenades were never indulged in by them with any great show of delight. Her Majesty likes moving swiftly, and the chair-bearers always run when she leads the procession. We sped along for about fifteen minutes, when the chairs suddenly stopped. I looked to see for what reason, as we were in the open, with no shelter anywhere near, and the rain still falling. I was surprised to see Her Majesty was already out of her chair and walking off toward a gourd-arbor at the side of the paved walk.

===Chinese===
皇太后爱自然的一切面貌，也爱天气的一切模样；然在我看来，她似乎尤其爱雨。她曾说过，雨给山水添了一种诗意的韵致，为它披上一层柔和的神秘，又将一切瑕疵都涤荡净尽。北京是个干燥之地，落雨本就稀少，她这偏好大概由此而来。皇太后兴致极好，但宫中其他女眷却不像她那般爱雨，这种雨天出游，于她们而言从不是什么乐事。皇太后喜欢走得快，她领头之时，轿夫们一路都小跑着。我们疾行了约一刻钟，轿子忽然停了下来。我四下张望，想看是为了什么——我们正在旷处，附近并无避雨之所，而雨还在下。却见皇太后已下了轿，正朝着石板路旁一处葫芦棚走去，我看了好生诧异。

===Original===
The gourd is much esteemed by the Chinese. It is emblematic of Fruitfulness and Prosperity, and is a special favorite of Her Majesty’s. Those cultivated at the Palace, and known all over China as the “Imperial Gourd,” have long been famous; but have reached a greater state of perfection than ever before, under the special care and training given them during Her Majesty the Empress Dowager’s reign. They are of one shape only, with a contracted neck and two equal parts above and below; but they are of all sizes, from one to twelve inches, the one-inch size being as perfect as the larger ones. They are grown on trellises, about seven feet high, and the vines are very carefully trained, so that each of the much-prized fruit may attain its best development and have its proper quota of light and sun.

===Chinese===
葫芦是中国人极为看重的一种东西。它象征着多子与兴旺，是皇太后格外钟爱之物。宫中所种、被国人称作「御葫芦」的，久负盛名；然在皇太后当朝之时，因特别精心地养护调理，竟达到了前所未有的精妙境地。它们只有一种形状——颈部收束，上下两半大小相称；却有各种尺寸，从一寸到一尺二寸不等，便是那一寸大的，也与大的一样周正完美。它们栽在约七尺高的棚架上，藤蔓都料理得极为仔细，务使每一颗这宝贵的果实都能尽得其宜，得着应得的光与日。

===Original===
Her Majesty walked through the mud to the arbor. The white kid six-inch-high soles of her shoes sank deep into the soft, rain-soaked soil. The eunuchs made vain attempts to protect her from the rain, but she went imperturbably on and was soon under the gourd-arbor. Here she leisurely tried several of the gourds, to see if they were properly ripe; for they must be pulled at a certain time or they do not dry well. After looking at and trying a number, she had several gathered and went back to her chair. The young Empress and the other Ladies had, of course, got out of the chairs when Her Majesty stopped. Luckily, she did not ask us to go into the arbor with her; but etiquette obliged us to stand on the marble walk, which though not muddy and not so disagreeable as the walk to the gourd-arbor, was, however, running with water. When Her Majesty took her chair again, we resumed ours, with a sigh of relief; for, though we were unprotected even in the chairs, we felt the truth of the Oriental saying, “It is better to be sitting than standing,” etc.

===Chinese===
皇太后踏着泥泞朝那棚子走去。她那白色羊皮鞋六寸高的鞋底，深深地陷进被雨水浸软了的泥土里。太监们徒然地想替她挡雨，她却神色自若地一路过去，转眼便到了葫芦棚下。她不慌不忙地把几只葫芦逐一一试，看是否已经熟得恰好——摘葫芦须在一定的时辰，否则便不易晾干。她看过、试过若干之后，吩咐人摘下几只，便回到轿中。皇后与众位命妇，在皇太后停下时，自然也都下了轿。幸而她并未叫我们随她进棚；但礼数所拘，我们只得立在大理石道上——此道虽不泥泞，也不似去葫芦棚那一段那样难行，却也是水流如注。皇太后复又上轿之时，我们也各自回到轿中，长舒一口气——虽说即便在轿里也并无遮蔽，然我们倒深觉那句东方谚语不假：「坐着总比站着强」云云。

===Original===
After another quarter of an hour, the chair-bearers stopped again. We had come to another gourd-arbor! Her Majesty got out of her chair and examined the gourds in this arbor with the same deliberation and interest as she had looked at those where we first stopped. The rain was now falling in torrents, but Her Majesty’s spirits seemed to go up in proportion to its coming down. The Ladies were again obliged to get out of their chairs! They stood in two dejected lines, with the eunuchs holding, as best they might, the red umbrellas over each, and they vainly tried to keep up an appearance of interest and enjoyment. The brave finery of the eunuchs, who may not carry umbrellas when on service, was now hanging in limp folds about them, and their fine feathers were much bedraggled. The Chinese Ladies had their two-inch-high, kid-covered cork soles to protect their feet from the water; but mine, in thin kid slippers, were soaking. The picture of the dejected Ladies, the rain-soaked eunuchs, was, however, so amusing, that I quite forgot my own discomfort and thoroughly enjoyed the situation. After another twenty minutes’ run, with the rain still falling, Her Majesty gave the word and the procession turned toward the Hall of the Mongolian Princes. The great doors were thrown open, and we were, at last, under shelter.

===Chinese===
又过了一刻钟，轿夫们又停了下来——我们竟到了又一处葫芦棚！皇太后下轿，将棚里的葫芦细细察看一番，与方才在那第一处所看的，竟是同样地从容、同样地兴致盎然。此时雨已倾盆，然皇太后的兴致，似乎与雨势成正比地越发高涨。命妇们又不得不下轿！她们分作两行，神色恹恹地立着，太监们竭力替各人撑着那红伞，她们则徒然地强作一副有兴趣、又觉欢喜的神气。太监们当差之时不许撑伞，他们那一身华美的服饰如今软塌塌地贴在身上，那一身漂亮的翎毛装饰也被淋得狼狈不堪。中国女眷们有那两寸高的羊皮包面的软木底护着，脚尚可不着水；我穿的却是薄羊皮便鞋，早已湿透。然而这一幅命妇恹恹、太监淋雨的景象，委实有趣，我竟忘了自家的不适，将这情景看得十分有趣。又跑了二十来分钟，雨犹未歇，皇太后一声令下，行列便折向蒙古王公觐见殿。沉重的大门一齐敞开，我们终于得着了遮蔽。

===Original===
A yellow chair was placed for Her Majesty in front of the dais, and she had some of the gourds she had gathered brought to her. She selected one for herself, gave one to her principal Lady-in-waiting, Sih-Gerga, and handed one to the Chief Eunuch Li—the Princess and the Chief Eunuch both being proficient in the art of scraping them. A sharpened piece of bamboo was brought to Her Majesty and she began to work on the gourd she had taken, scraping off the outer skin. She told me to stand near and watch her scrape it, as it was a very difficult thing to do well! She certainly did it well, and it was most interesting to watch her beautiful little hands, as they gracefully moved the piece of bamboo back and forth, quickly removing the outer skin, in the most approved way. Though apparently thoroughly interested in scraping her gourd, she asked me how I had enjoyed my promenade of the day before, and what I thought of the Sea Palace. She called my attention to the inscriptions on the tablets behind the Throne, saying they were in Manchu and Chinese characters, pointing out their difference of form and also speaking of the differences in the two languages. She said she thought Manchu would be easier for a foreigner to learn than Chinese, as Manchu has an alphabet and is constructed more on the lines of a European language. Presently Her Majesty turned to speak to someone else, and I immediately withdrew, as is the custom at the Palace. We went out and joined the Empress and Princesses, who had already retired from the Throne-room and were having tea and cigarettes reclining on the couches in one of the rooms in the rear. After an hour’s rest in the Mongolian Hall, the rain having ceased, we continued our promenade through the grounds much more pleasantly than we had begun it, and Her Majesty took me for a walk in the Gardens of the Sea Palace, as she had promised.

===Chinese===
殿内的台前为皇太后安了一把明黄交椅，她叫人把方才摘的几个葫芦取几个来。她自挑了一只，又给她那位首席女官四姑娘（Sih-Gerga）一只，再递给李莲英大总管一只——这位公主与大总管都是刮葫芦的能手。一根削尖的竹片被呈到皇太后手中，她便着手料理自己取的那只葫芦，将它的外皮刮去。她叫我站到近处看她刮，说要把这事做好实在不易！她这事的确做得极好，看她那双美丽的小手优雅地将那竹片来回移动，迅捷地以最地道的手法将外皮削去，实在是一件极有趣的事。她虽然看似全心全意地在刮着葫芦，却问我昨日游赏可尽兴，又问我看这西苑如何。她指我留意宝座后那两块匾上的铭文，说那是满洲字与汉字，一一指给我看二者形制的不同，又谈起这两种语言的差异。她说她以为，让一个外夷之人来学，满洲文当比中文易些——因满洲文有字母，结构上更接近欧洲的语言。过了一会儿，皇太后转向旁人说话，我便依宫中规矩立即退下。我们出到外面，与皇后和公主们会合——她们早已从宝座殿退下，正在后面一间屋子里的榻上倚着，饮茶抽烟。在蒙古王公觐见殿歇了一个时辰，雨也已停，我们便继续在苑中游赏，比开始时愉快得多；皇太后又依着她所许下的诺言，亲自领我在西苑的御花园里散了一回步。

===Original===
After dinner, we were rowed over the lake to the Gates. Just beyond them a company of archers was practising with their bows and arrows; for archery is still in vogue in China, and fine marksmanship among the archers is rewarded by substantial advancement in the army. Archery is also practised as a sport by the young Manchu nobles. It is said to educate the eye and materially develop the chest and arms. The Chinese pay great attention to position in archery. They stand stiffly erect, the chest thrown well forward, the head held high, the bow and arrow at rigidly prescribed angles; and if this position be not observed, however true the flight of the arrow, it goes for naught. From the shelter of my chair, I watched the company’s practice until I heard the “Sunset call” resounding through the Palace grounds; echoed and reechoed until it reached the outer gates, which began to move upon their huge hinges until they clanged together for the night.

===Chinese===
晚膳之后，我们被船摇过湖面，送到宫门。门外不远处，正有一队弓手在练习弓箭；盖因射箭在中国至今仍盛行，弓手中凡射术精绝者，俱得在军中实授升迁。满洲的年轻贵族也以射箭为戏。据说此技可练眼力，又能大大发达胸臂。中国人于射箭极讲身法。他们挺身直立，挺胸昂首，弓与箭各依严定的角度；若不合此身法，则箭纵射得再准，亦不算数。我在轿中遮蔽之下，看着那一队人操练，直至听见「落日号」在宫苑之间响起来——一声声回响叠叠，直至传到外门，那巨大的门扇便开始在沉重的门枢上缓缓转动，终于当的一声，合拢起来，闭门过夜。

## XXVI Some Social Customs—Manchu and Chinese / 第二十六章 满族与汉族的若干社会习俗

===Original===
I met the wives and families of all the Princes, nobles, and high Manchu officials in Peking, for they came to the Court at stated intervals, besides on many special occasions, when they were invited by Her Majesty. The most frequent of these visitors to the Palace were Prince Ching’s wives and daughters, the wives of the Emperor’s brothers, his father’s secondary wives and their daughters, and the sisters of the young Empress, one of whom is the clever Princess Schun. The widow of the Grand Secretary Yung Lu, who lost, in one year, her husband and a promising son, and who was nearly crazed by grief, also came often. She was not very brilliant, nor the kind of woman to appeal to Her Majesty; but her grief seemed to touch the Empress Dowager, and she received special marks of favor when in the Palace, and came and went as she willed. A stepdaughter, whom she had herself brought up through a very delicate childhood, was the wife of the Emperor’s brother, Prince C’hun. Should they have a son he will probably be the next heir to the Throne.

===Chinese===
在北京，我见到了所有王公、贵族以及满洲高官的女眷和家人——他们除皇上特召的种种场合之外，还按规定的时日入宫朝见。入宫最勤的，要数庆亲王（Prince Ching）的夫人和女儿们、皇上兄弟们的福晋、皇上父亲侧室及其所出之女，还有皇后的姐妹，其中一位便是那位聪慧过人的顺公主（Princess Schun）。大学士荣禄（Yung Lu）的遗孀也常来——她于一年之内连丧夫君和一个前程远大的儿子，悲恸之下几乎发了疯。她为人并不十分出众，也算不得能讨皇太后欢心的那一类女子；但她的哀痛似打动了皇太后，故在宫中蒙受格外的恩遇，出入宫禁悉听其便。她有一位养女——自幼多病，是这位夫人亲手拉扯大的——后来嫁与了皇上的兄弟醇亲王（Prince Ch’un）。他们若生下一子，多半便是皇位的下一任继承人。

===Original===
On their marriage the brides of nobles of a certain rank go to the Palace to be presented to the Empress Dowager. This ceremony corresponds to the presentation, on their marriage, of ladies at the English Court. These brides are always magnificently dressed in embroidered gowns of rich colors, and wear, for the first time, the Court coiffure of the married ladies, the magnificent golden filigree, jeweled construction, which I have already described, and for this occasion they wear a profusion of jewels. The Manchu ladies use much more discretion in wearing jewels than the Chinese ladies. The latter will sometimes wear as many as fifteen bracelets on each arm, and the number of jewels they put in their coiffure seems to be limited only by the space they have at their disposal.

===Chinese===
贵族之家的女子出阁，依其品级，婚后须入宫觐见皇太后。此一礼数，与英国宫廷中新妇于婚后入宫觐见相仿。这些新妇无不盛装华服，所着绣袍色彩鲜艳华美，并首次戴上已婚夫人的朝服头饰——便是我已描写过的那华美的金丝累丝嵌珠头面——逢此场合，她们所佩的首饰更极尽繁多。满洲夫人在佩戴首饰上较汉人夫人克制得多。后者有时一条胳膊上能戴多至十五只镯子，至于她们头面上插戴的珠翠之数，似乎只受她们头上所余空位的限制。

===Original===
The brides come to the Palace in red satin bridal chairs, accompanied by their husband’s mother and his married sisters, if he has any; if not, by his nearest women relations. On their arrival in the Precincts they first go to the Throne-room and make their bows and prostrations before the Empress Dowager, to thank her for the gifts she has sent. Sometimes Her Majesty would speak to them at some length, seeming to give them advice. After making their obeisances to her, they then make their salutations to the young Empress. They spend the day at the Palace, take luncheon with the young Empress and Princesses, and leave about three o’clock. These brides were generally very young girls, though sometimes I was surprised to see that they had well passed the first bloom of youth, for I had thought that all Oriental women were married very young. The young Empress was always charming to the brides, and seemed to watch over their pleasure, and try to make them enjoy this rather trying day, when they were the observed of all observers. Among these brides, the winter I was in Peking, was the wife of the Emperor’s youngest brother, a charming young girl with sweet manners, far more attractive in every way than Yung Lu’s daughter, the wife of Prince C’hun.

===Chinese===
新妇们乘朱缎花轿入宫，由婆母和丈夫已婚的姐妹陪同；若丈夫无姐妹，则由其最亲近的女性长辈陪伴。一入宫禁，她们先到宝座殿，向皇太后行礼叩拜，谢她所赐的礼物。有时皇太后会与她们说上好一番话，似是谆谆训诲。向皇太后行过礼之后，她们又向皇后行礼请安。她们在宫中盘桓一日，与皇后及诸位公主福晋一同用午饭，约莫三点钟方才离去。这些新妇大都是非常年轻的少女，不过有时我也诧异地发觉，她们之中竟有早已过了青春盛年之人——我原以为东方女子都是早婚的。皇后待这些新妇总是十分亲切，似乎处处留心她们的处境，务使她们在这万众瞩目、颇为难熬的一日里能尽兴。我在北京那个冬天所见的新妇之中，有一位是皇上幼弟的福晋——一位仪态温婉、讨人喜欢的少女，无论哪一面看，都比荣禄的女儿、那位醇亲王福晋要动人得多。

===Original===
The Chinese look upon a daughter, at her birth, as a misfortune, one of the ills that must be endured, and while loving her individually, a daughter is not welcomed into the family nor allowed the privileges of a son. It is, however, quite different with the Manchus. A daughter not being able to sacrifice to the ancestors, even Manchus prefer a son; but a daughter is a welcome member of the family, and she has a distinct and independent position of her own. One of the Chinese ministers to Washington once told me that the only unmarried woman in the world whose position is analogous to that of the “American Girl,” in her own family, is the Manchu girl.

===Chinese===
汉人视生女为一种不幸，是必须忍受的苦厄之一；纵然私下里也疼爱这个女儿，她却并不为家族所欢迎，也不得享有儿子所享的种种权利。然而满族却大不相同。女儿既不能奉祭祖先，满洲人家便也宁愿生儿；但女儿始终是家中受欢迎的一员，且自有其独立分明的地位。曾有一位中国驻华盛顿公使告诉我：世上唯一在自己的本家地位可与「美国姑娘（American Girl）」相比的未婚女子，便是这满族姑娘。

===Original===
As long as the Manchu girl remains unmarried, she is a veritable power in the household. She ranks as high as her brother, and always takes precedence of her brother’s wife, even if that wife be double her age and married before she was born. She precedes her mother even, as she is of the Blood and her mother of “another family.” Not only has she these social privileges, but she has well-defined legal rights. Her father cannot make a disposition of his property without his eldest daughter’s consent. She can go into her brother’s house, dismiss his servants, and generally direct his affairs. Her word has more weight as to the bringing up of her brother’s children than his wife’s, as she is a sister, a born relation, and the wife is only an acquired relation. When she marries, however, she becomes a member of the family into which she marries; but even then, such is the ascendancy of the girl in the Manchu family, even after her marriage into another family, she often goes on dictating to her brother’s family and her own as before, if she does not find her own household duties and her own family sufficient occupation to keep her from doing so. Such is the force of consanguinity among the Manchus, and the position of the daughter in the family.

===Chinese===
满族姑娘一日未嫁，便一日是家中名副其实的一柄权柄。她的位次与兄弟同等，永远排在兄弟之妻前——纵然那位嫂嫂年纪比她大一倍、出嫁时她尚未出生，亦无例外。她甚至排在母亲之前，因为她身上流着本族的血，而母亲则出身「别姓」。她不仅享有这些社交上的特权，其法律上的权利亦有明文规定。父亲处分财产，非经长女首肯，不得擅自为之。她可径入兄弟家中，斥退他的奴仆，乃至过问、处置他的家务。论及兄弟孩子的教养，她的话比兄弟之妻更有分量——因为她是姐妹，是天生的至亲，而妻子不过是后天结成的亲眷。可一旦出阁，她便成为夫家的一员；纵然如此，满族姑娘在家族中的这种威势，即便嫁入别家之后仍旧不减，往往还要照旧对兄弟之家乃至自家事务指手画脚——除非她自己那一摊家务和家事已经够她忙的，无暇及此。满洲人家血亲之情之重，以及女儿在家族中的地位，便是如此。

===Original===
The unmarried Manchu girl has not only this liberty in her family, but she has more liberty in the outside world than any other Oriental woman.

===Chinese===
这位未嫁的满族姑娘，不仅在家中享有这般自由，便是出外行走于世间，也比任何其他东方女子都要自由。

===Original===
They are not so restricted in their social intercourse as any other Oriental women, and while they are not so literary as the Chinese, they have more social qualities and are brighter conversationalists, being both witty and gay.

===Chinese===
她们的社交往来并不像其他东方女子那样处处受限；她们虽不及汉人女子那样博学，却有更丰沛的社交才能，谈吐也更为机警风趣——既聪敏，又爽朗活泼。

===Original===
They are not forced to marry against their inclinations and some remain single to the end of their days, or marry late in life if they so desire. These unmarried ladies are not only looked up to by their own families, but they are not regarded as being objects of commiseration by the world at large. On the contrary, they are rewarded with triumphal arches and splendid monuments if they have passed a long and exemplary life of maidenhood. Although the brides that came into the Palace were generally young, one who came to make her bow to the Empress Dowager, while I was there, was a lady of forty-two summers. She had brought up two or three families of brothers’ children and directed their households; but she finally succumbed to the charms of a wealthy official, who had lost his wife two years before and who had a number of children on which she could continue to practise her theories as to their bringing up. Had she held out longer and died a maiden, she might have had an arch built to her memory after death and gone down to posterity.

===Chinese===
她们不会被逼着违反本愿而婚嫁，有些人便终身不嫁，或随己愿到晚年才择人而事。这些未嫁的女子不仅为本家所敬重，世人也并不视之为可悯可怜之人。相反，她们若长年如此、毕生为闺范之表率，朝廷反以牌坊和宏丽的碑碣来褒奖她们。虽说入宫朝见的新妇大都很年轻，我在宫中之时，却有一位入朝向皇太后行礼的新妇，已是四十二岁了。她曾抚养过两三房兄弟的子女，并主理他们的家务；然而她终于抵不过一位富绅的求亲——此公两年前丧妻，膝下子女成群，正可让她继续施展她那一套教养儿女的心得。倘若她再矜持些，待字闺中以至老死，死后或许还能获赐一座牌坊，从此名留青史。

===Original===
Only ladies, young girls and boys under seventeen were ever guests of the Empress Dowager in her Palace. The Manchu nobles and high officials were invited on certain days to the Theater, but there was always the high intervening screen between them and Her Majesty’s and the Ladies’ loges. The Princes and nobles who have official positions, see the Empress Dowager in the Audience Hall, and she is now over sixty. She has more liberty than before, but generally their Audiences are with the Emperor alone, and they never come into the Ladies’ Precincts. At the performance of the European circus in the Palace grounds I saw, for the first time, nearly all the Princes and Manchu officials.

===Chinese===
唯有女眷、少女以及十七岁以下的男童，方得作皇太后宫中的客人。满洲贵族与高官虽于特定日子蒙召入园看戏，但他们与皇太后及女眷们的包厢之间，始终隔着一道高高的障纱。有官职在身的王公贵族，得在觐见殿觐见皇太后——她如今已年过六旬。她比从前更为通达，但大抵而言，这些人觐见的只是皇上一个人，他们绝入不得女眷所居的内廷。直到那次西洋马戏在御苑内开演，我才头一次同时见到几乎所有王公和满洲大员。

===Original===
The Manchus are a taller race than the Chinese and more athletic-looking. They are fond of exercise, indulge in archery, riding, etc, and do not look down upon a military career, as do the Chinese. It is said that polo playing, which the English got from India, originated among the Tartars, and that it is still played in Manchuria. I never saw polo played by the Manchus, but I have seen some daring riding done by the young nobles that would seem to show they could play polo if they would. The Manchu nobles have an inherited military rank, and they also receive military advancement for proficiency in archery and riding. The warlike spirit that prompted the Manchus and their progenitors, the Nu-Chih Tartars, who not only conquered China, but, as “the Huns,” almost overran Europe itself, is no longer so militant as it was. The modern Manchu is becoming almost as peace-loving as the Chinese themselves, but there are still qualities which show their descent from a race of warriors.

===Chinese===
满人比汉人身材更高，看上去也更为矫健。他们好活动，喜骑射，并不像汉人那样瞧不起武人一途。据说英国人从印度学来的击鞠之戏，最初便发源于鞑靼人之中，至今满洲仍有此戏。我从未见过满人打马球，但曾见那些年轻的王公贵族纵马飞驰，胆气纵横，看那架势，他们若肯下场击鞠，原也不难。满洲贵族不仅有世袭的武职品级，且因弓马娴熟亦可获军功升迁。当年那种驱使满人及其先人——女真鞑靼（Nu-Chih Tartars）——不仅征服了中国，更以「匈奴」之名几欲席卷整个欧洲的尚武之气，如今已不似从前那般凌厉。今日的满人已变得几乎和汉人一样爱好和平，但身上仍可见若干迹象，表明他们是武士之族的后裔。

===Original===
They wear the ordinary Chinese costume, and though it is said “the shaven head and wearing of the queue” were instituted as marks of degradation for the Chinese when they were conquered by the Manchus, the Emperor himself and all the Manchu nobles shave their heads and wear the queue! They wear satin boots with white kid soles. Their hats, in summer of finely woven straw, and of fur in winter, have the crown covered with a tassel of red silk, surmounted by the jeweled button denoting their ranks. From this button stands out, almost at right angles, a jade-mounted aigret, mixed with the peafowl feathers, if they have attained that rank. In winter, they wear splendid sable short coats. Except these sable topcoats, fur is never worn on the outside of a garment in China, but is used only as a lining.

===Chinese===
他们着汉人寻常衣冠；虽说据传「剃发留辫」本是汉人被满人征服之后所加诸其身的屈辱之记，然而皇上本人和所有满洲贵族，自己也一样剃发留辫！他们足蹬白羔皮底的缎靴。其冠帽夏用编织精细的草辫制成，冬用皮毛；帽顶覆以红丝缨，缨上承一颗珠宝顶子，用以别其品级。顶子上斜斜挑出，几成直角，是一支玉镶的翎管所插的翎羽，若已得到那种品级，翎上还须杂以孔雀翎。冬日里他们披着华美的短貂褂。除这种貂褂而外，在中国，皮毛绝不见于衣服的外面，只用作衣里。

===Original===
When I saw the Manchu nobles at the circus at the Summer Palace, they wore the splendid summer Court costume, embroidered in the double dragon, reaching below the knee. They were tightly belted in around the waist, and very full and ample across the shoulders, giving the men the appearance, at least, of broad shoulders, and enhancing their already fine figures. One could see that the Emperor was the “glass of fashion and mold of form” of the young nobles; for they all aimed, as much as possible, at his slenderness of figure and even imitated his carriage. The young dandies, however, wore a much greater profusion of ornaments than His Majesty ever indulged in. The belt buckle, the handsomest ornament worn, was of carved jade, ruby quartz, or of beautifully chased gold set with precious stones. They were then wearing a profusion of ornaments dangling from their belts—embroidered cases for fans, chopsticks and knives, and many other ornaments besides the watch, an indispensable adjunct to every Chinese gentleman’s costume. This is worn hanging from the belt in a handsome, embroidered case with an open front, so that the elaborate case, generally studded with jewels, beautifully enameled, or curiously incised, could be seen. This case had a sort of fob attachment made of silken cord, woven into quaint designs and finished generally with a wonderfully carved piece of jade, ruby quartz, or some other curious stone.

===Chinese===
我在颐和园看马戏时所见的那班满洲贵族，身着华美的夏日朝服，上绣团龙，衣长过膝。腰间束得紧紧的，两肩却宽阔丰盈，使这些男子至少看上去肩宽背挺，把他们本就出色的身段衬得越发挺拔。看得出来，皇上便是那些年轻贵族「风尚与仪范之典范（the glass of fashion and mold of form）」——他们无不竭力效仿皇上清瘦的身段，乃至模仿他的举止仪态。不过那些年轻的花花公子们，所戴的饰物比皇上平日所用要繁多得多。腰间所佩最华美的一件——带扣——是雕玉的、红玉髓的，或以雕花黄金镶嵌各色宝石而成。他们当时腰间还挂着一大串零碎：绣花的扇套、筷子、小刀，还有许多别的零碎，更不消说那怀表——怀表乃每一位中国绅士衣冠上不可或缺的物事。怀表挂在腰间，盛在一个华美的绣花套子里，套子前面开口，好让人看清那做工讲究的表套——上面一般嵌着珠翠，或绘珐琅，或雕镂奇巧的花样。这表套还缀着一条用丝线编结而成的表坠，结成种种古雅的花样，末端往往坠着一件雕工奇绝的玉饰、红玉髓，或别的什么奇石。

===Original===
Manchu ladies wear their gowns long and loose, hanging from the shoulders, and never show the line of the waist, nor the outline of the figure; but the men belt in their gowns tightly, and are very proud of a small waist.

===Chinese===
满洲夫人的长衣自肩披垂而下，又长又宽，从不显露腰身，更不显体态之轮廓；而男子的袍子却紧紧束在腰间，他们并以腰细为傲。

===Original===
Among the social customs in China, which obtain also among the Manchus, is “concubinage.” But it exists in such a form that in its actual state, it might more properly be called “plurality of wives.” The concubine, or secondary wife, as I will call her, is taken from the bosom of her family, and her position in her husband’s family is considered as secure as that of the first wife. Though the first wife only has a legal standing, custom gives the secondary wife equal rights, and she is no more likely to be put aside than the first wife. There are, I suppose, men in China who put away a secondary wife, if they are wealthy enough to have taken one or several, but they would be socially and generally ostracized.

===Chinese===
中国的若干社会风俗之中，也通行于满族的，有「纳妾」一事。但其存在之形态，就实际而言，更恰当的称呼乃是「多妻」。这位妾室——我下文姑且称之为侧室——是正经八百地从她本家迎娶过来的，她在夫家的地位，被视为与正妻同样稳固。虽说唯有正妻方有法律上的名分，但习俗赋予侧室同等的权利，要休掉她和要休掉正妻一样不易。我揣想中国也并非没有那种把侧室休弃的男子——只要他们富裕到足以纳一房乃至几房妾——但凡这样做的人，必为社会所不齿，为人所共弃。

===Original===
The man marries in China as soon as he reaches manhood. Some young girl who is of the same social standing and has the requisite qualities for his wife is chosen for him by his parents. This is the legitimate wife. She is the first and remains first always, taking precedence of any and all others that may be chosen. The secondary wife is often of the same class as the first wife. She is generally chosen by the man himself, and is taken from some good family who may be poor, and she is an honest young girl.

===Chinese===
中国男子一及冠，便要成婚。父母为之拣选一位门当户对、具备为人妻之品德的少女，便是他的正妻。正妻居先，终身居先，凡日后所选任何侧室，都须让她一头。侧室往往出身与正妻相同之门第，多由男子亲自选定，迎自清白而或贫寒的人家，是位正经的少女。

===Original===
She is received, on her entrance into the household, by the wife and the man’s mother, if she be alive, and her position in the family is assigned to her. While she must pay court and due respect to the first wife, she has her own servants and her own rights, and leads her own independent life. The first wife has entire authority, in certain matters, over the secondary ones, but they generally live amicably together. As the first wife is married several years before any second wife is taken, and as she is also generally their superior in age, this entitles her to their respect, aside from her legal standing and her position as first in the household. The secondary wives stand in the presence of the first wife until she asks them to sit. Should they have any children, the latter call the first wife “mother,” and though the mother has her part in bringing up the child, it calls her, if she be a secondary wife, by her first name, and, in important matters, her authority over the child must give way to that of the first wife. But the first wife rarely abuses her authority over the children any more than over the other wives, and does not interfere except for, what she thinks is, the child’s good.

===Chinese===
侧室入门之日，由正妻和婆母——若婆母尚在——出迎，她在家族中的位次也由此而定。她须对正妻执礼致敬，但她自有奴仆、自有权利，也自有她独立的一份日子。在某些事上，正妻对侧室有全权，但她们大抵能和睦相处。因正妻比任何侧室进门都早几年，年纪一般也较长，单凭这早进门和年长两条，已足令侧室们敬重她——更不必说还有她法律上的名分和居长之地位。侧室在正妻跟前须站着，候正妻叫坐方可坐下。侧室若有子女，子女唤正妻为「娘」；他们的生母虽也有份抚育，可孩子唤她——既为侧室——只直呼其名；遇有大事，她对子女之权亦须让位于正妻。但正妻罕有滥用此权之时——无论对其他侧室还是对这些孩子——她都不过分干预，除非是为了孩子的好处，且以她所见的好处为度。

===Original===
In theory, according to our ideas, and with American or European women, this would be a sad state of affairs, but practically, as it exists in China and with Chinese women, it seems to work well. The arrangement of the houses in China is also well adapted for this kind of life. There are a number of courts surrounded by pavilions, each court and its pavilions forming a unit—a separate dwelling-place—this unit being a part of a great whole.

===Chinese===
照我们的观念——尤其换作美国或欧洲女子——理论上，这情形未免可叹；但实际上，照中国的实情和中国女子所见，它似乎运行得颇为顺遂。中国的宅院格局也极宜于这种生活方式。一座宅子里有若干围以厅堂的院落，每座院落连同其厅堂自成一单元——一处独立的居所——而这单元又是这个庞大整体的一部分。

===Original===
The wives live in harmony together, and seem like a family of sisters. The first wife apparently takes pride in the good conduct and handsome appearance of the others, and there seems to be very little jealousy among them.

===Chinese===
几位妻妾和睦同处，看去好似一家姐妹。正妻似乎以其余几位之品行端正、容貌姣好为荣，她们之间，似乎甚少嫉妒。

===Original===
If this be the position of the secondary wife in the families of the gentry and nobility, one may imagine how much more exalted it is in the Imperial family and how the secondary wife of an Emperor would be considered. To have their daughter chosen as the secondary wife of an Emperor is looked upon as an honor in the highest Manchu families. Of course, they would prefer to have her the first wife, for she has more power, but none of them would demur at an alliance of the secondary kind for their daughter, for she may thus become the mother of an Emperor, and she does become, by this marriage, a member of the Imperial family, and is treated as such. She ranks higher than any of the Princesses or Ladies of the Court, and takes precedence of all except the first wife, or a secondary wife, of the Emperor, who may have been married before she was. Her place is at the side of the first wife, the Empress. In the Palace she is called by the same title as the first wife, a Manchu word meaning “Mistress.” She cannot wear the Imperial yellow, it is true, but she does wear the Imperial orange, which no other Lady at Court can wear.

===Chinese===
倘若仕宦贵胄之家，侧室之地位已是如此，那么皇室之中，其地位之尊崇更可想而知，皇上的侧室之受人看重，更不待言。自己的女儿能被选为皇上的侧室，在最显赫的满洲世家看来，乃是一桩荣耀之事。当然，他们自然更愿女儿为正妻，因正妻之权更大；但若以侧室之位与皇上联姻，他们中也无一会有异议——因为如此一来，她或可成为未来皇上之母；而一经此婚，她便成为皇室的一员，亦以皇室之礼相待。其位次高于宫中一切公主福晋和女眷，唯居两种人之后：一是正妻，二是比她先入宫的皇上侧室。她的位置在正妻——亦即皇后——之侧。宫中对她的称呼与正妻相同，是一个满语词，意指「主子」。她固然不能穿御用明黄，但她却能穿御用杏黄，这是宫中任何其他女眷所不能穿的。

===Original===
These secondary wives are not taken for some physical quality from among the masses; they are not in the Palace as the result of a caprice of the Emperor. They are from the highest families in the land. They are generally chosen by the Emperor’s mother, if she be alive, with as much care as the first wife, and her position is inferior, only from an official standpoint, to that of the Empress. She may even become Empress herself on the death of the first wife and those who precede her. The Emperor of China has no “harem,” but he may have as many wives as he wishes. His wives never live together in the promiscuity of a harem, where all individuality is lost. Each wife has her own establishment and her own position, and is not dependent on her physical charms for her maintenance in that position, any more than is the first wife. Should she be the mother of children, she may advance beyond the others who have none, excepting always the first wife; and even should she have no children, she has always her separate establishment and is considered a member of the family. The Emperor Kwang-Hsu has two wives, both designated by the same title in the Palace. In this account of my experiences I only allude to the first wife, because it would be confusing to speak of two Empresses where there is also an Empress Dowager, and also because the first wife, in this instance, is so much the stronger character and the more interesting personality.

===Chinese===
这些侧室并非因其容颜身段之美，自民间选入；她们入宫，也并非皇上的一时心血来潮。她们皆出自全国最显赫的世家。这些侧室一般由皇上的母亲——若其尚在——亲自拣选，其用心不下于选正妻；她的地位，仅在公家名分上不及皇后一人而已。倘若正妻和位在她之前的侧室相继去世，她甚至可能自己被立为皇后。中国皇帝并无「后宫佳丽」之说，但他愿娶几房妻子便可娶几房。他的妻妾绝不像回教后宫那样混居一处、泯灭个人；每一位都有自己的属官、自己的位次，其地位之维系并不靠容貌姿色，一如正妻之地位不靠姿色一般。倘若她生有子女，便可越过无出之侧室而进位——唯不能越过正妻；纵使无出，她也有自己独立的府邸属官，被视为宗族中的一员。光绪皇上共有两位妻室，宫中对二人皆用同一称呼。在我这本经历记述中，我只提那正妻——其一是因为既有皇太后在，若再说两位皇后，便要教读者糊涂；其二则因为就这一次而言，正妻的性情为人，远比另一位刚强得多，也更有可传之处。

## XXVIII Some Winter Days at the Palace / 二十八 宫中的几个冬日

===Original===
The big, official portrait for St. Louis was advancing. I was able to accomplish much more now that I had a place where I could work uninterruptedly, and quietly study the painting when I was not working. Her Majesty came, with her usual retinue, to pose, but it was not at fixed times, and was often when I did not expect her. She was looking more and more anxious these days; but she came to pose whenever it was necessary, and was very particular as to all the details in the portrait. She often had the jewels and ornaments changed, and her pearl mantle was made over, after she saw it in the first sketch, as she did not like its form.

The throne, about which there had been a question when I began the portrait, and which had been a present to Her Majesty from the late Emperor Tong Chih, her son, had been “lost” during the Boxer troubles, but Her Majesty thought it might be reproduced from descriptions and from sketches by the Palace painters who had seen it; but I could not consent to work either from memory or other painters’ sketches, and I was finally obliged to paint, faute de mieux, one of the carved teakwood thrones of which Her Majesty is so fond. This throne did not suit the straight lines of the composition so well as almost any other in the Palace would have done, but Her Majesty wished it.

I found the representation of the nine life-size phoenix, in vigorous colors, on a blue cloisonné screen placed almost touching the throne, very difficult to represent, so that they did not seem to be real birds flying around her head. The vases of flowers and ornaments were also placed at exactly equal distances on either side of the throne, but it was necessary to paint them this way. It would not have been “proper” otherwise. The figure was in the exact center of the threefold screen, and so near it, it was impossible to get any atmosphere in the background. There was not a fold in either gown or sleeves; but I had now resigned myself to convention and tradition, and I copied mechanically what was placed before me, and made no more efforts at artistic arrangements, nor tried any experiments in execution. I worked like a good artisan, finishing so many inches a day.

===Chinese===
那幅送往圣路易斯博览会（St. Louis Exposition）的大幅御容正像，渐次推进。如今我总算有了一处可以不受打扰地作画的所在，不挥毫之时亦可静静揣摩那画，于是所成便多了许多。皇太后照例带着她的随从前来坐班，但时辰并不固定，又往往在我意料之外。这几日她神色愈见焦灼；但凡有必要，她仍来坐班，对御容上的一切细节亦十分讲究。她常常把珠宝首饰换来换去；那件珍珠氅衣，自她在初稿中瞧见之后，因不喜其式样，也命人重新做过。

宝座一事，自起稿之时便有争议。此座原是先帝同治（Tong Chih）——太后之子——所赐，庚子拳乱那一年「遗失」了。太后原以为，可凭口述与曾见过此座的宫中画师所绘的草图，再仿制一座出来；然我断不能凭记忆或他人草稿作画，到末了，退而求其次（faute de mieux），也只好绘了一座雕花柚木宝座——正是皇太后素来钟爱的那一种。这座宝座于画面那笔直的线条而言，并不如宫中几乎任何一座宝座那般相宜，但皇太后属意于它。

那座蓝色景泰蓝屏风几乎贴近宝座，上绘九只与真鸟等大的凤凰，色彩浓烈鲜明，要把它们画下来、又不至于像真鸟绕着她的头颅飞翔，却颇费我一番工夫。宝座两侧那些花瓶与陈设，也都摆放得丝毫不差地等距，但又不得不照此画来。若不如此，便「不合规矩」了。人像位于三扇屏风正中，与之相距甚近，背景里全然透不出一丝空濛的气韵。袍服与衣袖竟无一褶皱；然我如今既已向成规与旧传统低头，便也机械地照着眼前所陈一一描摹下来，再不作任何艺术布局上的努力，笔下亦不另作新样的尝试。我像一个本分的匠人那样作画，每日完工若干英寸便罢。

===Original===
The weather was now too cold for anything but the short constitutional, and, besides, there was no place in the Winter Palace to tempt one to promenades—only the walled-in courts and the shut-in walks, between high walls. Even Her Majesty’s promenades were confined to going to the Audience Hall in the morning, and walking through the courts, from one Throne-room to another.

Every day we saw the Empress Dowager for some moments in her Throne-room before I went to my work. On Theater days, I made her my morning salutation in her loge at the Theater, and when the light faded and I could paint no more, I would go into the young Empress’s and the Ladies’ loge for the last play and the spectacular finale, when there were always some good illuminations and pretty effects. Her Majesty and the young Empress seemed now to perfectly understand that I wanted to work, and must work, in order to finish the large portrait for the St. Louis Exposition. They saw I appreciated the amusements and ceremonies, etc, but that I did not wish them to interfere with my work. When there was a special festival, or some fine ceremony, I was always called in, but otherwise I might go or not, as I wished.

===Chinese===
此时天气已冷得厉害，除了短短地散一回步以舒筋骨，再不能做什么；况且紫禁城（Winter Palace）之内，亦无可诱人游赏之处——不过是被高墙围起来的几重院落，以及夹在几道高墙之间的逼仄游廊。便是皇太后散步，也只限于清晨往觐见殿（Audience Hall）去这一程，再就是由一座宝座殿（Throne-room）穿过庭院，走到另一座宝座殿而已。

每日我去作画之前，先到太后的宝座殿中见她片刻。遇着唱戏之日，我便到她在戏台（Theater）上的看阁（loge）里请晨安；待到日色渐暗、不能再画之时，我便到皇后（the Young Empress）与女官（Ladies）们的看阁里去，看最后一出戏与那排场浩大的煞尾——届时总有些好看的灯彩与精巧的景致。皇太后与皇后如今似已完全明白，我想要作画，也必须作画，方能将圣路易斯博览会的那幅大御容赶制完工。她们看出我虽欣赏这些游乐与典礼等等，却不愿它们扰了我的活计。每逢特别的节庆或盛大的典礼，总会来叫我；除此之外，去不去皆由我自便。

===Original===
I lunched generally with the Ladies, with the charming young Empress as gracious hostess, and dined at night at Her Majesty’s table. Two huge copper braziers had now been placed in the Throne-room, and though so picturesque with the blue flames curling above their openings in the top, they made but little impression upon the temperature of this lofty room. The curtains over the immense doors that opened on the courts were constantly being raised for the passage of some eunuch, and it was very drafty. But one could at least warm one’s hands by the braziers, and they were so beautiful and picturesque, I was reconciled to being a little cold; besides, I soon became accustomed to the temperature. The Chinese Ladies wear heavy fur-lined dresses in the house, and cannot stand the rooms very warm.

At dinner, a large carpet was now placed under the table, which was an improvement over the cold marble floors. This was done for my comfort, for the Chinese Ladies wear two-inch-thick cork soles to their fur-lined shoes. Down the center of the table, during the winter, there were several silver chafing-dishes, with burning charcoal beneath their steaming contents. Soups, vegetables, and meat stews were thus kept boiling hot on the table. One night I suggested to one of the eunuchs to place the claret-bottle near the fire before serving it, that the chill might be taken off. One very cold day, soon after, the eunuch brought in a large teapot, and began pouring the boiling claret out of this! The Chinese drink their wines hot, and he thought he would improve on my suggestion of “taking off the chill,” and he naively remarked “it was better for me to drink it thus on such a cold day!”

===Chinese===
午饭我多与女官们同用，由那位落落大方、妩媚动人的皇后做殷勤的主人；晚饭则在皇太后桌上用。宝座殿里如今添了两只大铜火盆，盆口的火苗幽蓝，卷曲而上，煞是好看，然对于这间高旷殿宇的温度，却几乎无济于事。那些开向庭院的硕大门扇上所挂的门帘，因有太监不停出入，须臾便要掀起一回，穿堂风甚是凛冽。好在火盆旁至少还能烘一烘手；它们又是那样华美、那样入画，我便也甘愿稍稍受些冷；况且不久我也就习惯了这温度。中国女眷在屋中皆着厚重的皮袍，受不得太暖的房间。

用晚饭时，桌下如今也铺上了一大幅地毯，比之冰凉的大理石地面要舒服许多。这是为我的舒适而设的——因中国女眷所穿的毛皮鞋，底下是两英寸厚的软木底。冬季里，餐桌正中要摆上几只银暖锅，底下烧着炭，锅里腾着热气。汤、菜蔬与炖肉，便这样在桌上保得滚热。一日傍晚，我向一名太监提议，可将红酒（claret）瓶先放在火旁温一温再上，以去其寒气。未几，遇着一个极冷的日子，那太监竟捧来一只大茶壶，将滚沸的红酒从壶里往杯中斟！中国人本就喝热酒，他自以为在我那「去去寒气」的话上又加了一层心思；还天真地说：「这样冷的天，我喝这滚热的，才相宜呢！」

===Original===
When there was no Theater, and it became too dark to paint, I would join the young Empress and Ladies in their sitting-room at the left of Her Majesty’s Throne-room and there await dinner. The young Empress would then teach me Chinese. She was very particular about my accent and seemed to take a real interest in my progress. The Chinese language is very difficult for a beginner, even for one who has a good ear, for the “tone” or inflection with which you pronounce the word may change its meaning. Sometimes one after the other of the Princesses would repeat the same word in different tones and make me repeat it and then give the meaning of each tone. They would sometimes make puns on words, or give me a string of difficult words for the accent and to improve my enunciation, as the French teach the children, “Trois gros rats dans trois gros trous.” When I would finally get quite tangled up with these words I would retaliate with “Peter Piper picked a peck of pickled peppers.” This would end the lesson for that day, for they would all try to say it and get so hilarious that there was no effort at further study, and dinner would be announced in the midst of the fun.

Sometimes the young Empress and the Ladies would play cards in the evenings. Her Majesty seemed only to like her fairy game! The cards were narrow slips of pasteboard with curious devices on each, but little more than an inch wide, and there were one hundred and fifty in a pack. I never succeeded in getting into the merits of the game. Sometimes when the Ladies felt industriously inclined, they would weave a kind of braid. The threads, gold, silver, or silk, were attached to the center of a wooden table and were weighted at the ends. They would weave these in and out into cunningly fashioned braids and ribbons. The Princesses did a great deal of beautiful embroidery, making their own shoes, which are of exquisitely embroidered satin, but they could not do this at night, for only candles are used in the two Peking Palaces, the Summer Palace being the only one in China lighted by electricity.

===Chinese===
逢到不唱戏、天又暗得不能再画的日子，我便到皇太后宝座殿左侧皇后与女官们的起居室里去，与她们同候开晚饭。其时皇后便教我中文。她对我的口音十分讲究，对我进益与否，似乎真心关切。中文于初学者而言极为艰难，即便耳朵灵光的人也不容易——因一个字你用什么「声调」、什么抑扬去念，意思便随之而变。有时公主（Princesses）们便一个挨一个，用不同的声调把同一个字念出来，叫我跟着念，再逐一告诉我每一种声调各是什么意思。她们有时拿字做双关戏谑，有时则抛给我一连串难念的字，练我的重音、正我的吐字，恰如法国人教孩童那般：「三只大老鼠，藏在三个大洞里（Trois gros rats dans trois gros trous）。」等我终于被这些字缠得舌头打结之时，我便回敬一句：「彼得·派珀拣了一撮腌胡椒（Peter Piper picked a peck of pickled peppers）。」这一日功课便到此为止了——因她们皆要跟着念这一句，念着念着便笑得前仰后合，再无心向学，晚饭也在这一片欢腾之中报了上来。

晚间，皇后与女官们有时也打牌。皇太后似乎只喜欢她那「神仙戏」！那牌是窄窄一条硬纸片，上绘稀奇的花样，宽不过一寸有余，一副共一百五十张。我始终未能摸出这牌的奥妙来。女官们若来了兴致，有时也编织一种绦子。金银丝线或丝线，系于一张木桌正中，线头各悬重物。她们把线穿插交织，编成种种精巧别致的绦带。公主们做着大量精美的绣活，自己所穿的鞋亦亲手而制——皆是绣工绝妙的缎面——但这等细活夜里却做不得，因北京两处宫殿里只点蜡烛，全中国唯有颐和园（Summer Palace）一处通了电灯。

===Original===
One night at dinner the young Empress asked me to come earlier than usual the next morning, as there was something she wished me to see. Several eunuchs were waiting at the gate of the Palace to conduct us to the young Empress when we arrived at nine o’clock the next morning, and I then learned this was her Birthday. I hurried in and found the Imperial Princess and all the Ladies of the Palace, besides a number of visitors, standing in front of the young Empress’s pavilion. They told me she had asked them to wait to present their congratulations until I came, and said that I was to go in first. I did so, and there, on a throne, sat the young Empress in full Court dress, wearing the Court coiffure, with its veil of pearls, which was most becoming to her narrow patrician face. She was looking very sweet and gracious and held out her tiny hand to me on my entrance. I bowed low over it and kissed it, and wished her from the bottom of my heart “ten thousand” years of happiness and all kinds of “felicitous omens.” I then started to move out, but she told me to remain in the room at one side and watch the Princesses and Ladies as they came in. Each made the prostrations before her and presented a jade ruyie,8 which she received with due ceremony—the same ceremony as for the Emperor’s and Empress Dowager’s Birthday!

===Chinese===
一日傍晚用饭时，皇后嘱我次日清晨比往日早些到，说有一样东西要我瞧。次日早晨九点我们到时，已有几名太监在宫门候着，引我们去见皇后。我这才知道，这一日原来是皇后的千秋（Birthday）。我急忙进去，只见大公主（Imperial Princess）和宫中一应女官，还有若干来客，都已站在皇后的阁前。她们告诉我，皇后吩咐她们先候着，待我到了再一同拜贺，又说我该先进去。我便依言先进去，只见皇后端坐于宝座之上，着全副朝服，戴着朝冠，冠上覆着珍珠面帘——这一身装束，最衬她那一张清秀狭长的贵胄之脸。她神情和悦可亲，我进去时便向我伸出她那只小小的手来。我深深俯下身去，吻了她的手，诚心诚意祝她「万年」福祉，并种种「吉祥佳兆」。我随即要退出去，她却叫我留在一旁，看公主与女官们依次进来。每一个人在她面前行跪拜之礼，献上一柄玉如意（jade ruyie），她便依足礼数接过——所行之礼，与皇上、皇太后万寿之时一般无二！

===Original===
But these winter days were not all given up to the Theater and festivals. There were some days of sadder import. Days of mourning were often celebrated at the Palace. The anniversary of the deaths of some Emperor or Ancestor was of frequent occurrence. It seemed to me they celebrated the anniversary of the death of every Emperor of the Dynasty! On these days there would be sacrifices at the ancestral tablets and religious ceremonies early in the mornings. The Empress Dowager and the whole Court would wear mourning for the day and there was never any sort of amusement. White, which is full mourning, is not worn on these anniversaries after the third, but violet and blue (second mourning) is put on. The flowers worn in the coiffure were also in violet, white or blue, the mourning colors. One night at dinner the young Empress, who acts as Mistress of Ceremonies in the Palace, told me the following was a day of mourning. She asked me if I would wear one of the mourning colors, as it was the anniversary of the death of the Emperor Tung-Chih (the Empress Dowager’s son).

The next day I put on a black dress, our mourning, and wore violet flowers in my hair. When we entered, Her Majesty was sacrificing at the small shrine in her sitting-room. She was dressed in dark violet, heavily trimmed with black, and had not a flower of any kind in her hair—only a few pearls. She looked very sad and was more earnest and reverent at the sacrifice than usual, but when she had finished her sacrifice, she bade us “Good morning” and inquired after our health, with her usual consideration. We soon left the Throne-room for my working-hall, and I did not see her again until after our dinner with the Empress and Ladies, when we went into the Throne-room to make our adieus. As I had not been wearing black for some time (as Her Majesty said she didn’t like it), she now noticed that I had it on and she asked Lady Yu-Keng, in an aside, “why.” She was told that when I knew what anniversary it was, I had put it on on that account. She seemed much touched, took my hand in both hers, and said, “You have a good heart to think of my grief and to have wished to sympathize,” and tears fell from her eyes on my hand, which she held in hers.

===Chinese===
然而这些冬日，并不全交付给了戏台与节庆。也有几个意绪悲凉的日子。宫中常常过忌辰。某位先帝或某位祖先的冥诞忌日，时有举行。我瞧着，简直本朝每一位先帝的忌日他们都要过！每逢此等日子，清晨一早便要在神主牌位前献祭，行种种祭礼。皇太后与全宫廷皆着素服一日，绝无任何娱乐。素白乃大丧之服，第三次以后的忌辰便不再穿，改穿紫与蓝（即期服之色）。头饰上所戴的花，也用紫、白、蓝这几种素色。一日傍晚用饭时，皇后——她在宫中司礼——告诉我次日是个忌辰。她问我可愿着一种素色，因那一日是同治皇帝（Emperor Tung-Chih，即皇太后之子）的忌日。

次日我穿了一件黑衣——我们的素服——发间簪了几朵紫花。我们进去时，皇太后正在她起居室里的小神龛前上祭。她身穿深紫，缘边重重镶着黑，发间不簪一朵花——只缀着几颗珍珠。她神情甚是哀戚，献祭之时比往日更肃穆更虔诚；待祭毕，她照旧与我们道「早安」，又照素日的体贴问我们安。我们不久便离开宝座殿，到我的画室去。直到与皇后、女官们用罢晚饭，再到宝座殿辞驾之时，我才又见到她。因我已有一阵子不着黑（皇太后说过不喜欢），此时她留意到我身上穿黑，便低声问裕庚夫人（Lady Yu-Keng）「这是为何」。裕庚夫人告诉她：我知道了今日是什么忌辰，特地为此穿的。她似大为感动，双手将我一只手握住，说道：「你能念着我的伤心，又肯与我分忧，你这心肠真好。」说着，泪珠便从她眼中落下，滴在我那只被她握着的手上。

===Original===
