Chinese architecture—and one grows to admire it very much when studied in its own environment—is tent-building, carried to its greatest perfection and made enduring by the use of materials that last. The grouping of the Chinese buildings is on the same order as the congregation of tents of some roving tribe of Nomads. The downward curve and upturned tilt of their roofs is but the natural slope of the canvas and its uplifting by the tent-poles. These slender tent-poles have developed into the supporting pillars of the verandah, and the raised canvas door of the tent has grown into its buoyantly curved roof. The ornamental eaves are but the solidified silken fringes and embroidered valances of the tents of old. The curious roof ornamentations of the modern Chinese house replace the weights that held the tent-canvas steady. These weights, from rough stones, have now become carven images, cunningly wrought. The Chinese even erect their houses as their ancestors raised their tents. The builder places the columns and puts on the roof before the walls are built. Except the pagoda—and this even seems like so many superposed tents—the Chinese building remains today, in spite of its elaborate roofs, its lacquered pillars, and elaborate ornamentation, like some splendid tent, grown into greater fixity and beautified by some magician’s wand. It is admirably suited to the calm pastoral landscapes in which it rests, and seems a part of Nature itself, and is never out of keeping with its surroundings!

===Chinese===
中国建筑——若在其本来的环境中细细观摩，便会逐渐深为欣赏——实乃帐幕之建筑，发展至极致，并以经久的材料使之坚固长存。中国建筑的群组布局，与游牧部落帐幕的鳞次栉比，遵循的乃是同一种章法。屋顶向下弯垂、两端翘起的弧线，不过是篷布自然的坡垂以及立柱将其撑起之状。那细长的帐幕立柱，已演化为游廊下支撑的柱列；而帐幕门口撩起的篷布，则长成了那轻盈上扬的弧形屋顶。华美的檐头，不过是旧时帐幕上的丝质流苏与绣幔凝固而成的模样。今日中国民居屋顶上那些奇特的饰物，所取代的正是当年压住篷布使其稳固的重物。这些重物由粗陋的石块，已变为精雕细琢的造像。中国人盖房子，至今仍如其先辈之竖立帐幕。工匠先立柱、上屋顶，而后才砌墙。除却宝塔——便连宝塔也仿佛是一层层叠摞起来的帐幕——中国建筑时至今日，尽管有了繁复的屋顶、漆柱和华美的装饰，仍像一顶华美的帐幕，只是被某位术士的魔杖点化得更加稳固、更加华美罢了。它与所安处的宁静田园山水异常相宜，仿佛是自然本身的一部分，与其周遭的环境绝无一丝违和！

===Original===
Wherever available, in the grounds of the Summer Palace, flowers are planted, and they succeed each other almost the whole year round, for the Chinese are wonderful gardeners. The extensive grounds are, however, not given up entirely to flowers and beautiful constructions; there are great fields of grain. Wheat and millet, and even vegetables, are raised in these pleasure grounds. It was curious to me to see how picturesque so prosaic a thing as a field of turnips might become, when properly placed in a large pleasure domain. By the planting of these useful crops, a great deal of fertile land is utilized, without any detriment to the landscape, and the utilitarian spirit, so strong in the Chinese, is satisfied.

===Chinese===
凡是可以利用之处，颐和园的苑囿里都栽满了花，且几乎一年到头此花谢而彼花开，前后相继，因中国人是极高明的园艺家。然而这片广阔的苑囿并非全然献与花卉与华美的建筑——其间还有大片的庄稼地。这些游乐之苑中也种着麦、粟，甚至蔬菜。在我看来颇为新奇的是：像萝卜地这般乏味的事物，若布置得宜、置於一片广阔的御苑之中，竟也能显出如画的意趣。藉着栽种这些实用的庄稼，大片肥沃的土地得以利用，於风景并无丝毫损伤，而中国人素来强烈的务实之心，也由此得到满足。

===Original===
There is one terraced hillside in the grounds of the Summer Palace, called the “Flowery Mountain.” In the season of the peonies, which the Chinese call “The King of Flowers,” this is really a flowery mountain—one mass of blooms of exquisitely blended colors and faint evanescent perfume. The China Aster is also brought to great perfection by the Chinese gardeners, and in the time of the chrysanthemum the grounds fairly blaze with this autumnal glory. The Chinese do not go in for the cultivation of the chrysanthemum of extraordinary size. Her Majesty does not care much for these; but her gardeners arrive at some wonderful combinations of colors and some most curious shapes. The year I was in the Palace, Her Majesty was delighted with a beautiful green variety, that the gardeners had succeeded in getting, and that year there was also a new variety whose petals were like threads, they were so thin and hairlike.

===Chinese===
颐和园苑囿中有一处呈阶状的山坡，名为「花山」。牡丹盛放之时——中国人称之为「花中之王」——这确是一座花山——一片繁花之海，色彩调配精妙，香气幽淡而转瞬即逝。中国的园艺家所培植的翠菊也十分出色，至菊开时节，苑中简直为这秋日的盛景所点燃。中国人并不着意培植特大花朵的菊花。皇太后并不甚喜此等大花；但她的园艺家却能造就若干奇妙的色彩搭配与极其别致的形态。我在宫中的那一年，皇太后对一种秀美的绿色品种尤为欢喜，那是园艺家们培植而成的新品；那年另有一种新品，其花瓣细如游丝，薄如发缕。

===Original===
Old Ruins in the Summer Palace

===Chinese===
颐和园中的旧日遗迹

===Original===
The Temple of the Ten Thousand Buddhas is so-called from its being built in glazed yellow tiles, each representing a niche, in which is seated a Buddha, of which there are many more than ten thousand. The interior is composed of three chapels. In the central one thrones the Great Buddha. There was another famous Buddha in this temple, which was invested with peculiarly sacred qualities, but it was hurled into the lake below and broken into a thousand pieces when the foreign troops were in possession in 1900. Her Majesty seemed to feel the depredations to the temples, by the foreigners, more keenly than anything else. The Chinese are so perfectly tolerant in matters of religion, they cannot understand our attitude toward any other religion but our own, and our contempt for any other kind of worship except that in which we ourselves indulge.

===Chinese===
万佛寺之所以得名，是因其以黄色的琉璃砖所建，每一块砖代表一个佛龛，龛中各坐一尊佛像，其数远多於一万。寺内分为三座佛堂。正中一间高踞其上的，是大佛。寺中本还有另一尊著名的佛像，被视为具有特别的神圣特质，但 1900 年外国军队占领此地时，它被抛入山下湖中，摔得粉碎。对於外国人劫毁寺庙一事，皇太后似比对其余任何事都更为痛心。中国人於宗教之事最为宽容，他们全然不能理解我们对自身所奉宗教之外其他宗教的态度，也不能理解我们对自身所行之外其他礼拜方式的轻蔑。

===Original===
The Chinese are said to hate the foreigner. They certainly have not much reason to like him; nor to admire our much vaunted civilization. The European Christian soldier in China has burned, destroyed, and killed with as much barbarity as the heathen, and in many instances has given the latter points in cruelty.

===Chinese===
据说中国人憎恶外国人。他们确也无多少理由喜欢外国人，亦无理由仰慕我们自诩的文明。在中国，信奉基督教的欧洲士兵烧杀毁掠，其野蛮并不亚於他们所称的「异教徒」，在残忍一事上，许多时候反倒给后者作出了榜样。

===Original===
On the slope behind the terraced hill of the Ten Thousand Buddhas are the ruins of the old Summer Palace, destroyed by the European troops fifty years ago. After this, the site of the dwelling Palaces was changed, and they were massed on the southern side of the lake. Her Majesty has nearly hidden all trace of the 1900 devastations to the Summer Palace, but these old ruins of the former Palace still remain, and they are not a blot upon the landscape. On the contrary, they have become picturesque with time, and give the one note of somberness to this smiling demesne that is needed to accentuate its charm. There is a small lake not far from these old ruins, built around with smiling pavilions and a curious tower-like construction which is used as a private temple. There are landing-places and small boats. It looks like a charming bit of old Venice. We never went here, however, but once. There are some unhappy associations connected with this beautiful spot, and Her Majesty did not seem to care to visit it. The promenade in the direction of the old Palace also seemed to sadden her, for she had passed the early years of her married life in these now crumbling ruins.

===Chinese===
在万佛寺那层叠的山后山坡上，是旧日夏宫的遗迹，五十年前为欧洲军队所毁。自此以后，居住宫苑的基址便改换了，集中建於湖的南岸。颐和园 1900 年所遭的毁坏，皇太后已几乎将其痕迹全数掩去；但那座旧宫的遗迹犹存，且并非风景上的瑕疵。相反，岁月使之别具画意，为这一片明媚的苑囿添上了一抹所需的沉郁色调，反倒使其风致更为突出。距这些旧迹不远，有一座小湖，湖周环以明丽的亭阁，另有一座奇特的塔形建筑，用作私人的庙宇。湖边有码头，湖上有小舟。它仿佛是一小片迷人的旧日威尼斯。然而我们只去过那里一次。这处胜景牵连着若干不快的回忆，皇太后似乎并不愿往游。朝旧宫方向的散步似乎也令她神伤，因为她婚后的早年岁月，便是在这些如今已倾颓的废墟之中度过的。

===Original===
From the highest elevations in the grounds of the Summer Palace, we could see the road from Peking! Sometimes Her Majesty and the Ladies would watch from some of the summerhouses, the carts and chairs and vehicles as they passed along. Several times we saw the Emperor and his suite returning from some ceremony in Peking, over the road cleared for his passage. Her Majesty, herself, would be the first to descry him, and she would say, “The Emperor comes.” Then the Empress and Ladies would all look, for it was not against the Proprieties for them to look at His Majesty at such a distance. These views of the high road from the eminences of the Summer Palace were all Her Majesty and the young Empress ever saw of the outside world and common humanity; for neither at the Winter nor Sea Palaces could they get any views from a distance, nor was there any opportunity of seeing beyond the walls. When Their Chinese Majesties go abroad—and this is generally only from one Palace to another—quaint, triangular flags are placed along the Imperial route, warning the people that Their Sacred Majesties are to pass, and that the road will be reserved for them between certain hours. No vehicles or pedestrians are allowed for some time before and after the Imperial passage. In the City of Peking, the inhabitants, even on the streets where the Imperial cortège is to pass, are shut into their houses and not allowed to go out of their doors during the time, and at the intersection of the transversal streets huge curtains are hung, shutting them off from the Imperial way. For these progresses of Their Majesties, the roads are covered with yellow sand.

===Chinese===
自颐和园园内最高的几处眺望，可以望见自北京来的那条路！有时皇太后与命妇们会在某处凉亭里，眺望大车、轿子和各色车辆在路上来往。我们曾数次望见皇上及其随扈，自北京某处典礼归来，沿着为他清出的道路行进。皇太后总是头一个望见他，便会说：「皇上回来了。」於是皇后与众命妇便都去看，因为隔着那般距离，望一望皇上并不算违礼。自颐和园高处所见的这条大道，便是皇太后与皇后所能见到的外部世界与凡俗人世的全部；因为在紫禁城与西苑两处宫苑，她们都无法远眺，也没有任何机会望见墙外之物。皇上、皇太后出行时——大抵只是由一处宫苑移往另一处宫苑——沿途御道上便会插起古雅的三角旗，告示百姓「两宫圣驾将行」，道路将在特定时辰内为他们清出。圣驾经过前后的一段时间内，一应车马行人皆不得通行。在北京城内，即便是在御驾所要经过的街道上，居民也被禁闭於家中，在此期间不得出门；於横向街口的交汇处，则悬挂起巨大的帷幕，将这些路口与御道隔开。凡皇上、皇太后出行，所过道路皆铺以黄沙。

## XXIV The Winter Palace / 二十四 紫禁城

===Original===
The Summer Palace was always the Empress Dowager's favorite Palace, but after the Boxer rising and the subsequent occupation of Peking by the Allies, when foreign troops were stationed in both the Peking Palaces, and so much damage done them, she would have preferred to have lived the whole year round at the Summer Palace. As it is, she occupies it from eight to nine months of the year, going out to it at the first opportunity in the spring, and leaving it only when it is so cold as to make it impracticable. There is a system of heating it by furnaces beneath the floors, but Her Majesty never used these, and the small Chinese porcelain stoves, sorts of braziers, were quite insufficient for heating the immense halls. This, however, would not have influenced her, as she never minded the cold, but it was very difficult for the officials to take the long trip to the Summer Palace during the winter, and this consideration alone caused her to move into the Winter Palace when the weather became very cold. The members of the Cabinet and the Princes had summer homes in the immediate vicinity of the Palace, but there were thousands of officials who were obliged to come out every day from Peking.

The time had now come for the Court to move in definitively to the Winter Palace, and shortly after the Birthday festivities, Their Majesties took up their residence in the Capital. Before I left the Summer Palace, the young Empress suggested that I should go to the Winter Palace the next day in time to assist in receiving Her Majesty on her arrival there, for, as usual, I left the Summer Palace the day before the Court, and went in to the United States Legation. At every change of residence of the Empress Dowager, the young Empress, Princesses, and Ladies of the Court precede her by a few hours, and stand upon the threshold of her own dwelling Palace to receive her when she arrives. Full Court dress is worn for this reception, and it is, as is everything touching Her Majesty, a ceremony!

===Chinese===
颐和园（Summer Palace）向来是皇太后最心爱的宫苑，然而自庚子义和团之乱、联军随后占据北京（Peking）之后，外国军队驻扎在京中两处宫城，宫苑损毁甚巨，她原是恨不得一年到头都住在颐和园里的。如今她一年之中倒也有八九个月住在此处，一到开春便趁早出城，直到天寒地冻、再住下去实属不便，方才离开。园中本有地下火炉供暖一套法子，皇太后却从不一用；那些中国式的瓷质小炉子，形如火盆，要烘暖那些高大的殿宇，实在力有不逮。然而纵使如此，也并不能动摇太后——她本不畏寒；只是到了冬天，百官要跋涉长路赶赴颐和园，实在太过辛苦，单为这一桩，天气一冷，她便移居紫禁城（Winter Palace）。内阁大学士与王公们在颐和园左近都置有别业，可还有成千上万的官员，每日都得从北京城里赶出来。

如今宫里是要正式搬进紫禁城了。万寿庆典过后不久，两位陛下便起驾入京驻跸。我离开颐和园之前，皇后（the young Empress）便吩咐我，次日须赶在太后銮驾抵达之前先到紫禁城，好随着众人一同迎驾；因我照例比宫里早一日出园，先行回到美国公使馆（the United States Legation）。每逢皇太后移跸迁居，皇后、福晋格格与诸宫眷必先太后数个时辰启程，预先候于太后新驻宫苑的门槛之内，俟她老人家驾到便出迎。迎驾之时须衣冠朝服齐整——凡是与太后相关的，哪一桩都是仪制，此事自不例外！

===Original===
The day of the Empress Dowager's entrance into her loyal City of Peking for the winter, in December, 1903, was a typical Peking winter day; the air was crisp and clear, the atmosphere positively sparkling, and like champagne. One seemed to breathe an elixir. For her progresses from one Palace to another the Empress Dowager always had, what they call in England, "Queen's weather."

The City of Peking is composed of three walled towns—the Chinese, the Tartar, and the Imperial City. Within the Imperial City lies the Winter Palace, its battlemented, turreted walls surrounded by a moat. After passing through one of the great gates, in the wall surrounding the Imperial City, and crossing the stone bridge that spans "the Grain-bearing Canal," we soon came in sight of the splendid walls and lofty gates of the Palace enclosure. The red outer walls of the Palace, faded by Time and weather to a charming gray-pink, with their beautiful corner constructions of airy-looking turrets reflected in the still waters of the moat beneath, were most picturesque. We were carried along the raised road beyond the moat until we came to a marble bridge (formerly a portcullis), that leads into the gate of the Palace in front of the Manchu Banner quarters, at the foot of the Coal Hill. Our chairs, by special arrangement, were allowed to enter the enclosure proper, of the Winter Palace; but even after entering the exterior gates, one winds in and out between high walls, through massive gates and heavy wooden doors studded with huge iron nails and ornamental copper balls. Against the high wall on either side of this approach, wooden sheds were built as sleeping-places for the guards and soldiers. Each shed had a front of latticework, with paper pasted over the interstices. Within was a cemented platform, which the Northern Chinese use as beds. These have a place underneath for building a fire, for they keep warm at night by sleeping on hot beds and use very little cover.

===Chinese===
1903 年 12 月，皇太后入北京这座效忠于她的京城过冬，那一日正是一个典型的北京冬日：空气清冽澄澈，天色明净得几乎闪闪发光，犹如香槟一般。人仿佛吸进了一口琼浆玉液。皇太后自一处宫苑移跸另一处，每每都能遇上英国人所说的那种「女王天气」。

北京城由三重城墙圈起来的城池组成——汉城、鞑靼城（满城），以及皇城。紫禁城便坐落在皇城之内，城墙上雉堞连环、角楼耸峙，墙外环绕着护城河。穿过皇城城墙上一道大门，走过横跨「运粮河」的石桥，我们一行很快便望见宫城那壮丽的城墙与高耸的宫门。宫城的外墙本是红色，经岁月与风雨剥蚀，已褪成一种惹人喜爱的灰粉色；那美丽的角楼，构造轻灵，倒映在下方平静的护城河水中，景致如画。我们被人抬着沿护城河那边的垫高大路前行，直到一座汉白玉石桥跟前（此桥旧时原是一处闸门），由此进入煤山（the Coal Hill，即景山）脚下、八旗营房前面的宫门。承蒙特旨，我们的轿子准许抬进紫禁城的内里；然而纵使进了外城门，也得在高墙之间左穿右绕，穿过一道道厚重的门、一重重钉着大铁钉、镶着铜饰圆球的木门。这条甬道两侧高墙根下，搭着些木棚，是护军与兵丁歇宿之所。每座棚子前面都是木格窗棂，格眼里糊着纸。棚内是一方泥抹的土炕——北方中国人就以此为床。炕下有一处可烧火取暖，他们夜里全靠睡热炕御寒，身上盖的衣物极少。

===Original===
Just beyond the last of these guardhouses, our official "green chairs" were put down between two high walls, with forbidding gates in front of us. Here we took the red Palace chairs which were awaiting us. We were swiftly carried through still other gates and past a very labyrinth of walls. The courts were all paved in large flagstones of white marble, and surrounded by high walls with heavy doors. We finally reached a charming court, where, standing under the overhanging branches of a beautiful cedar, we found the young Empress and Princesses, in full Court dress, already awaiting the coming of Her Majesty. It was a pretty group that stood there, gowned in their splendid Court costumes, the sunlight glinting upon the jeweled crowns of their fur caps, and giving a touch of nature to the brilliant flowers in their hair. My plain, foreign, tailor-made gown was the only dark spot in this bright group of gorgeously attired ladies.

Presently the cymbals and flutes sounded the weird notes of the "Imperial Hymn." The great wooden doors of the court were thrown open and the Imperial procession came in sight. Splendidly gowned eunuchs advanced in two lines, walking with rigid bodies and stately step. At a sign from the young Empress, a hush fell upon the chattering group of Princesses and each took her proper place. Then the Imperial chair-bearers crossed the threshold, with Her Majesty sitting erect in one of her "open chairs," for as soon as she gets into the Palace grounds she leaves the closed palanquin, in which she is obliged to travel abroad and which she very much dislikes on account of its stuffiness. The Ladies, as if moved by one impulse, made the formal bow at her approach, and repeated the usual Imperial salutation "Lao-Tzu-Tzung Chee-Siang," which I repeated with the others. Her Majesty had her chair stopped in the center of the Court and got out, and I went up to salute her. She shook hands, and said she hoped I would be happy in the Winter Palace, but that it was a dull, depressing sort of a place, with too many walls and gates, after the open brightness, of the Summer Palace. After a few minutes' conversation she went into the Throne-room, followed by the Empress and Ladies.

===Chinese===
过了最后一座护军棚，我们坐的官用的「绿轿」便落在两道高墙之间，眼前是一道道森严的门。在此处我们换上等候着的红色宫轿。轿子飞快地抬过一道又一道门，绕过一重重如迷宫般的墙。院落都用大块的汉白玉铺地，四周都是高墙与厚重的门。末了我们来到一处极可爱的院子：只见一棵秀美的雪松，枝叶低垂如盖，树下站着皇后与众位福晋格格，都已朝服齐整，先在此恭候太后銮驾。这一群人立在那儿，煞是好看：个个身着华美的朝服，日光映在她们貂帽上镶珠嵌宝的冠饰上，闪闪烁烁，又为她们发间那姹紫嫣红的鲜花添了几分生意。这一群服饰绚丽的贵妇之中，唯我这身素净的外国式裁剪袍服，是其间唯一黯淡之处。

不一时，钹与笛奏起「国歌」那奇异的调子。院子那两扇厚重的木门訇然洞开，仪仗进入眼帘。衣着华美的太监（eunuchs）分两列前行，身板笔挺，步履庄严。皇后使个眼色，方才还在喁喁私语的福晋格格们顿时静了下来，各归本位。随即，抬御轿的轿夫跨过门槛——皇太后正襟端坐于一顶「敞轿」之中；因她一入宫禁，便舍了那出门必乘的封闭銮舆，那物闷得严严实实，她老人家最是厌弃。众位宫眷仿佛为同一冲动所驱，待她行近，便齐齐行那正式的礼，口诵惯常的御前问候「老祖宗吉祥」（Lao-Tzu-Tzung Chee-Siang），我也随众人一同念诵。皇太后吩咐停轿于院子正中，下了轿，我便上前请安。她与我握手，说但愿我在紫禁城中过得顺心如意，只是此处是个沉闷压抑的所在，墙也太多、门也太多，远不及颐和园的开朗明亮。闲谈几句之后，她便进了宝座殿，皇后与众宫眷随之而入。

===Original===
Her Majesty's Throne-room at the Winter Palace fronted on a court which was surrounded by well-built walls with curiously shaped doors and windows and ornamental yellow and green tiled designs at intervals. In the center of the wall in front was the immense gateway, with wooden folding-doors, which had just opened for her passage. The verandah of the Throne-room had two rooms projecting upon it, making of it a rectangular space with walls around three of its sides. This verandah was quite different from any at the Summer Palace, where they run the whole length of the buildings, back and front.

Entering, I was struck by the beauty of the great central hall—the harmony of its proportions, the somber splendor of its color. It seemed to me the most satisfying, the most picturesque of all the restful, harmonious Chinese interiors I had seen. Its dull red walls, splendid coffered ceiling glowing in color and glinting in gold, its central dome, with elaborately carved pendentives, was painted in brilliant primary colors, subdued into a rich harmony by the demi-obscurity, for it had no "lantern" and received its light from the windows below.

Court in the Winter Palace—"Her Majesty Comes"

===Chinese===
皇太后在紫禁城的宝座殿，面朝一处院落，院子四周是构筑齐整的高墙，门窗形制奇特，间隔处饰有黄绿两色的琉璃花饰。前墙正中是一道巨大的门洞，门扇为木制对开，方才正为她通行而洞开。宝座殿的廊下，有两间屋子凸出来伸到廊上，把这段廊子围成一处三面有墙的长方空间。这廊子与颐和园里的任何一处都大不相同——颐和园的廊子，前后都贯通整座殿宇的长度。

一走进去，我便为正中那座大殿之美所折服——比例之谐调，色彩之沉郁华美。在我所见过的那些静谧、谐调的中国式室内之中，这一处最叫人心满意足，最富画意。它那暗红的墙壁、华美的藻井——色彩绚烂，金光点点——以及正中的穹顶，其精雕细琢的帆拱，皆施以鲜艳的原色，却在半明半暗之中被调和成一片浓郁的和美；因殿内并无「宫灯」，光线全靠下方的窗子透进来。

紫禁城觐见——「太后驾到」

===Original===
The curious feature of the domes in several of the palaces in the Violet City, so effective from within, giving elevation and space to the interiors, is that they are not visible from the outside of the edifice. The beautiful straight line of the roof, with its upturned corners, remains intact in its purity and retains its restful simplicity.

The hall was paved with great blocks of highly polished black marble, which dimly reflected the glowing splendor of the walls and ceiling. In the center of one side was a low dais, richly carpeted, on which stood a great antique throne and footstool of red lacquer, framed in ebony and inlaid with cloisonné—the three-leaved screen behind was of bronze, with landscapes in low relief. On each leaf a poem in golden characters gave the needed touch of brilliancy to the somber massiveness of the dull bronze.

Great wooden doors, with huge gilded dragons in high relief, opened into apartments on the right and left of this splendid hall. These portals were always thrown wide, and heavily padded satin portières hung from the lintels. The front and rear of the hall was almost entirely of glass, with the pillars that supported the roof standing clear between the windows—the lower half of plate-glass, the upper, of transparent Korean paper.

===Chinese===
紫禁城（the Violet City）中有好几处宫殿都设穹顶，其奇特之处在于：自内观之，这些穹顶效果极佳，为室内平添了高度与空间感；然而自建筑外观望去，却全然不见其踪影。那屋脊秀美的直线，配以两端翘起的飞檐，丝毫不失其纯净，依旧保留着那份恬淡的素朴。

大殿地上铺着巨大的、打磨得光鉴可人的黑色大理石块，隐隐映出墙垣与天花板上那灼灼的华彩。一侧正中是一座低矮的宝座台，铺着厚厚的毯子，台上设着一张硕大的古色古香的红漆宝座与脚凳，以乌木为框，嵌着景泰蓝——其后的三扇屏风乃是青铜所制，上有浅浮雕山水。每一扇屏叶上都有一首金字题诗，恰为这暗青铜沉郁厚重的气派添了所需的一抹亮色。

厚重的木门，门上浮雕着巨大的鎏金蟠龙，高浮雕凸出，门开处便是这座华美殿堂左右的厢房。这些门洞总是大敞着，门楣上悬着厚厚夹衬的缎子帷幔。大殿前后几乎全是玻璃，承托屋顶的柱子则于窗间独立——下半截是平板玻璃，上半截则是半透明的高丽纸。

===Original===
The apartments to the right, where, at a sign from Her Majesty, I followed the Ladies, were her day-rooms. Her sitting-room, projecting on the verandah, brilliantly lighted by two sides of windows, was in dazzling contrast to the somber splendor of the Throne-room. The sun pouring through the windows, the gay flowers and growing plants, the fruits piled high in great painted bowls, the divans, beneath the windows, with satin cushions, the touches of femininity, the subtle perfume, even the small shrine to Buddha—everything bespoke the characteristics of its august mistress, who, in her hours of ease, loved sunshine and flowers, and reveled in beauty and perfume.

On entering, Her Majesty approached the small shrine, lighted three slender tapers of fragrant incense, and placed them upright in the perfumed ashes of the golden censer at the feet of Buddha. She rearranged the offerings, placed a picture of the Mother of Buddha behind the Image, and then stood in reverent attitude a few seconds before turning to her waiting tirewomen to have her outer garments removed.

===Chinese===
右侧那几间屋子，太后示意我随众宫眷同去，便是她日常起坐之所。她的起居室凸出于廊下，两面皆窗，光线明媚，与宝座殿那种沉郁华美恰成眩目的对照。日光从窗户倾泻而入，鲜艳的花朵、青葱的盆栽，硕大的彩绘碗中高高堆叠的鲜果，窗下铺着缎垫的软榻，处处可见的女性气息，那若有若无的幽香，乃至那一座小小的佛龛——无一不在诉说着这位至尊女主人的性情：她在悠闲之时，最爱的便是日光与鲜花，沉醉于美与芳香之中。

一进门，皇太后便走到那小佛龛前，点上三支纤细的香，将它们端端正正插在佛足前那只金香炉中盛满的香灰里。她重新摆弄了一番供品，将一张佛母（the Mother of Buddha）的画像置于佛像之后，然后恭恭敬敬地立了几息，这才转身让候着的侍女替她宽去外衣。

===Original===
As I had now learned that my interest in her surroundings pleased her, I looked around the room. It was as lofty as the Great Throne-room, but the rear wall was divided into two stories, and a hidden stairway led to the upper rooms. In an alcove, under the second floor, was built the bed where she took her siesta in the afternoon, screened from the sitting-room by beautifully embroidered satin curtains. The walls of carved teakwood had a rare frieze of panels of flying birds and bats in mother-of-pearl. There were scrolls bearing quotations from the classics; and, of course, many beautiful and curious clocks adorned the dragon tables, the window-seats, and carved chests!

In prominent places, each flanked by good-luck pennants, hung two steel-engravings: the first representing Queen Victoria in regal array; the second, the Queen and Prince Consort, surrounded by their children and grandchildren. I was surprised to see them here in Her Majesty's living-room, though I had heard that the Empress Dowager had a great admiration for the Queen, and that she thought there were many points of similarity in their reigns. They had each been widows the greater part of their lives, and had each ruled over great empires. She said she noticed in the Queen's face the same lines of longevity that she, herself, had. She probably dreams of as long a life as the great Queen of England had.

===Chinese===
我既已知道，我对她周遭物事留心观瞧，她老人家心里是欢喜的，便将这屋里打量了一番。这屋子与正大殿一样高大，只是后墙分作上下两层，一道暗梯通到楼上的房间。楼下壁凹之内，设着一张床，是她午后小憩之所，与起居室之间用绣工精美的缎帘隔开。雕花的柚木墙上，有一圈稀世的雕饰带，每一格都镶嵌着螺钿的飞鸟与蝙蝠。墙上挂着抄录经史子集的字画；更有许多精美而奇巧的钟表，陈设在雕龙案上、窗座上与雕花的柜子上——这是自然！

显眼之处各挂着两幅钢版画，每幅两侧皆缀着祈福的幡旗：头一幅画的是维多利亚女王（Queen Victoria）盛装御容；第二幅则是女王与王夫，为他们的子女孙辈所环绕。皇太后的起居室里竟挂着这些画像，我看了未免惊讶；虽说我也曾听闻，太后对这位女王极为景仰，以为自己与女王的在位多有相似之处。她们俩大半生都是寡居，又都统治着泱泱帝国。她说她瞧见女王面上也有她自己那样的长寿之纹。她大抵盼着也能如那位英国伟大的女王一般高寿。

===Original===
The Empress Dowager was astonished that I had seen so many members of the English Royal family, and the Queen herself, when I had never had an "Audience," and was still more so when she learned that the Great English Empress took her daily promenade outside her Palace Walls in "an open chair," and could be seen by anyone who happened to pass that way.

Her Majesty told me I might go up the hidden stairway, leading from her bed-alcove to the floor above, where was her private chapel. Here, on special occasions, services were held by lama priests. It was a beautiful haven, in whose dim, religious light one might meditate or pray.

Its high altar, with a great golden Buddha of fine design, had tall, golden candlesticks, shining with pearls and rubies. Richly wrought and enameled vases held bouquets of jeweled flowers, and censers, damascened with gold, sent up spirals of perfumed smoke. The floor was covered with a splendid silken rug of Imperial yellow, and small, exquisitely executed, paintings of the saints and personified attributes formed a dado around the walls.

===Chinese===
皇太后十分诧异：我既未曾正式「觐见」（Audience），何以竟能见过这么多英国王室成员，连女王本人也见着了。待她得知这位英国女皇每日竟坐着「敞轿」在宫墙之外散步，凡路过之人皆可一瞻御容，她更是吃惊不置。

皇太后允我沿着那道暗梯上去——暗梯从她的床龛通往楼上，楼上便是她的私人佛堂。遇有节庆，便由喇嘛僧众来此诵经。这是一处静美的所在，在那幽暗的宗教光影之中，人尽可冥想，或祈祷。

那高高的供案之上，供着一尊造型精美的大金佛，两旁立着高高的金烛台，其上珠光宝气，红蓝宝石闪闪发亮。雕工繁复、珐琅斑斓的花瓶里，供着一束一束珠玉缀成的花朵；鎏金的香炉里，袅袅升起一圈一圈的香烟。地上铺着一方华美的明黄色丝毯；墙上有一圈护墙饰带，画的是诸位圣者与拟人化的诸般德相，幅幅皆绘制精妙。

===Original===
Curiously shaped windows, with bits of translucent shell set into the elaborate latticework, shed but a dim light, and out of mysterious depths shone the splendid jewels of the altar ornaments, the dull gold of the Great Buddha, and the gleaming dado of red-and-gold clothed saints! This was Her Majesty's favorite chapel. She had followed me up and showed it with pride. She appreciated its perfect artistic quality as much, I am sure, as she loved its religious element.

Here she could come, from the privacy of her bed-alcove, mount the hidden stairs when she willed, unnoticed and unattended, and here seek that peace which seemed so far away those troubled days of January, 1904, when all looked so dark for her country.

Her Majesty's Throne-room is in the first of three large halls in the northeastern corner of the enclosure, which, with their courts, extend to the exterior walls of the Palace. The buildings are raised about eight feet above the marble-paved court and are approached by handsome, white marble steps. Leading up to the second, for the first time I saw a "spirit-stairway" used in secular architecture. This "spirit-stairway" consists of a block of marble placed in the center and reaching from the top to the bottom of the stairway. This block, instead of being cut into steps, is elaborately carved with the double dragon. It lies in the middle of the stairway like a beautiful heavy carpet thrown over it, too stiff to take the form of steps. The "spirit-stairway," not to be touched by mortal feet, is used in the approaches to all the fine temples; and when, as in the case of the Temple of Heaven at Peking, the stairs are high, the effect is as beautiful as it is original and unique.

Confucian Temple—Spirit-Stairway in Central Flight of Steps

===Chinese===
窗子形制奇特，精巧的棂格间嵌着一片片半透明的贝壳，只透进一线幽光；自那幽深的暗处，闪出供案上宝饰的珠光、大佛那黯淡的金光，以及那幅红金两色衣袍的圣者护墙饰带的微光！这便是皇太后最钟爱的佛堂。她也跟着我上来，面有得色地把这地方指给我看。这一处完美的艺术造诣，我想，她之赏鉴，绝不亚于她对其宗教意蕴的钟爱。

此处，她可由自己那幽密的床龛中走来，随心登上那道暗梯，无人察觉，无人随侍，到此寻求一份安宁——而这份安宁，在 1904 年 1 月那段多事之秋里，似乎离她如此遥远，那时，她的国运看起来是那样黯淡。

皇太后的宝座殿，在宫城东北角三座大殿之中的头一座；这三座大殿连同各自的院落，一路延伸到宫城的外墙。殿宇高出汉白玉铺就的院子约八尺许，前面有华美的汉白玉石阶相通。登上第二座殿的台阶时，我头一遭见到「御路」（spirit-stairway，即石雕螭龙御路）用于宫殿建筑之中。这「御路」乃是一整块汉白玉，置于台阶正中，自顶到底贯下来。这一块石头并不凿成阶梯，而是精雕着双龙戏珠。它卧在台阶正中，犹如一块华美的厚重地毯铺搭其上，因过于方正刚硬，无法顺台阶成形——故名「御路」。这「御路」不许凡人之足践踏，凡精美神庙之前皆有此制；如北京天坛（the Temple of Heaven）之阶既高，其效既美，又是别出心裁、独一无二。

孔庙——中阶御路石

===Original===
The hall with the "spirit-stairway" is the handsomest of the three in the Empress Dowager's enclosure. Its interior, a height of fifty feet, has a splendid coffered ceiling, and its walls are of wonderfully carved wood, with cloisonné medallions, which give great richness and splendor. A balcony surrounds this lofty hall, with openings from it into rooms over the side apartments, which are of but the usual height. This great front hall, with a dais and throne, screen and ceremonial fans, showed it was for more formal receptions than the beautiful domed room we had first entered. Opposite the Throne dais stood a cistern of splendidly carved jade to hold water for cooling the temperature in summer. A handsome music-box, which had been sent as a present to the Dowager Empress by Queen Victoria, and several other presents from European Royalties, stood around. The apartments on the right were for His Majesty's use when he came to the Theater, which was near. On the left were Her Majesty's night apartments. Two doors led through the openwork screen which separated the hall from the entrance at the rear. Here there was another magnificent block of jade, about five feet high, elaborately carved in designs representing the manner in which the jade is mined and taken from its native mountains.

From the central hall, a raised marble platform led into the third of the buildings. Here, again, the central hall occupied the entire height, while the sides were divided into two stories. This was one of the Emperor's Throne-rooms, and he had graciously given it for my use while painting the Empress Dowager's portraits. I had been told I was to have a "magnificent place for working" in the Winter Palace, and so far as magnificence went, I had it here. But, lofty and spacious as the hall was, it was very dark, and there was also a disagreeable reflection from the shining, yellow-tiled roof of the Palace in front. The court was very small, and the reflection from the roof was consequently unavoidable. My heart fell. It was a dreadful disappointment to find that my studio, to which I had so looked forward, was so unsatisfactory as to light!

===Chinese===
设「御路」的那座大殿，是太后宫苑内三座殿中最为华丽的一座。殿内高约五十尺，上有华美的藻井，四周是雕工奇绝的木墙，间隔镶嵌着景泰蓝的圆饰，气象华贵，富丽堂皇。这座高大的殿宇四周环有走廊，由走廊可通入两侧厢房之上的房间——厢房本身则只是寻常高度。这座前殿设有宝座台、御座、屏风与仪扇，可见其用于比我们初入那座华美穹顶殿更为正式的接见。御座台对面，放着一只雕工极美的玉缸，用以盛水，于夏日纳凉降温。一只华美的八音盒——乃是维多利亚女王赠予皇太后的礼物——以及欧洲几位王室所赠的其他礼物，都陈列在侧。右侧的厢房，是皇上（His Majesty）到邻近戏楼听戏时所用。左侧便是皇太后的寝宫。两道门透过镂空的屏风隔断，将正殿与后头的入口连通。此处另有一块气象宏大的玉石，约五尺来高，上面精雕细琢，刻的是采玉与自原生山中将玉运出的种种情景。

由正殿出来，一道垫高的汉白玉石台通入第三座殿宇。此处仍是正中通高、两侧分作上下两层的格局。这原是皇上的（the Emperor）一处宝座殿，蒙他恩典，特拨给我为皇太后绘像之用。我曾被告知，在紫禁城中我必有一处「华美的作画之所」，若单论华美，此地诚然不假。然而殿宇虽高大宽敞，却十分昏暗，加上对面宫殿那闪亮的黄琉璃瓦顶的反光，颇令人不适。院子很小，故而那瓦顶的反光无从回避。我的心一沉。我满心期盼的画室，光线竟如此不如人意，真叫人大失所望！

===Original===
The Empress Dowager's quarters at the Winter Palace are separated by high walls and guarded gates from the Emperor's. The pavilions of the Emperor's enclosure are on a more magnificent scale even than those of the Empress Dowager. The Audience Hall of the Winter Palace is in the Emperor's enclosure. In Her Majesty's enclosure, there is a Theater, but the Imperial loge is small, indeed, when compared with the splendid hall at the Summer Palace. Tradition seemed to be more rigidly observed here than at the Summer Palace, and everything seemed to be referred to the Emperor; whereas Her Majesty seemed to be the first figure at the Summer Palace, and there, traditional laws were often in abeyance.

===Chinese===
皇太后在紫禁城中的宫苑，与皇上的宫苑之间，隔着高墙与有人把守的宫门。皇上宫苑里的殿宇，气派甚至比太后的还要宏丽。紫禁城的觐见殿（the Audience Hall）便设在皇上的宫苑之内。皇太后的宫苑里也有一处戏楼，只是那御用看阁与颐和园那座华美的戏殿一比，委实小得很。此地的旧例似乎比颐和园守得更严，凡事似乎都须请旨于皇上；而在颐和园，太后似乎是头一号的人物，许多祖制旧规在那里往往暂行搁置。

## V The Young Empress and Ladies of the Court / 第五章 皇后与宫廷命妇

===Original===
The young Empress, the first Lady of the Court after Her Majesty the Empress Dowager, was, to me, a charming character. She is the daughter of the Duke Chow, General of one of the Manchu Banner Corps and a brother of the reigning Empress Dowager. She is thus a first cousin of the Emperor, and is his senior by three years. Her mother, a lady of high birth, ancient lineage, and great distinction, brought her up with much care. She also had the advantage of being a great deal at the Court with her august Aunt, and is highly accomplished, according to Chinese standards. She was affianced at an early age to the Emperor, but, as the custom is, their marriage did not take place for several years later. It was celebrated with great pomp at the Winter Palace in February, 1889, the week before the young Emperor himself took in hand the reins of Government, held, up to that time, by the Empress Dowager, and became Emperor in reality.

The young Empress has the erect carriage and light, swift walk of Her Majesty the Empress Dowager. She is small, not quite five feet tall, with exquisitely dainty hands and feet, of most patrician type. She has a narrow, high-bred face, with a thin, high nose. Her eyes are more of the Chinese type, as we conceive it, than either the Emperor's or Empress Dowager's. Her chin is long and of the type generally called strong. Her mouth is large and extremely sensitive. Her eyes have so kindly a look, her face shines with so sweet an expression, criticism is disarmed and she seems beautiful. She has a sweet dignity, charming manners, and a lovable nature, but there is sometimes a look in her eyes of patient resignation that is almost pathetic. I should not say she possessed any great executive ability, though full of tact, but while Her Majesty the Empress Dowager was in retirement and she was the first Lady at Court, she is said to have shown great capability in her conduct of affairs. Her dignity, perfect breeding, and natural kindness of heart would insure this.

===Chinese===
皇后是皇太后之下宫廷里的第一位命妇，于我而言，她是个极迷人的角色。她是桂祥公爵的女儿——桂祥是满洲八旗之一的将军，又是当今皇太后的兄弟。因此她是皇上的表姐妹，比皇上年长三岁。她的母亲出身名门、家世古老、品貌端凝，将她精心抚养长大。她又多得机缘，常在宫中随侍她那位至尊的姑母，按中国的标准，是极有才学的女子。她自幼便与皇上订了婚，但按着当时的规矩，婚礼一直拖了几年才举行。大婚于 1889 年 2 月在紫禁城里举行，典礼煊赫盛大，恰在年轻的皇上亲掌大政的前一周——此前朝政一直由皇太后执掌——自此他才成了真正意义上的皇上。

皇后的身姿挺拔、步履轻捷，与皇太后一般无二。她身量娇小，身高不足五英尺，生着一双极纤巧精致的手脚，是极贵气的模样。她生着一张清秀的、教养极高的窄脸，鼻梁细而挺。她的眼睛比皇上或皇太后的眼睛都更像我们心目中的中国式眼睛。她的下巴略长，是属于常人所谓「坚毅」一类的。她的嘴略大，却极其敏感。她目中那一缕和善之意，面上那一派甜美之色，足以消解任何挑剔，使她看上去竟十分美丽。她有一种和婉的端庄，可亲的仪态，与一副可爱的性情，只是她眼中有时现出一种逆来顺受的、几乎令人怜悯的神情。我不敢说她有多么卓越的治事才干，倒是极为机敏得体；但在皇太后退居之时、她身为宫中首位命妇的那段日子里，据说她处理宫务颇显才具。她的端庄、完美的教养，以及天生一副仁厚心肠，自能保她处事稳妥。

===Original===
The next Lady, after the young Empress, is the only secondary wife of the Emperor. She is said to have been extremely beautiful at the time she was chosen as his second wife by the Empress Dowager. She belongs to an excellent family, being the daughter of a Viceroy, but though only twenty-eight years old when I knew her, she was already very stout, and there were few remains visible of great beauty. She has very large, full-orbed, brown eyes, and still has a beautifully clear complexion, but her nose is flat, her mouth large and weak; the contour of her face is marred by layers of flesh, her forehead does not indicate much intelligence, and she has very little distinction in appearance. She seems good-natured, but is neither very clever nor tactful. She is not a favorite among the Ladies generally, and is not nearly so interesting, in any way, as the young Empress. She is, however, treated with the most kindly consideration by the young Empress and has precedence over all the other Ladies, and her position at Court is second only to that of the young Empress. Whenever I mention the young Empress, it may be understood that the secondary wife followed immediately after her, coming before the Princesses or any other of the Ladies forming the Court of Her Majesty. I have often seen allusions made to the "Imperial Harem"; there is no such thing as an Imperial Harem at the Court of His Majesty the Emperor Kwang-Hsu. He has only these two wives.

===Chinese===
皇后之下的一位命妇，是皇上唯一的一位侧室。据说当年皇太后为她选作皇上的第二位妻子时，她美艳非常。她出身极好的家庭，是一位总督的女儿，可在我认识她时，她虽不过二十八岁，已十分肥胖，那昔日绝色的风韵已所剩无几。她生着一双大大的、圆睁的棕色眼睛，肤色仍十分明净，只是鼻梁扁平，嘴大而显得懦弱；脸的轮廓被一层层的肉破坏了，额头也显不出多少聪慧，气度上更说不上有什么出挑之处。她看上去脾气温和，却算不上聪明，也算不得机敏。她在命妇之中并不得人缘，论哪一方面都不及皇后那样有趣。然而皇后待她极其和善体贴，她在所有命妇之中位次最尊，在宫廷中的地位也仅次于皇后。每当我提到皇后，便可理解为这位侧室紧随其后，位次在各位王妃或皇太后宫廷中其他任何命妇之前。我常常见人提及所谓「三宫六院」；在皇上光绪的宫廷里，并无所谓的三宫六院。他只有这两位妻子。

===Original===
Her Majesty's Ladies-in-waiting are principally Princesses of the Blood or the widows of Imperial Princes. Her first Lady, Sih-Gerga (Fourth Princess), daughter of Prince Ching, the Prime Minister, is a widow of twenty-four. She married, at the age of sixteen, a son of a high Manchu official, Viceroy of Tientsin, and was left a widow a few months later. She is a beautiful young woman, with face a perfect oval, large brown eyes, and a clear, magnolia-leaf complexion of exquisite texture. She would be called beautiful, judged by any standard. She has no children of her own, but, like most ladies of position who are widows or childless, has an adopted son. Adopted children in China are much closer relationships than is a child, by adoption, with us. In many instances their own parents are still living when they are adopted, and even these parents speak of their child as the son of the adopted mother or parents, and bow to her wishes in bringing up the child.

===Chinese===
皇太后的命妇主要是宗室的王妃，或是皇子们的遗孀。她的第一位命妇四格格（Sih-Gerga），是首相庆亲王的女儿，年方二十四，已守寡。她十六岁上嫁给了一位满族高官、天津总督的儿子，婚后不过数月便成了寡妇。她是位美丽的少妇，脸是完美的鹅蛋形，生着一双棕色的大眼睛，肤色明净如玉兰叶一般，质地极细腻。不论以什么标准衡量，她都当得起「美丽」二字。她自己没有子女，但与大多数有地位的命妇或寡妇、无子之妇一样，她有养子。中国的养子关系，比我们西洋的养子关系要亲密得多。许多情形下，孩子被过继时，亲生父母尚在人世；即便如此，亲生父母也称自己的孩子为养母或养父母之子，在抚养孩子的事上也要听从养母的心意。

===Original===
The Young Empress Ye-Ho-Na-Lah

The Princess Imperial, First Lady of the Court

The next two Ladies of the Court are two Duchesses﻿—also widows. Widows in China never remarry, or if they do, they lose caste and reputation. They are not sacrificed on the funeral pyres of their departed husbands, as in India; but a voluntary suicide on the part of a widow in China is still looked upon as a noble act. A widow who remains faithful to the memory of her husband during a long life is rewarded by the greatest respect and consideration during her life, and honored after death.

===Chinese===
皇后隆裕，叶赫那拉氏

长公主，宫廷首位命妇

宫中接下来的两位命妇是两位公爵夫人——也都是寡妇。中国的寡妇绝不再嫁，若再嫁，便丧失了身份与名声。她们不像印度的寡妇那般，在亡夫的火葬柴堆上殉葬；但在中国，寡妇自愿殉夫，至今仍被视为一种崇高的行为。一个寡妇在漫长的余生中始终守节、忠于亡夫之念，她生前便备受尊崇与优遇，死后亦享哀荣。

===Original===
If a girl prefers to remain unmarried, if a widow remains faithful to the memory of her husband, she is honored after her death with much pomp and ceremony! And great memorial arches are erected in her memory! All over China, one is constantly coming upon these arches to widows and virgins. If the family is not sufficiently wealthy to raise these monuments themselves, public subscriptions are taken, all the relatives contribute, and often the inhabitants of the village or the country where the heroine lived beg to be allowed to have their part in raising a monument to her memory. These arches, of stone or wood, are elaborately carved, sometimes with remarkable sculptures of fabulous animals, flowers, and thousands of birds of every kind (these latter showing the immortality the soul has acquired). Across the entablature of the arch, cut deep into the stone or wood, and gilded or painted in glowing vermilion, shines the name of the virgin or widow to whom it is erected, and on the sides of the arch is inscribed an account of her virtuous acts.

A girl is sometimes affianced at the early age of from six to eight years, and the affianced is from that time spoken of as her husband. Should he die before they marry, which is never earlier than sixteen for the bride, she is considered a "widow," and must henceforth live the life of a recluse. She can never marry anyone else. She may adopt a son, who will call her "mother"; but she may never hope for the joys of family life of her own, without calling down upon her head the obloquy of all whose respect she desires. She wears deep mourning the first three years after his death, and then second mourning; and she can never again put on the festive red, joyous green, or any other color except blue or violet﻿—second mourning.

===Chinese===
倘若一个女子情愿终身不嫁，倘若一个寡妇始终忠于亡夫之念，她死后便要享极盛大的祭奠！并为她建起巍峨的牌坊！走遍中国，处处都能见到这些为节妇贞女所立的牌坊。倘若其家资不足以独自建此牌坊，便由众人捐资，亲戚无不解囊，乃至那贞节女子所居之村庄、之乡里百姓，也每每请求允许他们出一份力，共襄此举，以志纪念。这些牌坊或石或木，雕琢繁复，有时还饰以精绝的雕刻：神异的瑞兽、花卉，以及成千上万只各色飞禽（这后一种花样，象征亡魂所获的不朽）。牌坊的额枋之上，深深地刻进石里或木里、或贴金、或涂上鲜亮的朱漆，闪耀着那受表章的贞女或节妇的姓名；牌坊两侧则镌刻着她一生贞烈事迹的记述。

女子有时自六至八岁便订了亲，那订亲之人从此便被称作她的丈夫。倘若他在迎娶之前去世（新娘从无不早于十六岁出嫁之理），她便被视为「寡妇」，从此须过着隐居的日子。她永不能再嫁他人。她可以抱养一个儿子，唤她「母亲」；但她永不能再指望自己享受天伦之乐——若想如此，便要招致她所敬重的所有人的唾骂。亡夫死后头三年，她身着深重的孝服，其后则服轻孝；她再不能穿上喜庆的红、欢悦的绿，或任何别的颜色，只可着青色或紫色——即所谓轻孝之服。

===Original===
The Northern Chinese and the Manchu ladies use a great deal of paint and powder on their faces; but a widow can never add one artificial iota to the rose of her cheek, to the cherry of her lips, or the lily of her brow. She can nevermore use paint or powder. In most instances the Chinese ladies are but the prettier for this, for they have beautiful skins, and the use of powder and paint is carried to such an excess as to be quite unnatural.

There are only eight of Her Majesty's Ladies who live always in the Palace, but this number is increased about four times on festive occasions. The Princess Imperial, the Empress Dowager's adopted daughter, is the first of the Princesses at Court, and, when she comes to the Palace, ranks next to the Empress and the secondary wife of the Emperor.

One evening, at dinner, in the Throne-room, Sih-Gerga undertook to tell me the relationships of the different Princesses to each other and to the young Empress. Incidentally, this made them related to the Emperor and the Empress Dowager, but neither of Their Majesties' names was mentioned in this connection, for such would have been a great piece of presumption, amounting almost to sacrilege. They might be related, but no Princess would dare mention such a thing. It would be against all the laws of Chinese proprieties. I found, after this explanation of Sih-Gerga's, that the Ladies were all related by consanguinity or marriage to each other and to the young Empress.

===Chinese===
中国北方和满族的命妇们脸上敷很重的脂粉；但寡妇却绝不可在双颊的玫红、唇上的樱桃、额上的凝脂之上添一分人造的颜色。她再不许使用胭脂水粉。多数情形下，中国的命妇们不施脂粉反显得更美，因她们天生好皮肉，而敷粉涂朱则往往过火，反显失真。

常住在宫里的命妇只有八位，但每逢节庆，此数约增加四倍。长公主——皇太后的养女——是宫中王妃里居首位者，她来到宫中时，位次仅在皇后与皇上侧室之下。

有一天晚上，在宝座殿里用晚膳时，四格格为我一一讲明各位王妃相互之间以及与皇后的亲缘关系。如此一来，她们自然也与皇上和皇太后有了亲眷之谊，但提起这事时，皇上皇太后二位的圣讳却一字不曾提及——若提及便是大大的僭越，几乎是亵渎之罪。她们也许确有亲眷之谊，但哪一位王妃也不敢如此提及。这有悖于中国礼数的全部规矩。经四格格这一番讲解，我才明白这些命妇彼此之间、以及与皇后之间，不是出于血亲，便是由于姻亲，无一例外。

===Original===
There are a number of tiring-women and maids in the Palace who are called by outsiders "slaves"; but they are not slaves, or, if they are so, it is but for a time, a space of ten years. Every spring, the daughters of the lowest of the Manchu families, the Seventh and Eighth Banners, are brought into the Palace to be chosen from, by the Empress and Empress Dowager, for maids and tiring-women. One day, on going to the Palace, I saw a number of ordinary carts near one of the Postern Gates, and I learned they had brought crowds of these girls of the families of the Eighth Banner. They are first passed in review by the Head Eunuch, and he selects from them, those he thinks may please Her Majesty. These pass before her, and she tells the Head Eunuch which ones are to remain in the Palace. They are brought to the Palace from the ages of ten to sixteen years. They remain in service for ten years, after which time they are allowed to return to their families; and in case they have been satisfactory and pleased Their Majesties, they are given a comfortable dot and are provided with a handsome marriage outfit, which causes them to make much better marriages than they would otherwise do. During their so-called ten years' slavery in the Palace, they live upon the fat of the land, have beautiful clothes and many advantages. They wear, while in Her Majesty's service, blue gowns, with their hair plainly parted at the side and braided in a single long braid (tied with red silk cords), which hangs down the back. They wear bunches of flowers over each ear. The young Empress and secondary wife, as well as each of the Princesses, have their own maids and tiring-women, who remain in the private quarters of these Ladies.

===Chinese===
宫里有许多梳头侍女和使女，被外人称为「奴婢」；然而她们并非奴婢，纵然是，也不过是一时之事，为期十年而已。每年春天，最低微的满族人家——第七、第八旗——的女儿们被送进宫里，由皇后与皇太后挑选作使女和梳头侍女。一日我进宫时，见一扇旁门附近停着许多寻常的骡车，方知它们载来了一群第八旗人家的女儿。她们先由总管太监过目，由他从中挑出那些他认为可能合皇太后心意的人。这些人再从太后面前走过，由她吩咐总管太监哪几个留在宫里。她们自十岁至十六岁之间被送进宫中。服役十年之后，便准其回家；若她们当差称职、又合了皇上皇太后之意，便得一份丰厚的资财，备一份体面的嫁妆，使她们能比寻常攀上好得多的亲事。在宫中那所谓十年「为奴」的日子里，她们锦衣玉食，有华美的衣裳，又享许多好处。在皇太后跟前当差时，她们身穿蓝色的长袍，头发在侧边分明地分开，编成一根长长的辫子（用红丝绳束住），垂在背后。她们两耳上方各戴一簇花。皇后和侧室，以及各位王妃，都各有自己的使女和梳头侍女，这些侍女住在各自主子的私室里。

===Original===
Besides these young maids, there are in the Palace a number of old women, servants of Her Majesty, who have been married and have children; these overlook the younger women, direct the work of the lower eunuchs, and are in a position somewhat similar to housekeepers with us. Among these is a Chinese woman who nursed Her Majesty through a long illness, about twenty-five years since, and saved her life by giving her mother's milk to drink. Her Majesty, who never forgets a favor, has always kept this woman in the Palace. Being a Chinese, she had bound feet. Her Majesty, who cannot bear to see them even, had her feet unbound and carefully treated, until now she can walk comfortably. Her Majesty has educated the son, who was an infant at the time of her illness, and whose natural nourishment she partook of. This young man is already a Secretary in a good yamen (Government Office).

No Chinese lady of position ever dresses herself or combs her own hair, and she generally has three or four personal maids. These are, in many instances, bought outright from their parents, and might be considered really slaves; but they are treated with great consideration and even friendliness by their mistresses, and have in most instances a happy lot. As these maids are bought when they and their mistresses are children, they grow up together, and though the maid never forgets the respect due her mistress, they are on a much more friendly footing than mistress and maid could ever be in Europe in such cases.

The first of a lady's maids stands behind her at table, no matter how many servitors there may be; goes out with her, sits with her, and sleeps either in her room or at her door, and is almost her constant companion. When the time comes for them to marry, they are given a comfortable outfit by their mistresses, and are cared for to the third and fourth generation; but the children of the so-called slaves are free, unless the mother or parents decide, of their own free will, to sell them, as they have been sold, to some good family.

===Chinese===
除了这些年少的使女之外，宫里还有一些老年的妇人，是皇太后的仆妇，都曾嫁过人、有儿有女；她们监管着那些年轻的侍女，指使那些品级低微的太监们做事，地位与我们西洋的管家婆颇有几分相似。其中有一位汉人妇人，约莫二十五年前皇太后缠绵病榻许久，便是她看护痊愈的，她并以自己的乳汁进奉太后，救了太后的性命。皇太后是个从不忘恩的人，一直将这妇人留在宫里。她是汉人，从前裹过脚。皇太后连看那种脚都看不得，便叫人为她解了裹脚布，悉心调治，直到如今她走路已全无妨碍。皇太后又供那孩子读书——太后生病那阵他还是个襁褓中的婴儿，太后便曾饮过他生母的乳——这年轻人如今已是一个体面衙门（政府官署）里的文书了。

中国但凡有地位的命妇，从不自己穿衣梳头，通常都有三四个贴身使女。这些使女有许多是直接从她们父母手中买来的，未尝不可视为真正的奴婢；但女主人们待她们极其周到，甚而是亲热友善，她们的命运多半也算美满。这些使女被买来时，自己与女主人都还是孩童，她们一同长大，虽使女从不曾忘记对女主人应尽的恭敬，但彼此相处，远比欧洲此等情形下的女主人与使女要亲厚得多。

命妇的贴身使女中居首位的，在女主人用膳时，不论有多少仆从伺候，都立在她身后；与她同出同入，同坐同止，夜里便睡在她的房里或房门口，几乎是她形影不离的伴侣。到了她们出嫁之时，女主人为她们备一份丰厚的嫁妆，连她们的子孙三四代也都照拂周全；只是那些所谓奴婢的孩子，生来便是自由身——除非其母或其父母心甘情愿，将自己卖入了好人家那般，也把自己的孩子卖与一户好人家。
